## The module package

**R09. `module-kit` writes the canonical text of the package with its own code: one function over a tree of JSON values writes it as the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 25, says, and stops with an error on a value that cannot be in the format. The canonical view is built from the JSON value of each file of the module as it is. `encoding/json`, `jsontext` and the types of `formatv0` do not write the texts of the package.**
- **What is decided:**
  - the function of the canonical text gets a tree of JSON values — an object, an array, a string, an integer, `true`, `false`, `null` — and writes it by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 25:
    - the members of an object go in the order of the UTF-16 code units of their names, and there is no whitespace between tokens;
    - an integer is written in decimal digits, a negative one with the sign `-`;
    - in a string only `"` and `\` are escaped, the five control characters — `\b`, `\t`, `\n`, `\f` and `\r` —, and the other characters up to U+001F, as `\u00` and two lowercase hexadecimal digits. Everything else is written as it is, `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029 too;
    - the text is UTF-8, with no BOM and no newline at the end;
  - on a value that cannot be in the canonical serialization, the function stops with an error rather than converting it. These are a number with a fractional part, an integer outside ±(2^53−1), a string that is not valid UTF-8 and a value that is not JSON;
  - an integer that the YAML reader gave as a double — `50.0` or `5e1` — comes into the tree as the integer `50`: so the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 25, says, and the build converts it, before the function;
  - a package is built only from a module that has passed the check. The schemas hold the numbers of the parts as integers in ±(2^53−1) (R45 spec), and the rule `task.template-solver` holds the solver's results ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 24). So a refusal of the function on a checked module means a defect in the code of `module-kit`, not an error of the author;
  - the texts of the package — the canonical view, the listing of the device part and the inventory — and of the module index are written only by this function;
  - the canonical view is built from the JSON value of each file of the module "as it is": from what the YAML reader gave, without a pass through the types and without default values. The parts, the tiers, the instances of templates, the inventory and the package as files — by R62 spec, and step by step — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2;
  - the tests:
    - CI passes all the vectors of `format/vectors/packages/` and `format/vectors/tiers/` from `spec.Format` (R64 spec): the digest and size of both parts and the package digest. CI computes the solver's results of the vector `with-solver` by a run of the solver of its own, and does not take them from the vector (Д44 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`);
    - unit tests go through each refusal and the escaping table — [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 25;
    - a fuzz test compares the function with `jsontext.Value.Canonicalize` of Go's standard library on values that the format allows. The reference lives only in the tests, in a file with `//go:build goexperiment.jsonv2`: with `GOEXPERIMENT=nojsonv2` this file is not built, and the other tests run. So the code that writes the authors' data will also have a fuzz test, which the reason Fuzzing in R33 spec expects (the 2026-10-21 amendment to R02).
- **The probe on the vectors** — in the session's folder, 2026-11-02, on Go 1.27.3; the prototype did not get into the repository. Six vectors of `format/vectors/packages/` of `spec` v0.6.3 were converted from YAML to JSON, and `server_part`, `device_part` and `inventory` of each were written in three ways. The digest and size of both parts and the package digest were checked:

  | Way | Package vectors | `1.5` | 2^53 | 2^63 | Broken UTF-8 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | a function of its own, about a hundred lines | all six | refusal | refusal | refusal | refusal |
  | `json.Marshal`, then `jsontext.Value.Canonicalize` | all six | writes `1.5` | writes `9007199254740992` | writes `9223372036854776000` | writes U+FFFD |
  | `encoding/json` with `SetEscapeHTML(false)` | five: in `strings` the server part is 1418 bytes instead of 1415 | writes `1.5` | writes `9007199254740992` | writes `9223372036854775808` | writes U+FFFD |

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02, while T01 was planned (RUN.md, T01, «Решено автором»):
- the format text sets the serialization itself, "so that an implementation does not depend on a library" (R62 spec). On the format's data — ASCII keys, only integers, no surrogates — the serialization comes down to sorting the keys and escaping. The second implementation of `spec`, `tools/format-check/canonical.mjs`, is code of its own too, 44 lines, without libraries;
- a refusal is better than a silent conversion. The package digest pins the attempt (architecture 1 spec), and a value converted silently would give a package that the second implementation of `spec` will not give;
- a function of its own pulls in neither a dependency nor a build constraint: anyone who imports the library builds it.

— *Rejected:*
- `jsontext.Value.Canonicalize` in the production code. In go1.27 the experiment `jsonv2` is on by default, and the package is imported without flags ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4). But:
  - it carries numbers through float64: `1.5` and 2^53 it writes, and 2^63 it rounds;
  - before it one more step is needed — `json.Marshal`, which silently replaces broken UTF-8 with U+FFFD;
  - whoever builds with `GOEXPERIMENT=nojsonv2` has no such package at all. The experiment is a property of the build of whoever imports the library, that is, of `api` and the eval runner of `tutor`;
- `encoding/json`: it always escapes U+2028 and U+2029, and `SetEscapeHTML(false)` removes only `<`, `>` and `&`. This is what the vector `strings` catches;
- `github.com/gowebpki/jcs` v1.0.2 under Apache-2.0. This is a dependency, and with it testify in `go.mod`, for what a function of its own does in a hundred lines. It too carries numbers through float64 ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4), and it fixed the errors with surrogates and broken UTF-8 only 2026-09-20;
- `github.com/cyberphone/json-canonicalization`: it has no `go.mod`, the GitHub API does not recognize its license — `NOASSERTION` —, and `ci-licenses` would have to name it as an exception. The import as documented is broken;
- to write the texts through the types of `formatv0` and `json.Marshal`. The fields of a struct go in the order of declaration, and the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 25, takes the JSON value of a file as it is. `spec` checks the writing back of the types only by value and only on its own corpus (`tools/types/roundtrip`).

— *Consequence:*
- the code is written by the code epic — row 3.3 of COORDINATION.md of `spec`. T01 writes no code: in E1 the code is only the skeleton of the CLI (R04);
- the point of the card "what to write the package's canonical text with" is decided, and the edit of the card is sent by the result of the detailing, T05 (architecture 3 spec);
- the format text, the card 3.7 spec and R62 spec say that `jsontext` in go1.27.3 is there "only under the experiment `jsonv2`", while this experiment is on by default — В9 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec».

— RUN.md, T01, table 3; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.1, 3.2 and 4.

## The generator

**R10. `module-kit` executes the generator and the places of randomness with its own code: a PCG stream with the DXSM output, a place from FNV-1a 64 and five functions — by the constants of the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 22, and on an argument outside the domain a function returns an error. Go's `math/rand/v2` and `hash/fnv` are only the reference in the tests. The generator lies in `internal/` and is not part of the library's API: what goes outside is the choice of an attempt's instance.**
- **What is decided:**
  - a stream is a generator with two words of state, high and low, taken as they are. A step is `state · M + C mod 2^128` through `bits.Mul64` and `bits.Add64`, the output is DXSM, and the constants are the numbers from the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 22;
  - a place of randomness is a seed and a list of names. Its stream is started by two words: FNV-1a 64 of the UTF-8 text "the seed in decimal digits, then `|` and the name, for each name", and the seed itself. There is at least one name, and a name is a non-empty string of valid UTF-8 without `|`; otherwise the place is not built, and an error is returned;
  - the functions are five — `integer`, `below`, `between`, `fraction` and `permutation`, those that the vectors check, — and each draws as many integers as the format text says. Their domains and how they compute — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3. On an argument outside the domain a function returns an error and draws no integers. There is no shuffle of a list (`Shuffle`);
  - the Go package is `internal/random`: it is not part of the library's API, and other Go modules do not import it. What goes outside is one function of the templates API — the instance of an attempt: the number `below(count)` of the place of the attempt's seed with the names `template`, the task's ID and `instance`, for count from 1 to 10,000. No other call is made on this place, and a module has no other places in format v0 (R60 spec; [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 23). The name of the function is given by the code epic;
  - the generator has no shared state: a stream lives within one call, and a place is built anew for each. The code of `module-kit` has no randomness not from a seed — neither `math/rand`, nor the top-level functions of `math/rand/v2`, nor `crypto/rand`. `api` draws the attempt's seed from a cryptographic source, and `module-kit` gets it as a number ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 22);
  - the tests:
    - CI passes all the vectors of `format/vectors/random/` from `spec.Format` (R64 spec). A vector is read by the same YAML reader and the same schema validator as in the module check — against `random-vector.schema.json` — and by the type `formatv0.RandomVector` (R72 spec), and the numbers written as strings are parsed by `strconv`. When a seed and names are given, the derived words are compared with `state`. Then the calls go in order, and each value is compared, `fraction` as a double. The test fails if there are no vectors and if a call has no known field;
    - the place of a template is checked by the vectors of `format/vectors/templates/` too: for each seed they name the instance;
    - unit tests go through each refusal and the number of integers that each function draws: `below(1)` and `between(a, a)` draw one, `permutation(0)` and `permutation(1)` none;
    - the comparison with the standard library: the stream, the five functions and the words of a place are compared with `math/rand/v2` and `hash/fnv`. An ordinary test runs on every `go test`: on the words of all the vectors, on the bounds — 1, powers of two, 2^63 − 1, 2^63 + 1 and 2^64 − 1 — and on several thousand inputs that the generator itself gives from a constant seed. A fuzz test compares the same on any words, bounds, ranges and names, and permutations of up to 1024 numbers, and the same inputs lie in its corpus. These packages are in any build of Go, and the tests need no build tag. This is the second target of fuzzing after R09 (R33 spec).
- **The probe** — in the session's folder, 2026-11-02, on Go 1.27.3; the prototype did not get into the repository. 12 vectors of `format/vectors/random/` of `spec` v0.6.3 were converted from YAML to JSON and read by the type `formatv0.RandomVector`:

  | Way | Generator vectors | Argument outside the domain | `GOARCH=386` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | its own code, about a hundred lines | all 12 | an error | all 12 |
  | `math/rand/v2` and `hash/fnv` | all 12 | a panic: `Uint64N(0)`, `Int64N` of a negative, `Perm(-1)` — in `make` | all 12 — by the path of `uint32n` |

  The fuzz comparison of the two ways — 6,864,160 runs in 60 seconds on amd64 and another 20 seconds on 386 — found no divergences.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02, while T02 was planned (RUN.md, T02, «Решено автором»):
- the stream of the library is set by the toolchain of whoever imports it. Which Go builds a program is decided by the lines `go` and `toolchain` of the main module ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4), and the main module is `api` or the eval runner of `tutor`. With the standard library the instance of an attempt would also depend on their Go, while the vectors pass in CI of `module-kit` only on its own. The stream of its own code is set by one version of `module-kit`, and the attempt keeps it, to repeat the verdict (R44 spec; architecture 1 spec);
- Go promises that the streams of `math/rand/v2` will not change, but not in the package's documentation: in the blog and in a comment on a regression test ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4). The format text sets the generator by numbers, "so that no implementation depends on a library" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 22), — like the canonical text, which `module-kit` also writes itself (R09);
- an error is better than a panic: the methods of `math/rand/v2` panic on an argument outside the domain, and a wrapper would check each argument itself anyway;
- its own code has one path on all platforms. In `math/rand/v2` on 32 bits `uint64n` goes through `uint32n`, and that the numbers are the same is held by the tests of Go;
- the reference in the tests catches a divergence from the standard library when Go is raised: the comparison runs on every `go test` in CI of `module-kit`, before the consumers.

— *Rejected:*
- `math/rand/v2` and `hash/fnv` in the production code — `rand.New(rand.NewPCG(…))` and the methods `Uint64`, `Uint64N`, `Int64N`, `Float64` and `Perm`. There is less code, standalone's simulator does so, and R72 spec and Д40 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec` call them the normative functions of R59 spec. But the stream would depend on the importer's Go, on an argument outside the domain the methods panic, and on 32 bits they take another path;
- an exported Go package of the generator, which `engine` or `sim` could take. This is a new link — an edit of the card «в реестр spec» — and a semver promise for one more API. The generator is one for the platform by its algorithm, not by its code: each component executes it by the format text and passes the same vectors (R27 spec; R59 spec), and `sim` reads packages without `module-kit` (architecture 3.9 spec);
- to copy Go's references into the tests — `TestPCG` and `regressGolden` —, as the tests of `random.mjs` of `spec` do. The vector `go-pcg-1-2` takes the first six of the twenty values of `TestPCG`, and the comparison with the standard library on every `go test` checks the words 1 and 2 too, from which Go's references start, and much beyond them;
- the shuffle of a list, `Shuffle`: it has no vectors, and `module-kit` shuffles nothing. If it is needed, it will come with the code that needs it, by the format text.

— *Consequence:*
- the code is written by the code epic — row 3.3 of COORDINATION.md of `spec`. T02 writes no code: in E1 the code is only the skeleton of the CLI (R04);
- the point of the card "how its CI passes the generator vectors" is decided, and the edit of the card is sent by the result of the detailing, T05 (architecture 3 spec);
- if the comparison with the standard library fails after Go is raised, Go has changed the stream of `math/rand/v2`. The format text names the generator as the generator of Go, and this is a question «в реестр spec», while its own code stays, as the format text says.

— RUN.md, T02; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.2, 3.3 and 4.

## The Starlark sandbox

**R11. `module-kit` executes Starlark in go.starlark.net of the version that the format will name — for now the latest, 4f4267fb140d. The clock is a safeguard of `module-kit` with a margin over the budget of steps: 1 s for the evaluation of a predicate and 60 s for a template solver. An evaluation runs in the caller's thread and with the caller's `context`, and the library holds no slots. Until stage 4 Starlark runs in the process, and third-party Starlark from stage 4 requires a process with a memory limit — this is a question for `spec`.**
- **What is decided:**
  - the interpreter is go.starlark.net of the version that the format names, and raising it is an edit of the format, not of `module-kit` alone. While the format names no version (В10 in table 3 of RUN.md), `module-kit` pins the latest in `go.mod` — `v0.0.0-20261021141710-4f4267fb140d`. In it the steps are counted by backward jumps and calls (#663), the depth of recursion is bounded (#619), and the methods of `set` do not panic (#672). go.starlark.net is excluded from Dependabot `gomod`, like the module `spec`;
  - the dialect is one for all the Starlark of the format: the options of the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 24, — `Set`, `While`, `TopLevelControl` and `GlobalReassign` are on, `Recursion` is off, `load` is forbidden. A check predicate goes in the same dialect. Of the options, two matter to an expression: `Set` — whether there is the name `set` — and `Recursion`. This is В11, «в реестр spec»;
  - the clock — constants of the release of `module-kit`, a safeguard with a margin of no less than 10× over the time of the budget of steps on the machine of the measurement:
    - a predicate — 1 s for one evaluation. When it fires, the clock gives "unchecked" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15);
    - a solver — 60 s per template: for the top level of the program and all the calls of `solve` together, like the budget of steps. When it fires, the clock gives a breach of `task.template-solver` with the numbers of the instance on which the solver stopped ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 24).

    The clock calls `Thread.Cancel` by a timer, and the interpreter sees a cancellation between instructions: it does not interrupt a built-in function;
  - the caller gives the `context`. Its cancellation is `Cancel` too, but with another reason, and it is an error without a verdict: a predicate does not get "unchecked", and the module check does not get a breach;
  - the library holds no slots. An evaluation runs synchronously, in the caller's thread, on its own `starlark.Thread`, and the sandbox has no shared state. There is no waiting for a slot either, which would give "unchecked" from the load. How many evaluations run at once is held by the caller: `api` — by its requests, the CLI — by template solvers one per CPU (`GOMAXPROCS`), each on its own thread. The CLI prints the summary in the order of the templates, not in the order of completion. The clock runs by wall time, so the caller holds no more concurrent evaluations than it has vCPUs: otherwise the clock of a predicate would depend on the load. That this and the cancellation of the `context` without a verdict are duties of `api` goes «в реестр spec», В13;
  - a panic of the interpreter is caught by `recover`: for a predicate it is "unchecked", for a solver a breach of `task.template-solver`. `recover` does not catch an overflow of the Go stack or a lack of memory. The recursion of Starlark functions is held by the recursion that is off. Built-in functions on deeply nested values go along the Go stack and for long — `str` of a list 100,000 deep takes 1.6 s on one step —, and they, like memory, are held only by a separate process from stage 4;
  - go.starlark.net does not count memory: a built-in function in one step takes up to 1 GiB by repetition and any amount by `list(range(n))`. At stages 1–3 the modules are only official (О-9), and Starlark runs in the process: the risk of memory is accepted — Д10 in table 4 of RUN.md. Third-party Starlark from stage 4 — the solvers in `module-registry` and the predicates in `api` — requires a process with a memory limit. A process is not available to the library, computation without input or output, and it is for `api` and `module-registry` to decide this — В12, «в реестр spec».
- **The measurement** — 2026-11-02, Go 1.27.3, AMD Ryzen AI 9 365, 20 threads. Standalone is cloned into a temporary folder of the session at commit 89986bc; the bench is in the session's folder and did not get into the repository.
  - The solver tests of standalone — 1410 runs of reference tasks and 66 runs of templates — on its version and with `replace` to the latest:

    | | 89a6a09411d5 | 4f4267fb140d |
    |---|---|---|
    | the heaviest solver | 2,413,617 steps, `pig-34-d4-2` | 2,725,355 steps, `pig-34-d4-2` |
    | the heaviest template | 959,962 steps | 949,969 steps |
    | the longest run | 25.8 ms | 25.3 ms |
    | all steps together | 94,732,336 | 77,489,284 |

    Over 738 solvers the ratio of steps new/old has a median of 0.96, and for 80% it is from 0.59 to 1.53. More than 2× only for solvers of about 50 steps.
  - Our own bench on both versions:

    | Program | 89a6a09411d5 | 4f4267fb140d |
    |---|---|---|
    | a turn of an empty `for` loop in a function | 6 steps | 10 steps |
    | one call of `solve` of the vector `past-the-budget-of-the-template` | 60,000,039 steps | 100,000,040 steps |
    | a predicate, 1,000,000 steps of brute force | 4.2 ms | 6.0 ms |
    | a predicate with a brute force of divisors up to 100,000 | 1,499,987 steps | 1,000,010 steps |
    | a simple predicate — `value % 3 == 0 and value % 4 == 0` | 19 steps | 20 steps |
    | a loop, 100,000,000 steps | 650 ms | 445 ms |
    | `len(list(range(50000000)))` with a clock of 100 ms | 763 MiB in 7 steps; a stop 217 ms after the clock | 763 MiB in 30 steps; after 209 ms |
    | `set([1]).difference()` | a panic: nil pointer dereference | the error "got 0 arguments, want 1" |
    | `(lambda f: f(f))(lambda f: f(f))` with `Recursion: false` | "function lambda called recursively" | the same |

    On the latest version a predicate that calls `sorted(range(1000))` on each of 100,000 turns fits in 1,000,000 steps in 588 ms. A solver of the same kind reaches 93,898,910 steps in 60 s, and the clock stops it. `str` of a list 100,000 deep takes 1.6 s on one step, and of one 1,000,000 deep more than 120 s, and the process does not crash, but the clock does not interrupt it either.

— *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-11-02, while T03 was planned (RUN.md, T03, «Решено автором»):
- the format's limits of steps are set "as go.starlark.net counts them", and the count of go.starlark.net changed 2026-10-05 (#663): the same limit in its two versions is different work, and two implementations of the format would diverge on one module. The latest version — because on the solvers of standalone, by which the limits were calibrated, the measurement showed no big difference, for predicates the new count is no dearer than the old — a turn of a list comprehension is 10 steps against 15 —, and the panic of the methods of `set` is fixed in it;
- a verdict is repeated by the stored observation by the release of `module-kit` that gave it (R03 spec; R44 spec; architecture 1 spec), and the acceptance of a template must not depend on the machine of CI. So the clock is a safeguard far above the time of the budget: a simple predicate spends 6 ms of 1 s, and a solver 0.45 s of 60. The clock fires before the steps only for a program whose steps call heavy built-in functions, and only for it does the outcome depend on the machine. Such a predicate may fit in the steps and still reach the clock: on the machine of the measurement a predicate with `sorted(range(1000))` on each turn takes 588 ms of 1 s, and on a machine twice as slow the clock will stop it. This is the accepted price of the safeguard: a predicate of one integer does not need such steps;
- the library is computation without input or output and may run on the device (repositories 1 spec). It has no shared semaphore and no waiting: concurrency is a property of the process that the caller owns. Waiting for a slot, as in standalone, would give "unchecked" from the load;
- the format explicitly left the clock and concurrent runs to `module-kit` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 15 and 24);
- one dialect for all the Starlark of the format: the author writes a predicate and a solver in one language, and the recursion that is off protects the Go stack for a predicate too;
- a separate process is needed when Starlark is written by a third-party author — from stage 4 (О-9). Until then the project checks the modules, and a process costs a start, an exchange and an implementation of its own for each OS of the CLI.

— *Rejected:*
- to pin the version of standalone, 89a6a09411d5. The limits and the comment of the vector would stay as calibrated, but the panic of the methods of `set` would stay too, and `module-kit` would fall behind the fixes;
- to leave the format without a named version: the same limit in two implementations of the format would mean different work;
- `module-kit` holds the slots, like the sandbox of standalone, `Limits{Steps, Timeout, Concurrency, Wait}`. This is shared state in the library and "unchecked" from the load; R125 standalone rejected four slots with a safety clock too: "Verdicts depend on the load";
- `api` holds everything, and `module-kit` has no clock: the format text names the clock of `module-kit` and leaves it to it;
- 2 s per run, as in standalone. A solver with heavy steps went through 93,898,910 steps in 60 s on the machine of the measurement, and in 2 s it would go through about 3,000,000: the clock would stop it deep inside the budget, and the acceptance of a template would depend on the machine;
- without a clock: the built-in functions on each step would run without a time limit;
- a separate process in the CLI now: this is an implementation of its own of the memory limit on linux, darwin and windows, and the predicates in `api` stay in the process anyway;
- zero options for a predicate: two dialects for one format, and a predicate would not know `set`, which a solver knows;
- `GOMEMLIMIT` or `debug.SetMemoryLimit` from the library: this is a setting of the caller's whole process, not of the sandbox, and the limit is soft — the garbage collector will not stop one built-in function. Whether to set it is decided by the process, that is, by `api`;
- a price in bytes for built-in functions, as for the helpers of standalone: the built-in functions are the code of go.starlark.net, not of `module-kit`, and the methods of the values themselves bypass it — "only a limit on the memory of the process can" (R53 standalone).

— *Consequence:*
- the code is written by the code epic — row 3.3 of COORDINATION.md of `spec`. Together with go.starlark.net it excludes it from Dependabot `gomod`, like the module `spec` (T05);
- the point of the card 3.7 spec "the clock and concurrent runs" is decided, and the edit of the card is sent by T05;
- «в реестр spec»: В10 — the version of go.starlark.net and the count of steps, В11 — the dialect of a predicate, В12 — the memory of third-party Starlark from stage 4, В13 — what of the sandbox `api` holds. The debt — Д10.

— RUN.md, T03, tables 3 and 4; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.3, 3.4 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-04:* format 0.3 names go.starlark.net of the version 4f4267fb140d — the one that `module-kit` pinned — and one dialect for all the Starlark of the format, the predicate's too, with the list of 33 built-in names. The limit of steps fires when the count reaches it (R88 spec; R17). So В10 and В11 are closed. `spec` answered В12 by an addition to О-9: from stage 4 the Starlark of a third-party module runs in a separate process with a memory limit, and the tasks of `api` and `module-registry` decide how. В13 — by the cards 3.5 and 3.7 spec: `api` has no more than `GOMAXPROCS` evaluations at once, and "vCPU" above reads so. To pin go.starlark.net and exclude it from Dependabot `gomod` is a task of the sandbox, T27. — RUN.md, T17; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.3 and 3.4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-04 (T27):* go.starlark.net is pinned in `go.mod` and excluded from Dependabot `gomod`, and the sandbox executes what is decided here (R28). The measurement of T27 on the same machine did not repeat the numbers about the predicate with `sorted(range(1000))` on each turn: on 4f4267fb140d such a turn costs 20 steps, and `[n for n in range(100000) if sorted(range(1000)) == None]` reaches the limit of 1,000,000 at the 50,000th turn, after 932 ms, rather than fitting in it in 588 ms. The conclusion does not change: such a predicate reaches the clock before the limit already on a machine slightly slower than the machine of the measurement. — RUN.md, T27.

## The tag of `spec` v0.6.5

## Version comparison

**R13. `module-kit` compares a new version of a module by packages, not by the author's YAML, with each published version below it by semver, withdrawn ones too. The comparison itself finds the lowest version part that has to be raised, and the author may raise a higher one, but not a lower one. Major — when something of what the learner had in the previous version is gone or means something else; a transition to a new format version, while the format is v0, is major. The CLI has a command of its own for the comparison — `diff`.**
- **What is decided:**
  - the principle of the version parts: major — an entry with a full ID is gone, the audience has narrowed, a skill no longer teaches an anchor skill, the check of a task that stayed has changed, the module has moved to a format version that may change verdicts, and everything that concepts 3 spec names. Minor — everything previous is in place and means the same, and the module gives more or leads otherwise. Patch — the same entries, checks and path, other words or pictures. The table by the fields of the schemas of format 0.2 — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5;
  - the author declares only the version number, and a change of an entry's meaning — by a new ID: for a skill R49 spec says so, for a task, a lesson, a trap and a technique this is a removal and a new entry;
  - packages are compared — the canonical view and the listing of the device part (R62 spec). The new version is the module's folder, which `diff` checks and builds in memory, or a package built by `build`; the published ones are packages checked by the chain of digests from the index. `eval/`, comments and the order of keys do not change the version: the package is the same;
  - a slot check is compared by meaning, as its check kind reads it: for `number`, `fraction` and `choice` — the key, `units`, `form`, the options and the trap values after normalization ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15), and the predicate of `predicate` — as a string. For a template the instances are matched by the values of the parameters, and the checks of each instance with its numbers are compared;
  - the rules of comparison (a)–(g) — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5: the version is above all the published ones, a step of one part no lower than the one found, the completeness and immutability of `retired_skills`, IDs that are not in the newest version do not come back, `languages` are the same. `diff` takes all the published versions of the module: the rules (c), (e) and (f) — by all of them, and the part found and (d) — by the newest. A withdrawn version is a published one, and the comparison does not read `withdrawn`;
  - a transition to a new format version: while the format is v0 — major, from v1 — the same part as that of the format's step. In a package of a previous format version `diff` reads the inventory, `languages`, the skills, `retired_skills` and the IDs of entries by the places that the format keeps in all versions;
  - in 0.y.z, as in Cargo, y plays the major, and z the minor and the patch. In 0.0.z each version is a major of its own: any edit raises z. The first version may be any;
  - `diff` is a command of the CLI over one function of the library: a module or a package, a checked index or an explicit "none published", the folders of the published packages. Code 0 — accepted, 1 — a refusal with the IDs of the rules, 2 — no comparison is possible; the report — the part found and the changes by paths. The CLI does not go to the network and checks no signatures ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5).

— *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-11-02 while T04 was planned (RUN.md, T04, «Решено автором»). The sources — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4, read 2026-11-02:
- the part is found by the comparison, as with gorelease and cargo-semver-checks: a part chosen not by the author but by the content will not diverge from the content. Semver lets the author go higher: a major "MAY also include minor and patch level changes";
- major is "is gone or means something else": within one major nothing disappears for the learner, and `api` can move them to a new version itself, and `api` decides which version a new attempt gets (architecture 3.5 spec). In the same way a removal is counted as major by semver, Frictionless — "delete or change the meaning of data" — and Elm, in which what is removed gives `M.MAJOR`;
- a command of its own: the comparison needs other inputs than the check — the packages of the published versions, which CI downloads and checks — and a report of its own, by which the author chooses the version. `spec` already calls the comparison `diff` (R49 spec);
- a format transition is major while the format is v0: a step of 0.y promises nothing ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2), and Д20 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec` suggests that a transition that changes verdicts is «сродни изменению ключа опубликованной задачи». The chain of comparisons must not break at a transition: `learner-model` transfers nowhere a skill that is in neither `skills` nor `retired_skills` of the current version, and there is no error in that (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R80 spec);
- packages, not YAML: the canonical view is the JSON values of the files as they are (R62 spec), and everything that does not go into the package does not concern the learners;
- the comparison of a check by meaning: the verdict depends on the check only through these normal forms, and `module-kit` holds the normalization for the verdict anyway. Only the predicate stays a string: the equality of two Starlark programs cannot be decided in the general case;
- the instances by the values of the parameters: the learner got a task, not an instance, and the attempt keeps its package and seed;
- all the published versions: the comparison with the newest suffices by induction only if each publication passed `diff`. For the versions that came out before `diff` there is nothing to start the induction with, and the IDs of tasks, lessons, traps and techniques have no table of their own, like `retired_skills` for skills. This is what an independent check of the scheme found during planning (RUN.md, T04, «Выяснилось при планировании»);
- 0.y.z by Cargo: semver says of 0.y.z "Anything MAY change at any time", and Cargo and oasdiff give y the role of the major. `api` and `diff` must read "the same major" alike, so the rule goes to the format.

— *Rejected:*
- the author declares the part themselves, and the comparison checks only `retired_skills` and the growth of the version, as in Frictionless and Conventional Commits ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4): the part could diverge from the content;
- exactly the part found, as in Elm: the author could not raise the major for the sake of what the comparison does not see, although semver allows it;
- major only from what breaks progress — the removal of a task, a lesson, a trap or a technique would be minor. Then `api` could not move a learner within a major itself: the lesson in which they stand could disappear;
- the comparison as part of `check`: `check` checks one module, and the comparison needs the packages of releases, which CI downloads and checks separately;
- the comparison only at the index build: the author would learn of an error at the release, after the merge;
- across a format transition to check only the growth of the version, as in В3 `modules`: nobody would check the retired skills in the pull request of the transition;
- a format transition is minor: in v0 a step of the format may change verdicts;
- to compare the author's YAML: a comment or the order of keys would require a new version, although the package is the same;
- strict equality of the fields of a check: `"2.50"` and `"2.5"` or rearranged trap values would give a major where no verdict changes;
- to count an added unit or trap value as minor, as an extension: some answer changes its verdict or its trap, and the author chose "the check has changed — major". Besides, an exact check of an extension is subtle: in `units` the longest unit at the end wins;
- to match the instances by numbers: a new constraint of a template shifts the numbers, and an edit that changes the check of no instance would become major;
- to compare only with the newest version, as T01 `modules` decided: see "Why";
- fixes of an old line, below the newest version: the module's folder has one line of versions (T01 modules), and a withdrawn newest one is fixed by a version above it (the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R63 spec). A version below the newest would have to be checked against the versions above it too, and against the rule (c) — each version above names its skills. It will be needed if `api` starts keeping a learner on an old major: then it is an edit of the rule (a);
- for 0.y.z — the same rules as for X.Y.Z: a module in development would go to 1.0.0 with the first retirement. Or 0.y.z without rules, by semver: then `api` and `diff` would read "the same major" differently.

— *Consequence:*
- «в реестр spec»: В14 — the rules of comparison, the table of version parts and the rule of 0.y.z — into the format text, with comparison vectors; В15 — the places that the format keeps in all versions, and the answer to В3 `modules`: CI gives `diff` all the published versions of the module;
- the point of card 3.7 spec "version comparison of a module" is decided, and the edit of the card — the fourth command of the CLI — is sent by T05;
- the code — the comparison function, the command `diff` and the tests — is written by the code epic, row 3.3 of COORDINATION.md `spec`.

— RUN.md, T04, table 3; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 1, 2.4, 2.5, 3, 3.5 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the author's decision in the planning of T05, by the addition to card 3.7 spec in v0.6.6 (R14). `diff` requires the package of the newest published version, checks the rules (c), (e) and (f) by those published versions that it was given, and names in the report which ones it did not compare with. Across a format transition `diff` checks only the growth of the version, and the graph is held by the review of `modules` until `spec` answers В15. So the CLI agrees with the card and with CI of `modules`, which gives only the newest. The argument "the versions before `diff` had no check" is in fact empty: `modules` has no releases yet. What remains is the rule (f), which by the newest alone sees only the IDs gone in it, and format transitions — this is В15. "`diff` takes all the published versions of the module" and the point on a format transition above read so. Across a transition `diff` checks the version by the rules (a) and (b) — above all and major —, and takes the format version of the previous one from the index's entry. Of "Rejected" above, two points are now accepted until `spec` answers: "to compare only with the newest version" — as what CI gives, and "across a format transition to check only the growth of the version" — with the major part. The "Consequence" on В15 stays a recommendation to `spec`. The rule (e) on an incomplete set of versions does not refuse but names the entry in the report. *Why:* the CLI must agree with card 3.7 spec and with CI of `modules` already now, and not after the answer to В15. *Rejected:* requiring all the versions until `spec` answers — CI of `modules` would not agree with the CLI; accepting the decision of `spec` whole and removing В15 — the rule (f) by the newest alone sees only the IDs gone in it, and nobody checks the skills across a format transition. — RUN.md, T16 and T05; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.4 and 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-04:* `spec` answered В14 and В15 with format 0.3 (R89 spec; R101 spec; R17). The principle of the parts, the table "change — version part", the rule of 0.y.z, the comparison of a check by meaning, the instances of a template by the values of the parameters and the rules of comparison are now the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27, with the vectors `vectors/versions/`, and `module-kit` has no rules and no table of its own. The format took the decision of this entry with corrections: the IDs of the rules are `module-version.*`, not by parts, and there are eight of them — the eighth holds the bands, `module-version.bands-kept`; the same version with another digest is a refusal of `module-version.above`, not of `module-index.one-package`; a new entry of `retired_skills` is a major change; a part also has "none". The 2026-11-02 amendment above held until the answer to В15: now `diff` requires the package of each version that the index names, and across a format transition checks the skills and the IDs by the places of the package that the format keeps in all versions ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2). The "Rejected" of this entry on comparing only with the newest and on a format transition holds again. — RUN.md, T17; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.4 and 3.5.

## The result of the detailing

**R15. The CLI of `module-kit` has a command of its own for open views, `open`, for the samples page build of `canvas`. It reads the index of a release of `modules`, the index of the newest release — for the marks of withdrawal — and the packages. It writes the file of open views on the model of `tutor-open-tasks`, the media files of the tasks, `LICENSE` and `NOTICE`, checked against the inventory, and it skips withdrawn versions. What it gives for a template and the digest of an open view the eval protocol will decide, and the command is built after it.**
- **What is decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.5 and 3.6):
  - the inputs — the index of the release, which CI of `canvas` checked with the recipe `release-verify`, the index of the newest release and the folders of the packages, which CI downloaded by the entries of the index. The command knows a package by the digest of the bytes of `inventory.json` and checks it by the chain, and the media files — by the listing of the device part;
  - for each module — the newest by semver version within the format's window that the newest index has not marked withdrawn. The rule is the same as for the version of a new attempt in `api`, and the library holds it;
  - the output:
    - `open-views.json` — `format`, `version` and the modules, each with its ID, version, `module_format_version`, package digest, the open view of the manifest (`open.Manifest`) and the open views of the tasks (`open.Task`), as a canonical text;
    - the media files of the tasks — in `media/<module ID>/<version>/<path>`, and beside them `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` of the same version: the device part carries them to everyone who receives the content ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10);
  - what the command gives for a template and how the digest of an open view is computed, the eval protocol will decide (В17 in table 3 of RUN.md), and the command is built after it, outside the first release (R16). Decided now is that the command exists and what form it has: this changes the card (В16);
  - that each module gets one version — the newest unwithdrawn one —, the executor chose in the execution: the author's choice named «каждую версию модуля», and taking all the unwithdrawn versions would mean repeating the module's tasks on the page. This is for the author to read through (RUN.md, T05, «Открыто»).

— *Why:* the author's decision 2026-11-02 in the planning of T05 (RUN.md, T05, «Решено автором»). The samples page build takes the tasks and the media files from a release of `modules` through the CLI of `module-kit` (R25 spec and the 2026-10-28 amendment to it). The open view is the format's semantics, and `module-kit` builds it by the marks `x-open` (R05 spec; R45 spec): `canvas` does not repeat it on its side, nor the rule of withdrawal (R63 spec). `canvas` expects from the CLI the open views, the media files, the ID and the version of the module, the package digest (architecture 3.5 canvas, `main` 0419717; Д4 canvas). The form of the output repeats `tutor-open-tasks` v0 (R64 tutor), which CI of `canvas` already reads, and the types are the same, `types/formatv0/open` of the module `spec` (R72 spec). Templates and the digest of an open view are a question of the eval protocol: how a sample names a task, it decides (В67 `tutor`), and the first batch of `modules` goes without templates (repositories 6 spec).

— *Rejected:*
- a narrow command — the open view of one part of one package to stdout: the loop, the withdrawals and the check of the media files `canvas` would repeat on its side;
- putting off the decision on the command until the eval protocol: `canvas` already expects it in its Д4, and the edit of the card would wait together with it. The command is built after the protocol anyway — what is put off is not the decision but the code;
- `canvas` builds the open views itself by `server.json`: the format's semantics would diverge from `module-kit`;
- deciding now what the command gives for a template — instances by numbers or all the instances: the form of the input would be invented before the protocol, and all the instances are up to 10,000 open views per template. These options stay options of the protocol (В17).

— *Consequence:* the edit of card 3.7 spec — the commands `diff` and `open` of the CLI — is В16 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»; the question to the eval protocol is В17.

— RUN.md, T05, table 3; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2, 2.5 and 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-04:* the eval protocol answered В17 (R93 spec; R17): for a template `open` gives the instance by the seed, and for each task and instance — the digest of the open view, `sha256:` and the SHA-256 of its canonical text, for an instance — with the numbers substituted. The command is built in the code epic, by its last task, after the first release: so the author decided in the slicing of E2 (R16 and the amendment to it). "the command is built after it" above reads so. — RUN.md, T17 and T40; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.5 and 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-06:* the command is built (R43). The call is `--index`, `--latest`, `--seed` and the package folders positionally, not `--packages` as one folder, as the sketch of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6, had it. The output is `open-views.json`: for each task the open view lies in an entry `{"open_view_digest", "seed", "task"}`, not as `open.Task` directly, and it holds the `release` of the entry. The media files with `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` are in `modules/<ID>/<version>/` with the layout `device/`, not in `media/<ID>/<version>/`. What is said above about the form of the output is history. — RUN.md, T40.

**R16. The next epic is E2 «Код формата 0.2»: the whole format 0.2 in the library and the CLI, by the dependencies of the parts. The tasks are merged one at a time, and the first release with code is the first release of the line 0.2: the last task raises `RELEASE_LINE` when the vector test passes without skips and the shared `release.yml` is pinned no lower than `.github` v0.1.7. The command `open` — after the eval protocol, outside the first release.**
- **What is decided** (RUN.md, table 2; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6):
  - the steps in order:
    1. preparation: the list `paths` by the template (Д7), coverage in Codecov (Д8), the module `spec` in `go.mod` at the tag of the submodule and its exclusion from Dependabot `gomod` (Д46 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`), gopls (Д5), the vector test with the skip list;
    2. the YAML reader and the module layout;
    3. schemas;
    4. the rules of parts and tiers;
    5. check kinds, normalization and the verdict;
    6. the Starlark sandbox and the predicate;
    7. the open view;
    8. the leak check;
    9. the package, the canonical text and reading a package by the chain of digests;
    10. the module index;
    11. the generator, templates and solvers;
    12. `diff`;
    13. the commands of the CLI `check`, `build`, `index` and `diff`, with a run of the binary on each OS (Д6);
    14. the first release — of the line 0.2.
  - before the epic — T08, the last task of E1: `release.yml` no lower than `.github` v0.1.7;
  - a format rule passes its vectors in the task that has the part by which it is checked: `task.trap-values`, `task.choice-key`, `task.choice-options` and `task.fraction-key` — with the verdict, `task.predicate-expression` — with the sandbox, `task.no-leaks` and `lesson.no-leaks` — with the leak check, the rules of templates and the vectors `packages/with-templates` and `packages/with-solver` — with templates and solvers, the open view of an instance — with them too. Until that task its vectors stand in the skip list;
  - the intermediate releases of the line 0.1 with code come out — each merge of code gives them (R07) —, but nobody takes them: CI of `modules` and `canvas` pin the tag of the first release of the line 0.2 (R77 spec), and `api` and the eval runner of `tutor` — the Go module at it;
  - the command `open` — after the eval protocol, when the protocol says what the command gives for a template and how the digest of an open view is computed (R15);
  - the release v0.2.0 is given by the change of `RELEASE_LINE` itself: with the list of Д7, the file `release.yml` is in `paths`. From v0.2.0 the skip list is empty, and the test does not let it grow, so each next release passes all the vectors too;
  - each task that chooses a library — YAML, JSON Schema, Unicode — names the options, the license and the exact version (RULES.md, Licenses; Exact versions only);
  - the exit code has one meaning for all the commands of the CLI: 0 — accepted, 1 — a refusal by the format rules, with their IDs, 2 — the command could not do its work. What is absent — a previous index, withdrawals, published versions — a command is told explicitly, not by a missing file;
  - the version of a module for a new attempt — the newest by semver unwithdrawn one within the format's window — is chosen by a function of the library, in answer to the question of `api` (architecture 3.3 api), and the command `open` uses it too ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6).

— *Why:* the author's decisions 2026-11-02 in the planning of T05 (RUN.md, T05, «Решено автором»):
- the first release with code passes all the vectors of format 0.2 — the Starlark predicates, templates and solvers too (R76 spec; Д53 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). But a merge of code issues a release by itself (R07), and with one task at a time releases come out all the time. The line 0.2 tells the first real release from the intermediate ones by a rule that is seen by the number, and it is raised by the task in which the vector test is already without skips;
- the order is by the dependencies of the parts: without the reader there are no schemas, without schemas — no rules, the verdict rests on normalization, and the predicate on the sandbox, the package on a checked module, the index on packages, `diff` on packages and the index. Templates and solvers come late: the first batch of `modules` goes without them (repositories 6 spec), and their vectors are needed by the first release anyway;
- `open` is needed by the samples page, and the page waits for the eval protocol (the epic E3 `spec`) and E5 `canvas`. The first app does not need it (COORDINATION.md `spec`, «Первое приложение»);
- `module-kit` reads "the first release with code" of R76 spec as the first release that others take — the first release of the line 0.2: the intermediate releases of the line 0.1 carry code, but not all of them pass the vectors. So this goes to `spec` too (В16);
- one meaning of the exit codes: CI of `modules` and `canvas` tell a refusal of a module from a failure without reading the output. An explicit "none" — as with the build of an index without a previous one (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R63 spec): a missing file will not pass silently;
- the version of a new attempt — by a rule of the library: the semver rule is one, and `module-kit` holds it, as it holds `diff` (R13).

— *Rejected:*
- the whole epic in one branch and one merge: a review of tasks without a merge, and the branch diverges from `main`;
- T08 as the last task of the epic, so that `release-verify` rejects the intermediate releases: it would hold by a side property, not by a rule, and T08 is a task of E1;
- first what `api` needs — the verdict, the open view, reading a package: `api` waits for the first release with all the vectors anyway, and the verdict without the module check has nothing to be tested on but the vectors;
- `open` in the first release: the form of its output for a template and the digest of an open view wait for the eval protocol;
- the version of a new attempt is chosen by the code of `api`: then there would be two semver rules;
- an exit code of its own for each command and an empty path as "none": CI would have to know each command, and a missing file would pass silently.

— *Consequence:* the epic E2 is in table 2 of RUN.md. Its tasks are sliced when the author takes the epic, after T08 and the archive of E1 («Как устроен план»). `modules` (row 3.6 of COORDINATION.md `spec`) and `api` (row 6.5) wait for its first release, and `canvas` (E5 canvas) for `open` too.

— RUN.md, T05, table 2; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.6.

## The tag of `spec` v0.9.2

**R17. `module-kit` is on `spec` v0.9.2, of the line 0.9 with module format 0.3: the submodule `spec/` is at this tag, the links to `spec` pinned to a tag name it, and `.devcontainer/` is byte for byte what the recipe of `spec` v0.9.2 builds. The tag answers all the rows «в реестр spec» В10–В18. The code epic E2 writes format 0.3, not 0.2, and begins with this move, T17; the tasks T18–T40 are written already on the new tag.**
- **What the tag brings `module-kit`** (`git diff v0.6.6 v0.9.2` in `spec/`, read 2026-11-04; tables 4 and 5 of RUN.md of `spec`, COORDINATION.md `spec`):
  - module format 0.3 — since v0.9.0, the first release of the line 0.9 ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2):
    - go.starlark.net — of the version 4f4267fb140d, one dialect for all the Starlark of the format — the check predicate, template expressions and the solver — with the list of 33 built-in names, and the step limit stops the computation when the count reaches it (R88 spec; [format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 15, 23 and 24). This is the answer to В10 and В11 — option (a) of both: the version that `module-kit` pinned (R11) has become the format's version;
    - version comparison of a module — the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27, with eight rules `module-version.*` and the vectors `vectors/versions/`, 152 cases, — and the places of the package that the format keeps in all versions, by the same text, section 2 (R89 spec; R101 spec). This is the answer to В14 and В15 — option (a) of both. Where it diverges from R13 is recorded by the amendment to R13 and [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5;
    - the index rule `module-index.version-above` and the form of withdrawals — `$defs/withdrawals` of the index's schema ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 26);
    - a module 0.2 is rejected by the rule `manifest.format-version`: while the format is v0, the window holds only the current version ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2);
    - schemas without `dependentSchemas`, and `$ref` does not stand beside `oneOf` or `anyOf` in one subschema ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 5);
  - the observation — the contract `novatrail.observation.v1` of the nested Go module `spec/contracts`, by the tag `contracts/vX.Y.Z`. `module-kit` joins two readings into one, by the slot check, and gives the verdict by the joined observation; the vectors are `contracts.Vectors` ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 10; R86 spec; the 2026-11-03 amendment to R64 spec). With the same code `module-kit` gives the eval runner of `tutor` the verdict by the truth of a sample's markup, and for the admission of a model only a report with the verdicts of `module-kit` is valid (R95 spec);
  - the eval protocol — the answer to В17: for a template `open` gives the instance by the seed, and the digest of an open view is `sha256:` and the SHA-256 of its canonical text, for an instance — with the numbers substituted (R93 spec). By this digest the eval runner knows the task of a sample, and the module package for it is built by `module-kit` ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 13). The check of a sample file by the CLI of `module-kit` the protocol rejected: it is held by the recipe of `spec` `sample-check` (R93 spec);
  - card 3.7 spec — the answer to В16: the commands `diff` and `open`, one meaning of the exit codes, the version of a new attempt — by a function of the library, the tag `contracts/vX.Y.Z` and the exclusion of the module `spec/contracts` from Dependabot `gomod`, and "To decide when detailed" is closed. "The first release with code" of `module-kit` is the first release of its line 0.2, and it passes all the vectors of format 0.3 (the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R76 spec);
  - the memory of third-party Starlark — the answer to В12: an addition to О-9 — from stage 4 the Starlark of a third-party module is executed in a separate process with a memory limit, and how, the tasks of `api` and `module-registry` decide (architecture 3.5 and 3.13 spec). Д10 in table 4 of RUN.md stays until stage 4;

## CI for the format's code

**R18. CI is ready for the format's code. Test coverage goes to Codecov from the shared `go.yml` of `.github` v0.1.15: the job "Go" calls it with `id-token: write`, and `ci-test` writes the profile `coverage/go.out`. `codecov.yml` is by the template of architecture 3.3 .github. The release is built by the list `paths` of the kind `go`. The link to `go.yml` was raised by hand, ahead of Dependabot, and the coverage badge went into the README by the same pull request.**
- **Coverage** (architecture 3.3 .github, «Покрытие», the release `.github` v0.1.15, commit 89d3cee, read 2026-11-04):
  - `ci-test` — `go test -race -count=1 -covermode=atomic -coverprofile=coverage/go.out ./...`: one Go profile by each package of the module, and a package without tests is in it too, with zeros. `.gitignore` holds `coverage/`;
  - the job `test` of the shared `go.yml` gives the profile as the artifact `go-coverage`. The job `coverage` — the only one with `id-token: write` — sends it to Codecov by OIDC, without a secret, with the Codecov CLI v11.3.2, by the sha256 sum in the file itself. A failed upload fails only a pull request. "Go / coverage" does not hold the merge; what holds it is `codecov/patch` (R48 spec);
  - `.github/codecov.yml` — word for word the text of the template: the threshold of changed lines is 90%, `codecov/project` only reports, and a comment comes only when the coverage changes. There is one upload, so `after_n_builds` is not needed. Codecov itself shortens the import path to the path in the repository;
  - the job "Go" in `ci.yml` — the text of the template: `contents: read` and `id-token: write`, the link to `go.yml` v0.1.15. Its `go.yml` is the same as in v0.1.12, the first release with the upload: of the shared workflows, v0.1.13–v0.1.15 changed only `release.yml` and `typescript.yml` (`gh api repos/altnovatrail/.github/compare/v0.1.10...v0.1.15`, 2026-11-04). `gh release verify v0.1.15 -R altnovatrail/.github` — "Release v0.1.15 verified!";
  - the coverage badge — in the README, after the badge of the Go version, by repositories 2.3 spec.
- **The list `paths`.** `release.yml` takes the list of the kind `go` from the template of architecture 3.3 .github: `*.go` without `*_test.go`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `justfile`, `LICENSE`, `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` and `release.yml` itself (the 2026-10-22 and 2026-11-01 amendments to R31 spec). The module has no files of `//go:embed` and no code in `testdata/`.
  - A release is now given by the code without tests, `go.mod` and `go.sum`, the recipes, the license, the Dockerfile and `release.yml`, the change of `RELEASE_LINE` included.
  - None is given by the tests, `devcontainer.json`, `.gitignore`, `.claude/`, the documents, the rest of CI and `.devcontainer/gopls/` without the Dockerfile: gopls is raised together with `GOPLS_VERSION` in the Dockerfile, and the release comes with it (R19).
  - The link to the shared `release.yml` is still v0.1.10: it is raised by Dependabot.
- **Checked:**
  - the release v0.1.4 is the first on the shared `release.yml` v0.1.10, at the merge commit 5e3bf34 of T17. `just -f spec/justfile release-verify module-kit v0.1.4 <file>` accepted all nine files: "signed by the shared release.yml of .github v0.1.10". `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` matched, and `module-kit_v0.1.4_linux_amd64 --version` answers `module-kit v0.1.4`. So it is checked that the shared release v0.1.10 signs as R77 spec expects;
  - the pull requests of Dependabot #27 (`go.yml` v0.1.2 → v0.1.10) and #26 (`codeql.yml` v0.1.3 → v0.1.10) — as in T08. Each has one line; SHA f3addb7 is the tag v0.1.10 (`git ls-remote`); `gh release verify v0.1.10 -R altnovatrail/.github` — "Release v0.1.10 verified!"; the checks are green, "CodeQL / analyze (go)" too; the commit of Dependabot is signed `Signed-off-by: dependabot[bot] <support@github.com>`. The author merged them 2026-11-04 — the merge commits 20abcc3 and 76b8233, — and #25, with a diff empty by that time, — 92d2922. These merges gave no releases: the edits are outside `paths`.

— *Why:* the format's code comes with its tests, and the merge with `codecov/patch`, at least 90% of the changed lines covered (RULES.md, How we work; R48 spec), and this must stand before the first line of the format's code (Д8 in table 4 of RUN.md). The list is needed so that the change of `RELEASE_LINE` gives v0.2.0 and so that edits that do not change the release give no releases: Dependabot would propose each such tag to `api` and `tutor` (R16; Д7). The link to `go.yml` was raised by hand by the author's decision 2026-11-04 (RUN.md, T18, «Решено автором»). Otherwise the pull request of the task would have shown neither "Go / coverage" nor `codecov/patch`, and Dependabot would have proposed v0.1.15 around 2026-11-09: the release came out 2026-11-02, the pause is three days, and the run is once a week. `spec` too raised it by hand the same way (the 2026-11-04 amendment to R30 spec). The badge — at once, also by the author's decision.

— *Rejected:*
- waiting for Dependabot for `go.yml` — the argument above. The order of the template — first the report, then the link — is needed when the link is raised by Dependabot: our own pull request carries both steps at once;
- raising by hand the other links to `.github` to v0.1.15 too: they need nothing new, and raising them is Dependabot's job, as R06 decided;
- `go.yml` v0.1.12 instead of v0.1.15: the file is the same, and the template says to take the latest release of `.github`;
- the badge after the first upload on `main`, by a separate pull request, as step 4 of the template says: one more pull request for a few minutes of "unknown" after the merge;
- checking the release that the merge of T18 will give in this same task: it comes out after the merge, and T19 checks it, as T18 checked v0.1.4;
- excluding `testdata/` from the list in advance: there is no code there, and the exclusion will be added by the task that puts code there (architecture 3.3 .github).

— *Consequence:* `codecov/patch` is put in the ruleset `main` by the author, after the first pull request of Dependabot with an upload, with `integration_id` 254, as for `spec` (rule 4 of RUN.md). The merge of T18 gives a release: in the list, `justfile`, the Dockerfile and `release.yml` changed. T19 checks it. Д7 and Д8 are closed. Amendments to R04 and R07. — `justfile`; `.gitignore`; `.github/codecov.yml`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `release.yml`; README.md; RUN.md, T18, T19, table 4; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 3.1 and 4.

**R19. gopls stands in the devcontainer image at an exact version, v0.24.0, and is built from its own lock file `.devcontainer/gopls/`, as in R12 tutor. This is a difference of the container of `module-kit` from the container of `spec`: on a move to a new tag, the Dockerfile is taken from the recipe of `spec` and these lines are put in again.**
- **What was set up:**
  - `.devcontainer/gopls/go.mod` — the module `devcontainer/gopls` with `tool golang.org/x/tools/gopls`, and the `go.sum` beside it holds the hash of each module of the build. They were given by `go mod init` and `go get -tool golang.org/x/tools/gopls@v0.24.0`;
  - in the stage `devcontainer` of the Dockerfile:
    - `ARG GOPLS_VERSION=0.24.0`, a `COPY` of the lock file and `go install -mod=readonly tool` into `/usr/local/bin`. A module without a hash or with another hash stops the build, as R34 spec stops an archive with a wrong sum;
    - `gopls version` must name `GOPLS_VERSION` as a whole line, `grep -xF`, so `v0.24.10` will not pass for `v0.24.1`;
    - everything the step writes stays in one temporary folder, and the same layer removes it: the cache of modules and of the build, and through `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` the telemetry counters of go and gopls too;
    - the stage `toolchain` does not change: the checks build it without a context and do not reach the `COPY`. So `ci-lint` checks `GOPLS_VERSION` in the Dockerfile against the version of gopls in the lock file. A pull request that raised one without the other, and a move to a tag of `spec` that lost the step, go red on "Go / lint", not on Rebuild Container;
  - `devcontainer.json` — the Go extension does not install or update its tools and does not lint on save: `go.toolsManagement.autoUpdate`, `go.toolsManagement.checkForUpdates` and `go.lintOnSave`. Linting is done by `just ci-lint`. The comment to `context` says that the image takes only `gopls/` from its folder;
  - `dependabot.yml` — `.devcontainer/gopls/` is not in the ecosystem `gomod`. Dependabot would raise gopls without `GOPLS_VERSION`, and the check would stop the build. gopls is raised by `go get -tool` in that folder together with `GOPLS_VERSION`. But Dependabot alerts and security updates are on for the repository (the GitHub API, `repos/altnovatrail/module-kit/vulnerability-alerts` and `automated-security-fixes`, 2026-11-04), and the dependency graph reads each `go.mod`, this one too. A vulnerability in a module of the lock file is a finding before the next task (rule 12 of RUN.md). Its pull request is checked as our own. One that raises a dependency of gopls is merged. One that raises gopls itself goes red on `ci-lint` until `GOPLS_VERSION` is raised in it too;
  - the license of gopls is BSD-3-Clause ([LICENSE golang/tools](https://github.com/golang/tools/blob/master/LICENSE); the GitHub API, `repos/golang/tools/license`, read 2026-11-04). gopls is a development tool: it is shipped nowhere and puts none of its own code into what ships.
- **Checked without Docker:** the lock file, copied to a temporary folder, builds gopls by the same `go install -mod=readonly tool` with temporary `GOPATH` and `GOCACHE`. `gopls version` — `golang.org/x/tools/gopls v0.24.0`: the check `grep -xF` passes, and the prefix `v0.24` does not. The telemetry went into the temporary folder, and `go mod verify` — "all modules verified". `ci-lint` passes, and with `GOPLS_VERSION=0.24.1` and without `ARG GOPLS_VERSION` it fails and names both versions. The image was not built here — Д14 in table 4 of RUN.md.

— *Why:* the author's decision 2026-11-04 in the planning of T18 (RUN.md, T18, «Решено автором»). Without gopls in the image the Go extension installs the latest version itself, not an exact one (RULES.md, Exact versions only) — Д5. `go install …@v<version>` in the Dockerfile SonarCloud counts as the finding docker:S8545 (R04), and the lock file fixes it by code. `tutor`, with the same step, has no findings S8545 (the SonarCloud API, `api/issues/search?componentKeys=altnovatrail_tutor&rules=docker:S8545`, 2026-11-04), and Scorecard Pinned-Dependencies marks in its Dockerfile only `npm`. A second `go.mod` without packages CodeQL autobuild passes, writing "No packages found." (R12 tutor), and the `./...` of the recipes does not go into it. gopls v0.24.0 is its latest release, of 2026-10-14 (`https://proxy.golang.org/golang.org/x/tools/gopls/@latest`, read 2026-11-04).

— *Rejected:*
- installing the lock module by `postCreateCommand`: the Dockerfile would stay a copy of `spec`, but each creation of the container would go to the network, and gopls would land in `~/go/bin`, which is not in `PATH` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R04);
- leaving gopls to the Go extension — the argument above;
- `go install …@v<version>`, with `-mod=readonly` or without, — rejected already by R04: S8545 marks both;
- marking the finding S8545 as false: rule 12 of RUN.md counts this as a last resort, and the lock file fixes it by code.

— *Consequence:* the container of `module-kit` again differs from the container of `spec`, although the 2026-11-02 amendment to R04 said that it did not. On a move to a new tag, `.devcontainer/` is taken from `editDevcontainer` and the lines of this entry are put in again: the gopls step in the Dockerfile, the comment to `context` and three settings in `devcontainer.json`, and `.devcontainer/gopls/` stays. So that this does not happen to each repository in Go, the same step for the kind `go` is proposed to the recipe of `spec` — В19 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec». Rebuild Container is a step of the author, Д14. Д5 is closed. — `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`, `.devcontainer/gopls/`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `justfile` (`ci-lint`); RUN.md, T18, tables 3 and 4; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 4.

## The modules of `spec` and the vector test

**R20. The code takes the format and the vectors as the Go modules of `spec` — `github.com/altnovatrail/spec` and `github.com/altnovatrail/spec/contracts`, both v0.9.2, at the tag of the submodule and outside Dependabot, and `ci-lint` checks them against the submodule. The library is eight Go packages by the parts of the format, and the mechanisms are in `internal/`. The vector test is one for all the cases, `internal/vectors`, and it fails on a kind that it does not know. What the code does not pass yet stands in the skip list, a line per case, and `ci-test` counts coverage for each package of the module.**
- **The modules of `spec`** (R64 spec; card 3.7 spec):
  - `go get github.com/altnovatrail/spec@v0.9.2 github.com/altnovatrail/spec/contracts@v0.9.2`. The nested module's version in `go.mod` is `v0.9.2` too, and the tag is `contracts/v0.9.2` at the same commit 6f86ff5. In `go.mod` there are two `require`, and in `go.sum` also protobuf v1.36.12: the tests of the Go package `contracts` require it;
  - the test takes only `spec.Format` and `contracts.Vectors`, and their Go packages import nothing outside the standard library. So neither protobuf nor connect-go goes into the build yet, and `ci-licenses` and `ci-vuln` do not see them. protobuf, BSD-3-Clause, will come in with the package of the observation `novatrail.observation.v1`, T28, and connect-go is not imported by that package;
  - Dependabot `gomod` does not raise them: `ignore` of both by name, as `tutor` excludes `spec` (R64 spec);
  - `ci-lint` checks three values: the commit that the git index recorded for `spec/` (`git ls-files --stage -- spec`), and the commit that the Go module proxy names as the origin of the version of each module (`Origin.Hash` in `go mod download -json <module>@<version>`), — and that the version of `contracts` is the same as that of `spec`. At v0.9.2 all three are 6f86ff5. The submodule is not needed for the check: in CI the index is the tree of the commit, and the checkout of the shared `go.yml` does not take the submodule. Here the index holds the move to the tag as soon as `spec/` is added, even before the commit. A move that shifted one side without the other goes red on "Go / lint", and a proxy that gave no origin the recipe names. A probe: with `spec` v0.9.1 or `contracts` v0.9.1 in `go.mod`, and also with the submodule at v0.9.1 added to the index, the recipe fails and names all three values.
- **Go packages** — each with a `doc.go`, which says what it holds, by which section of the format text, and who calls it. There is no code in them yet: it is brought by the tasks of the epic, and they also decide the names of functions and what is inside (R16). A shared internal Go package — the parsing of semver, the parsing of numbers — is started by the task that needs it a second time.

  | Go package | What it holds | Tasks | Who calls it |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `module` | the module check: the layout and the window of versions, the schemas, the rules of parts, the tiers, the rules of templates and leaks in texts; the report with the rules' IDs | T21, T22, T24, T25, T31, T32 | the CLI, `module-registry` |
  | `verdict` | the check kinds, normalization, the verdict of an answer slot and of an item, the trap; the join of two readings and the verdict by an observation | T26–T28 | `api`, the eval runner of `tutor` |
  | `openview` | the open view of the parts and of an instance, the digest of an open view | T23, T31, T33 | `api`, the eval runner of `tutor`, the command `open` |
  | `leak` | the leak check | T29 | `api`, the module check |
  | `template` | the instances of a template and the instance of an attempt | T30–T32 | `api`, the module check |
  | `modulepackage` | the canonical view, the package build, reading by the chain of digests | T33 | the CLI, `api`, the eval runner of `tutor`, `module-registry` |
  | `moduleindex` | the build and the reading of the index, the version of a new attempt | T34 | the CLI, `api` |
  | `moduleversion` | version comparison, the rules `module-version.*` | T35, T36 | the command `diff`, `module-registry` |
  | `internal/yaml` | the YAML reader and the rules `yaml.*` | T20 | the module check, the vector test |
  | `internal/schema` | JSON Schema 2020-12 and the marks `x-open` | T22, T23 | the module check, the open view |
  | `internal/sandbox` | the Starlark sandbox (R11) | T27 | the verdict, templates |
  | `internal/random` | the generator and the places of randomness (R10) | T30 | templates |
  | `internal/canonical` | the canonical text (R09) | T33 | the package, the index, the digest of an open view |
  | `internal/vectors` | the vector test and the skip list | T19 | — |

  `package` is a Go keyword, so the module package is named `modulepackage`, and with it `moduleindex` and `moduleversion`, like the prefixes of the rules `module-index.*` and `module-version.*`.
- **The vector test** — `TestVectors` in `internal/vectors/vectors_test.go`:
  - it sorts each file of `spec.Format` and `contracts.Vectors` into cases. The text, `VERSION` and the schemas are not cases, `eval/v1/` are vectors of `canvas` and `tutor` ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 12), and a file that no kind takes is a refusal with its path. The kinds are by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 3, 8 and 9, and the [contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 10: the examples of a part `valid/` and `invalid/` — for the nine parts with examples, the example of a whole module, `rules/<rule ID>/` — except the rules of the index and of versions, whose vectors lie in `indexes/` and `versions/`, — `open-view/<part>/` — by two files, and only by them, `verdicts/` — for the four check kinds that have a verdict, `leaks/`, `random/`, `templates/`, `tiers/`, `packages/`, `indexes/` and `versions/` — each folder with exactly what section 9 requires, nothing extra and nothing missing, — and `observation/v1/`. A new kind, a new part or a new check kind comes with a move to a tag, and the test goes red until the task of the move knows it;
  - a case is a subtest named by its path in `spec`: `format/vectors/rules/yaml.encoding/byte-order-mark.yaml`, `contracts/vectors/observation/v1/boxes.json`. At v0.9.2 there are 595 of them;
  - each kind is passed by its runner from the table `runners`. For now all the kinds have one — `noCode`, "no code for this kind yet". A task of the epic puts its own code in its place and strikes out of the list what it passed;
  - the list is `internal/vectors/testdata/skipped.txt`: a line per case, in the order of bytes, each once. Now all 595 are in it. It is not code, and it is not in the list `paths` of the release;
  - a case from the list is executed too: if it passed, the test fails and says to strike it out; if not, the subtest is skipped. A case not on the list must pass. The test also fails on a line of the list that is not in `spec`, on a kind that has no case left, and on a list out of order;
  - that a task only strikes out, the test holds as far as it can: only a case that does not pass can be added to the list — a regression or a vector of a new tag —, and such a line is seen in the diff. The rest is held by the review. From T39 the list is empty, and the test holds the emptiness (R16);
  - the tests of the test itself — `inventory_test.go`, on `fstest.MapFS`: each kind and the path of its case, the refusal on a file of each unknown kind and on a folder that lacks something, the parsing of the list, extra lines and all four outcomes of a case. A probe: a line struck out without code, an extra line and a line out of order fail `TestVectors`.
- **Coverage** — `ci-test` with `-coverpkg=./...`. By default Go counts for a package only its own tests: "The default is for each test to analyze only the package being tested" (`go help testflag`, Go 1.27.3, read 2026-11-04). Without it, `codecov/patch` would count as uncovered the lines of `verdict` that the vector test reached. The profile stays one file, `coverage/go.out`: a block that the tests of two packages covered is in it twice, and `go tool cover -func` reads it — 83.3%, as before T19.

— *Why:* the author's decisions 2026-11-04 in the planning of T19 (RUN.md, T19, «Решено автором»), all by the plan's recommendation:
- Go packages by parts: `api`, the eval runner of `tutor` and the CLI call different things, and each takes its own part, and the names follow the format's terms and the prefixes of the rules. Mechanisms that have no consumer of their own are in `internal/`, as the generator in R10;
