- a line per case: the tasks of E2 pass parts of kinds too — T24 the examples of a whole module, T29 the leak without templates, T31 templates without the solver, T35 and T36 the vectors `versions/`, — and "a case from the list passed" is seen only for the line of one case. What to strike out, the test tells the task itself;
- one test for all the cases and `-coverpkg`: the test itself sees each case and each kind, and coverage counts where the code lies;
- the check in `ci-lint`: R64 spec says to move the modules and the submodule by one move, and the check catches a move that shifted one of them. In the same way R19 holds `GOPLS_VERSION` with the lock file of gopls.

— *Rejected:*
- one Go package at the root of the module, `modulekit`, with the parts in `internal/`: a facade of wrappers and type aliases for each part, and a consumer takes one of its parts anyway;
- a list by kind and part, by prefixes like `format/vectors/rules/yaml.*`: shorter, but a partly passed kind would have to be split, and "the entry already passes" cannot be checked for a prefix;
- a line per case with the task that will strike it out: the split of `versions/` between T35 and T36 is decided by the planning of T35, and the signal "strike it out" the test gives itself anyway;
- the vectors in the tests of each Go package through a shared helper, without `-coverpkg`: the central test would not see whether a package executed its cases, and a forgotten kind would go unpassed silently;
- checking the version of the modules by a Go test: the checkout of the shared `go.yml` does not take the submodule, and the code does not read `spec/` (R21 spec);
- not checking, but holding it by the move to a tag and the review: a move that forgot one of the sides would pass green;
- `go get …@contracts/v0.9.2`, as T19 had it: the version of the nested module is `v0.9.2`, and Go finds the tag with the folder's prefix by it itself.

— *Consequence:*
- the task of the epic for a part puts its runner into `runners` and strikes out what it passed, and the lines of its code count for it, wherever the test passed through them;
- protobuf comes into the build with T28, and `ci-licenses` and `ci-vuln` will see it there;
- the list `paths` includes `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `justfile` and the new `doc.go`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1;
- on a move to a new tag of `spec`, the task moves the submodule and both modules together, and the new vectors of the tag go into the code or into the list, otherwise the vector test is red;
- amendments to R04 and R18.

— RUN.md, T19; `go.mod`, `go.sum`; `justfile` (`ci-lint`, `ci-test`); `.github/dependabot.yml`; `module/`, `verdict/`, `openview/`, `leak/`, `template/`, `modulepackage/`, `moduleindex/`, `moduleversion/`, `internal/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 3.1, 3.6 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-04:* the examples of a whole module are passed by T22, not by T24, as "Why" above says. With the runner of the kind `examples/module` an example passes as soon as `module.Check` does not reject it, and a passed case from the list fails the test. T24 takes them through everything that the check holds by that time, as its body says too. A move to a new tag of `spec` moves, together with the submodule and the Go modules, also the copy of the schemas `internal/schema/schemas/` — from the Go module cache —, otherwise `TestSchemasAreSpecs` goes red (R23). — RUN.md, T22.

— *Amendment 2026-11-06:* T39 removed the skip list together with its parsing and the stub `noCode`: "From T39 the list is empty, and the test holds the emptiness" above is carried out so that there is no list, and a case that does not pass fails the test (R42). What is said above about the list, about what a task strikes out of it, and "the new vectors of the tag go into the code or into the list" in "Consequence" is history: the new vectors of a tag come only with code. — RUN.md, T39.

## The YAML reader

**R21. A file of a module is read by `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` v4.0.0-rc.9 into a tree of nodes, and the package `internal/yaml` goes around it in passes: they read as YAML 1.2.2 where v4 keeps to YAML 1.1 or lets through what YAML 1.2.2 rejects, and they check the rules `yaml.*`. The value is a JSON value with an integer as `int64` and a double as `float64`, and a refusal names each breach with its rule ID and place, or says that the file is not YAML. The vectors `rules/yaml.*` are passed, all the files of the format are read, and the YAML test suite lies in `testdata` and holds the reader together with a fuzz test.**

- **What to read with** ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 4; read 2026-11-04; the sources — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4):
  - `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` v4.0.0-rc.9 of 2026-11-02 — the YAML organization, Apache-2.0, and the files carried over from libyaml are MIT, by its `NOTICE`; it has no dependencies. This is the go-yaml that R45 spec cites. v4.0.0 has not come out yet, and the README promised it for October 2026;
  - on the 2302 YAML files of `spec.Format` outside `rules/yaml.*`, each of which is read without breaches by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 8 and 9, v4 refuses not once. v3.0.6 refuses on 195: a plain scalar with `?` in a flow collection it cuts off, by YAML 1.1. goccy/go-yaml v1.19.2 refuses on the valid complex keys `? {a: b}` and `? [a, b]`, and does not check UTF-8.
- **Where v4 diverges from YAML 1.2.2 — and which pass closes it.** Each pass is in its own file of the package, and a test holds each row:

  | Where v4 diverges | Pass | Test |
  |---|---|---|
  | a BOM at the start it lets through silently, after `...` it is a parse error; `%YAML 1.2` it rejects: its parser expects 1.1 | the level of the stream (`text.go`): a BOM is `yaml.encoding`, a line beginning with `%` is `yaml.no-directives`; the BOM is removed, and `%` becomes `#` of the same length, and a directive not followed by `---` is "not YAML" | `TestRead`, "byte order mark…", "directive…" |
  | invalid UTF-8 and control characters it rejects with no place | the bytes (`text.go`): `yaml.encoding` at the first invalid byte; a character outside the printable set is "not YAML" with a place; a file not in UTF-8 is not read further: keys that differ only in invalid bytes would merge | "not UTF-8", "control character…" |
  | U+0085, U+2028 and U+2029 it counts as a line break, as YAML 1.1 does: `a: x<U+2028>  y` it reads as `"x\u2028y"`, and YAML 1.2.2 as `"x\u2028  y"` | replacement by characters of the private use area that are neither in the text nor among its escapes, and back in the values (`text.go`) | "line separator…", "next line…", "a stand-in written as an escape" |
  | an escaped surrogate and a code above U+10FFFF are a scanner error; an anchor `&😀`, an alias without an anchor and a tag with an unknown handle are parse errors | by `LoadError` (`errors.go`): a surrogate is `yaml.characters`, an anchor and an alias are `yaml.no-anchors`, a tag is `yaml.no-tags`; an anchor without a name, a tag outside the grammar of YAML 1.2.2 and a handle that `%TAG` did not give are "not YAML" | "escaped surrogate…", "8XYN…", "alias with no anchor", "tag …" |
  | a plain scalar it resolves by its own schema; `\/` it silently reads as `/`; a repeated key in the tree it does not reject | the walk of the tree (`walk.go`), as `Walker` in `spec/tools/format-check/yaml.mjs` | all the rules `yaml.*` |
  | under-indented lines of a quoted string and of a flow collection, tabs in indentation (9C9N, QB6E, DK95/01, Y79Y/003), leading empty lines of a block scalar (S98Z), an implicit key longer than 1024 characters, U+FEFF outside quotes | `layout.go`: each is "not YAML" (YAML 1.2.2, 5.2, 6.1, 6.3, 7.4.2, 8.1.1.1, 8.2.2) | "9C9N…", "QB6E…", "S98Z…", "an implicit key beyond the limit", "a byte order mark in…" |

  The messages of v4 by which `errors.go` reads are pinned by these tests: a version that says otherwise will fail them.
- **What remains:** 2SXE and W5VH of the YAML test suite — the anchor names `&a:` and `&:@*!$"<foo>:`, which the scanner of v4 does not read. Such a file breaks `yaml.no-anchors`, and the refusal is "not YAML": it is rejected all the same, but the rule is not named. This is Д16 in table 4 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md) and the list `suiteUnpassed` of the test.
- **The second implementation of `spec` in the same places.** Where YAML 1.2.2 and `yaml.mjs` agree, the reader reads as both do. The divergences of `yaml.mjs` from YAML 1.2.2 — CR as a line break, `...` at the start of the stream, U+FEFF outside quotes — go to `spec` as row В21. A character outside the printable set inside quotes R45 spec rejects, and YAML 1.2.2 says to accept it (5.1); the reader rejects it, as R45 spec does, and the question is row В20.
- **API** — `yaml.Read(data []byte) (any, error)`. The value is `map[string]any`, `[]any`, `string`, `int64` for a number with neither a fraction nor an exponent, `float64` for the others, `bool` or `nil`: the integer and the double are told apart by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 25, and `types/formatv0` holds integers as `int64`. A refusal is a `*Refusal` with the breaches `Breach{Rule, Line, Column, Message}` in the order of places: the line and the column from 1, the column in characters of the file as it is written, and a BOM is a character too. `Rule` is empty when the file is not YAML: such a refusal has no ID in the format. A panic of v4 is caught and becomes the refusal "not YAML". On a parse error, the documents read before it are checked the same way, except for a BOM outside quotes: where quotes stand beyond the error is not known.
- **Tests** (`internal/yaml`):
  - `TestRead` — a table: each rule, the places, the types of the value and each finding of the planning;
  - `TestReadsTheFormat` — all 2302 files of `spec.Format` outside `rules/yaml.*` are read without breaches. It also guards each raise of v4;
  - `TestYAMLTestSuite` — the YAML test suite, the data release `data-2022-01-17`, MIT, in `testdata/yaml-test-suite/`, in two files — `valid.json` with the valid cases and `error.json` with the invalid ones: 402 cases. An invalid one is rejected, and a valid one is read into the value of its `in.json` — 181 checked against it — or is rejected by a rule, not as "not YAML". A case from the list `suiteUnpassed` that passes fails the test;
  - `FuzzRead` — the corpus: 50 vectors, the YAML test suite and the cases of `TestRead`. On any input — a value or a refusal, without a panic, the value within the domain of the format, and its JSON text, written by a writer of its own, reads back into the same value. `json.Marshal` does not do for this: `1e20` it writes as an integer, and U+007F–U+009F and U+FEFF as they are;
  - the vector test `internal/vectors`: the runner of the kind `rules` reads a case `yaml.*` and passes when there is a refusal and each breach is of the rule of the folder ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 9). 50 lines left the skip list; at v0.9.2 it has 545.

— *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-11-04 when planning T20 (RUN.md, T20, «Решено автором»):
- v4 and the passes are the recommendation of the plan. v4 reads all the files of the format, its scanner is libyaml, and each of its divergences from YAML 1.2.2 is bounded and closed by a pass with a test;
- what v4 lets through and YAML 1.2.2 rejects is closed in T20 itself, together with the YAML test suite. The plan recommended moving this into a separate task before the first release, so that the review of T20 stays one check (rule 11 of RUN.md); the author decided otherwise, and the review of T20 is larger on purpose.

— *Rejected:*
- a parsing of YAML 1.2.2 of our own — the most exact, but it is thousands of lines and several tasks before T21, and a risk of its own in code that reads others' input;
- `go.yaml.in/yaml/v3` and goccy/go-yaml — by the probe above;
- waiting for v4.0.0 — the epic would stand still, and a raise from the RC to the release will be checked by the corpus test, the suite and the pinned messages;
- a separate task for what v4 lets through, and only a debt in table 4 — the author's decision above;
- refusing on U+0085, U+2028 and U+2029 instead of the replacement — the reader would reject a file that YAML 1.2.2 and the second implementation of `spec` read;
- `json.Marshal` in the fuzz test — for the reason above;
- parsing the errors of v4 by their sense rather than by their message: its errors have no other sign, and the tests catch a new message.

— *Consequence:*
- `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` is the first third-party module of the build. Dependabot `gomod` raises it: a pull request with v4.0.0 passes the corpus test, the YAML test suite and the pinned messages, and with it Д16 is taken up again;
- the release binary does not carry it yet: `cmd/module-kit` does not import the reader. With the command `check`, T37, the `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` of go-yaml must go with the release — Д15;
- the list `paths` of the release includes `go.mod`, `go.sum` and the files `internal/yaml/*.go` without tests, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1; `testdata` is not in it;
- the first `FuzzXxx` in the repository — the finding Fuzzing in Scorecard should close (R33 spec);
- in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md) — В20 and В21 «в реестр spec», in table 4 — Д15 and Д16.

— RUN.md, T20; `go.mod`, `go.sum`; `internal/yaml/`; `internal/vectors/vectors_test.go`, `internal/vectors/testdata/skipped.txt`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.6 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-06:* `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` is v4.0.0, not an RC: it came out on 2026-11-04 (`https://proxy.golang.org/go.yaml.in/yaml/v4/@latest` — `v4.0.0` of 2026-11-04T18:36:35Z, commit f2f9563, read 2026-11-06). It was raised by hand by the author's decision, before the `cooldown` pause of Dependabot (R45). From rc.9 it differs by five commits: documentation, the removed API of the plugin Limit, which the code does not take, and the comparison of numbers in its own test of the YAML suite (`gh api repos/yaml/go-yaml/compare/v4.0.0-rc.9...v4.0.0`). `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` are the same. The test of the format's corpus, the YAML test suite and the fuzz test of the reader pass. 2SXE and W5VH still do not pass — v4.0.0 itself keeps them in `yts/known-failing-tests` —, so `suiteUnpassed` has not changed. — RUN.md, table 4.

— *Second amendment 2026-11-06:* 2SXE and W5VH pass, and `suiteUnpassed` is empty: at an anchor or an alias whose name by YAML 1.2.2 ends with a colon, a pass of its own, `colonAnchor`, names `yaml.no-anchors` (R46). What is said in "What remains" above is history.

## The module check

**R22. The module check is `module.Check` over the folder of a module as an `fs.FS` and `module.CheckFile` over a file of a part. It reads the folder by the layout of the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10, holds the rules `module.*`, reads each file of a part with the YAML reader (R21), and takes the format version from the manifest before anything else: the window is one version, `FormatVersion`, 0.3. A module of a version outside the window gets this one breach. The report is the breaches with the rule ID and the place, in the order of places, not of the listing. The layout, the forms of names and the version the code holds itself, and the tests check them against the format text, the schema and `VERSION` of `spec.Format`.**

- **API** (`module`):
  - `Check(fsys fs.FS) (Report, error)` — the module whose folder is the root of `fsys`. An error comes only when the folder or a file cannot be read: that is code 2 of the CLI commands, and a report with breaches is code 1 ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6);
  - `CheckFile(part string, data []byte) (Report, error)` — a file of a part by itself, as an example of a part and a vector of a rule of a part laid out as a file are checked ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 8 and 9): the YAML rules, and for the manifest the version. A part outside the layout is an error;
  - `Report{Breaches []Breach}`, `Breach{Rule, Path, Line, Column, Pointer, Message}`. `Rule` is empty when the file of a part is not YAML. `Path` is the path from the module's folder with `/`, as the listing gives it; `.` is the folder itself, as `fs.FS` names its root, and a file of `CheckFile` has no path, and `Path` is empty. The line and the column are in the file of the part, as with `yaml.Breach`, and in `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` only LF ends a line, as with the format's plain text; 0 is a breach of the whole file or of a value in it. `Pointer` is the value, a JSON Pointer in URI fragment form, as the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 8, writes a place: `#/module_format_version`, `#/id`. The order is the path, the line, the column, the pointer, the rule, the message;
  - `FormatVersion = "0.3"` and `ReadsFormat(v string) bool`: while the format is v0, the library reads one version ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2).
- **The order of the check** — the rules of the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10, in order, and the version before them:
  1. the listing. The names are as the folder gives them, letter case and all. A file or folder beginning with `.` is not the module's, with everything inside it. A folder without files holds nothing. A link the listing does not follow: it is `module.layout`, as is everything that is neither a file nor a folder — `format-check` of `spec` decides the same way;
  2. the manifest. No file `manifest.yaml` is `module.manifest`. If there is one, it is read first, and a version outside the window, named by a string of the form MAJOR.MINOR, is `manifest.format-version`, and only it stays in the report. Without a manifest, or with a manifest that the YAML rules rejected, the module is checked by the reader's version: `Manifest.yaml` in place of `manifest.yaml` breaks both `module.manifest` and `module.layout`, as the format text itself says;
  3. the layout. A file not at a path of the layout is `module.layout`. A file of a part is read by the YAML reader, and its breaches go into the report with the file's path. In a file at a path with `<id>`, a string `id` not equal to the name before the extension is `module.file-ids`;
  4. `media/` — `module.media`: a path of the form `media_path` from `media/` and the signature of the format by the extension, from the first 14 bytes of the file;
  5. `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` — `module.license` and `module.plain-text`. Each of the three faults of plain text — not UTF-8, a BOM and CR — gives one breach, at its first place, and for CR the message also says how many there are.
- **What is decided where the format text is silent:**
  - a manifest that the YAML rules rejected names no version: such a file has no value. The module is checked by the reader's version, as without a manifest, and the report holds the YAML breaches of the manifest and everything else. A manifest of 0.4 with such an error will get breaches by 0.3, and `manifest.format-version` only when the author fixes the YAML: the test "a manifest of 0.4 that the YAML rules refuse…" holds this;
  - a version that is not a string of the form MAJOR.MINOR, a manifest without one and a manifest that is not an object are not `manifest.format-version`, but a breach of the manifest's schema by the reader's version, T22. So says the example `examples/manifest/invalid/format-version-as-number.yaml` — `type at #/module_format_version` —, and so does `format-check` of `spec`: "A version that is no MAJOR.MINOR string is the schema's to refuse";
  - `module.file-ids` is only a string `id` not equal to the name. No `id`, or one that is not a string, is the schema's: a rule says what the schema cannot say ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 7). `format-check` reads `id` only in a file that passed the schema; whether a rule of a part runs on a file that the schema rejected T22 decides;
  - the header of an MP3 frame is no shorter than four bytes. The format text names three bytes, and WHATWG MIME Sniffing, 6.2.3, "match an mp3 header", step 1, — "If length is less than 4, return false", and so does `format-check`. This is row В22 of table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md).
- **The code against the format text.** The layout is the table `layout` in `layout.go`; the forms `local_id` and `media_path` are a pattern, a length and `not`; the version is `FormatVersion`. The tests of `format_test.go` check the table against the table of the layout of the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10, — `README.md` from `spec.Format` —, the rules `module.*` against its table of rules, the forms against the `$defs` of `common.schema.json`, where nothing but `type`, `pattern`, `maxLength`, `not` and `description` should be, the extensions of `media_path` against the signatures, and `FormatVersion` against `VERSION`. A move to a tag that changes any of this turns red until the code catches up.
- **Tests** (`module`):
  - `TestCheck` — a table of modules on `fstest.MapFS`: each rule, names beginning with `.`, empty folders, links and pipes, letter case, paths with `<id>` and `media_path` at the bounds of length and of the names of Windows devices, signatures, versions in the window and outside it; `TestMessages`, `TestBreachString` and `ExampleCheck` — the text of the report;
  - `TestOrderOfListing` — the same report when the folder is listed in reverse order; `TestReadErrors` — an error, not a report, at each read; `TestSymbolicLinkOnDisk` — links to a file and to a folder on disk, through `os.DirFS`;
  - `TestChecksTheFormat` — all 397 folders of `spec.Format` laid out as a module — the examples of a whole module, the folder cases of `rules/`, the modules of the vectors of packages, of the index and of versions —, and all 236 files of parts — the examples, the file cases of `rules/` and `part.yaml` of the open view — give not one breach except of the rule of their case;
  - `FuzzCheck` — any file at any path of a module and any file of each part: a report without an error and without a panic, in form and in order. The corpus is the files of the vectors of `module.*` and `manifest.format-version`;
  - the vector test: a folder case is passed by `Check`, and a file case of a rule of a part by `CheckFile` of the part from the rule ID. 20 lines left the skip list — `rules/module.*` and `rules/manifest.format-version` —; it has 525.

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T21 — «—»):
- two functions: `check` of the CLI and `module-registry` check a module whole, and an example of a part and a vector of a rule in a file are a file by itself ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 8 and 9);
- the error apart from the report: the exit code of the CLI tells a refusal by the rules from a failure of input and output;
- the window as a constant with a test: a move of `spec` to 0.4 turns red on the test and the vectors, rather than silently reading a module of 0.4 with the code of 0.3;
- one breach for a module of another version: "A module is checked, built and run by the version it names" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2), and its layout and rules the reader does not know;
- the layout and the forms as Go data with a test: `format-check` of `spec` reads the table of the layout by parsing markdown, and the forms with the schema validator. `module-kit` has no validator before T22, and parsing a normative text at each check is one more dependency;
- the pointer already in T21: the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 8, writes the place of a value as a JSON Pointer, and T22 will put the place of a schema breach there too.

— *Rejected:*
- the window from `VERSION` of `spec.Format` at run time — a move to a tag with a new format version would silently shift the window of the code of the old one;
- checking the rest of the module even with a version outside the window — by the layout and the rules of 0.3, a module of 0.2 or 0.4 would get breaches that are not its own;
- stopping at a manifest that the YAML rules rejected: the author of a module of 0.3 would see one breach per round, and without a manifest the check goes by the reader's version anyway;
- searching for the version in a manifest that the YAML rules rejected: the value of such a file is not defined — which of two equal keys, which number outside ±(2^53−1);
- `manifest.format-version` also for a version that is not a MAJOR.MINOR string — two breaches of one error, and the format's example names the schema here;
- reading the layout and the forms from `README.md` and the schema at run time — above;
- the report as an error, like `*yaml.Refusal`: warnings too will go into the report of `check` — about a picture with transparent pixels ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6) —, and an empty report is not an error;
- one breach `module.plain-text` per file, as with `format-check`: the BOM and the CRLF of a file from Notepad the author fixes in one go only if they see both;
- the header of an MP3 frame of three bytes, as it can be read in the format text — it would diverge from WHATWG and `format-check`.

— *Consequence:*
- T22 will add the schema of the part and the keyword of a breach to `Check` and `CheckFile`, and the rules of the following tasks go into the same report;
- while there are no schemas, the check is passed by a manifest without a version or with a version that is not a string, and by a task file without a string `id` — until T22;
- the list `paths` of the release includes the files `module/*.go` without tests, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1. The binary does not carry the check while the CLI does not call it — until `check`, T37;
- in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md) — В22 «в реестр spec».

— RUN.md, T21; `module/`; `internal/vectors/vectors_test.go`, `internal/vectors/testdata/skipped.txt`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.6 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-04:* T22 added the check by the schemas (R23), and what is said above changes so:
- `Breach` has `Keyword` — the JSON Schema keyword that failed. Such a breach has an empty `Rule`, no line and no column, and its place is `Pointer`. Both are empty only for a file that is not YAML. `String` writes `schema <keyword>` in place of the rule;
- `module.file-ids` reads `id` only in a file that both the YAML rules and the schema of its part accepted: so `id` has the form `local_id` too. The question of "What is decided where the format text is silent" about a file that the schema rejected is decided: such a file breaks the schema and nothing more;
- `CheckFile` on a version outside the window gives this one breach, as `Check` does: otherwise a manifest of 0.4 would get breaches of the schema of 0.3;
- the order of breaches at one place of a file — the pointer by tokens, the number of an element as a number and before any name, then the rule, the keyword and the message;
- the forms `local_id` and `media_path` stay Go data even with the validator: the layout reads the names of files, not values, and before the schemas, and `TestFormsAreTheSchemas` holds them to `common.schema.json`.

— RUN.md, T22.

## Schemas

**R23. A file of a part is checked by JSON Schema 2020-12 with the library `github.com/santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema/v6` v6.0.3 — by the schemas of the version that the library reads: this is a copy of `schemas/` from `spec.Format`, embedded in `internal/schema` and checked byte for byte by a test. A breach of a schema names the keyword and the place of the value, as an example of the format names it, and a file that the schema rejected breaks it and nothing more: the rules of its part do not read it, and the rules between parts run only when all the files of parts have passed the schema. The examples `examples/` are passed, and the files of vectors the test reads by the schema and a type of `formatv0`.**

- **The validator** (read 2026-11-04; the sources — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4):
  - `github.com/santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema/v6` v6.0.3 — `@latest` at the Go proxy, the tag of 2026-06-28, Apache-2.0. R45 spec names it too. At Bowtie 2020-12 it passes 1300 tests of 1300 (v6.0.2). All the errors it gives as a tree with the place in the value, patterns it reads with Go's `regexp`, and numbers it takes of any Go type, through `big.Rat`, so the value of the YAML reader (R21) goes as it is. Annotations it does not collect;
  - it requires `golang.org/x/text` (BSD-3-Clause) — the messages. Its v0.14.0 from the library's `go.mod` govulncheck marked with two findings, GO-2026-6629 and GO-2026-5970, though the code does not reach them, and x/text is raised to v0.43.0, the latest release. `dlclark/regexp2` is only the library's tests and does not enter the build;
  - kaptinlin/jsonschema v0.9.10 is MIT, but Bowtie does not check it, and it has eight direct dependencies. google/jsonschema-go v0.4.3 stops at the first error and gives no place. Corvus Go v0.1.4 collects annotations, but reads patterns by ECMA-262 rather than as Go's `regexp`, takes input only as JSON text, and its first release came out on 2026-10-08. xeipuuv/gojsonschema and qri-io/jsonschema are not 2020-12.
- **Schemas** — `internal/schema/schemas/*.schema.json`, `//go:embed`: a copy of `schemas/` from `spec.Format` at the tag of `go.mod`, taken from the Go module cache, not from the submodule (R21 spec). `TestSchemasAreSpecs` checks the set of files and the bytes against `spec.Format`, and the `$id` of each against `VERSION`. The validator gets each schema by its `$id`, references between schemas resolve by it, and the default loader reads only `file:` addresses, so it does not go to the network.
- **API** (`internal/schema`) — `Validate(location string, value any) []Error`. `location` is the file name of a schema, `task.schema.json`, or that name with a pointer to a subschema in the fragment, `task.schema.json#/$defs/check`: so T23 will ask whether a subschema passes, T31 will check an instance, and T33 the canonical view. `Error{Keyword, Location []string, Message}`, and `Pointer` writes the place. A schema is compiled once and kept by its place, and `Validate` is safe from several goroutines: the compiled schema it reads without a lock, and the lock is held only by the compiler. An unknown place, a schema that does not compile, `false` anywhere but in `unevaluatedProperties`, and a value not from JSON are a defect of `module-kit`, and `Validate` panics, as `regexp.MustCompile` does: the error of `module.Check` stays only with reading (R22). `Keywords` is the list that the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 5, gives; a test holds it.
- **Which keywords are named** — by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 8, from the validator's tree:
  - `$ref`, `allOf`, `properties`, `items` and `if`/`then`/`else` are never named: the errors of `properties`, `items`, `then` and `else` the validator puts right at their place, and those of `$ref` and `allOf` under a node of their own, through which the collection descends, as it does through a group of several errors of one place and through the root;
  - every other keyword is named where it failed, and what failed inside it is not: the branches of `anyOf` and `oneOf`, the subschema of `not`, the elements that `contains` tried, and the names that `propertyNames` read. So the format's example names `anyOf` and `oneOf`, "when none of their branches passes";
  - `unevaluatedProperties` the format holds only as `false`, and `false` stands nowhere else ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 5). The validator puts a false schema on the field that it rejects, and the keyword is named at the object, as the example names it. It is recognized by the end of the place of the false schema, `/unevaluatedProperties`: a name of a field or of a definition of the format does not end so, because it is in snake_case;
  - a subschema that failed gives no annotations (JSON Schema 2020-12 core, §7.7.1.2), and the fields that its `$ref` declares become "unevaluated" beside it. The report holds these `unevaluatedProperties` too, as ajv does in `format-check`: the example allows it — "reports one with this keyword at this place". How the report of the CLI shows them T37 decides.
- **The workaround of a bug of the validator.** v6.0.3 gives an error of `propertyNames` not a copy of the place but a live slice of the validator, and the check of the next neighbour overwrites it: the error names another place, and which one depends on the order of the walk of a Go map (santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema#276, open; pull request #278 closed without a merge, #269 open; read 2026-11-04). At v0.9.2, `examples/task/invalid/parameter-named-if.yaml` went wrong this way: `propertyNames at #/items/0` instead of `#/template/parameters`. A vocabulary of its own, `propertyNames` (`names.go`), checks the names by the same subschema at the same place and adds an error through `ValidatorContext.AddError`, which copies the place. The error of the validator itself the collection passes over: both fail on the same names, and the value passes or not, as before. A fixed release is a reason to remove the vocabulary.
- **What v6.0.3 does not touch.** The panic on a number that `big.Rat` will not read — `fmt.Sprintf("%v", v)` with a huge exponent — is fixed after v6.0.3 (commit 9ae86900d). Only a `json.Number` of such a text gives it, and the YAML reader gives `int64` and a finite `float64`, which `big.Rat` reads; `FuzzValidate` holds this.
- **The place** — a JSON Pointer in a URI fragment (RFC 6901, §6): `#`, then `/` and a token, where `~` is written `~0` and `/` is written `~1`, and percent-encoding of what a fragment does not hold: `#/modules/example~1smallest/0.1.0/withdrawn`. The header of an example and the place of a report are compared by the pointer that they encode: `format-check` decodes the header too, and a fragment can write one place in different ways.
- **The module check** (`module`). A file of a part is checked against the schema of its part after the YAML rules, and the manifest after the version. A breach of a schema is a `Breach` with `Keyword` and `Pointer` (the amendment to R22). A file that both the YAML rules and the schema accepted the check holds by its path, and "all passed" it counts by the listing and these files, without storing it: `module.file-ids` reads only such a file, and the rules of parts and between parts of the tasks T24, T25, T29 and T31 will take from there, — a rule between parts runs only when all passed. So does `format-check` of `spec`: "so that a file the schema refuses breaks that and nothing more" (`tools/format-check/cases.mjs` at v0.9.2).
- **The vector test** (`internal/vectors`):
  - the runners `examples/valid`, `examples/invalid` and `examples/module`. A file of a part of the layout is checked by `module.CheckFile`, and `inventory` and `module-index` — they are no parts of a module — by the YAML reader and the root schema. A valid example gives not one breach. An invalid one breaks only the schema, and one of the breaches is the keyword at the place that its first line names. An example of a whole module `module.Check` does not reject. All 170 examples left the skip list — 121 invalid, 41 valid of nine parts and 8 whole modules —; it has 355;
  - `readVector` reads the file of a vector as the format says: the YAML rules, the schema of the vector, then strictly, without unknown fields, a type of `formatv0` (R72 spec). `TestVectorFiles` reads so the files of `verdicts/`, `leaks/`, `random/`, `templates/`, `tiers/`, `package.yaml`, `index.yaml` and `version.yaml`, and `part.yaml` of the open view — by its schema and the type of the part. The packages of a previous format version in `versions/` are read only by the places ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2), and `open-view.yaml` by the type of `open`, T23. The runners of these kinds will take `readVector` with their tasks.
- **Tests:** `TestSchemasAreSpecs`, `TestKeywordsAreTheFormats`, `TestEverySchemaCompiles`, `TestFaults`, `TestCollect` — on small schemas of its own, each collection and `propertyNames` before a neighbour, as in #276 —, `TestPointer`, `TestConcurrentValidate` under `-race` and `FuzzValidate`: any text through the YAML reader against each schema, the keyword from the list that the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 5, gives, the place within the value, the corpus the examples. In `module` — fixtures from the examples of a whole module of `spec.Format`, valid by the schemas and by the rules between parts, `TestPartsThatPassed`, `TestOrderOfPointers`, `TestChecksTheFormat` by the pair rule — keyword, and `FuzzCheck` with the examples of parts in the corpus.

— *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-11-04 when planning T22 (RUN.md, T22, «Решено автором»), all by the recommendation of the plan:
- the library: a validator of the subset that the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 5, allows is about a thousand lines of our own code, with `unevaluatedProperties`, and the JSON-Schema-Test-Suite in `testdata`, and the review of T22 would grow by two checks. santhosh passes 2020-12 at Bowtie in full, and the probe on all 121 invalid examples of v0.9.2 it passed with one workaround. The open view needs no annotations: `format-check` builds it by its own walk of the schemas by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 6, and asks ajv only whether a subschema passes — T23 will do the same;
- the copy of the schemas: the code holds the schemas of the version that it executes, as R22 holds the window of versions as a constant. A move to a tag with new schemas turns red on a test, rather than silently starting to check by them. And `spec.Format` at run time would carry into every binary with `module` — `api`, the eval runner of `tutor`, six binaries of the CLI — all of `format/` and `admission/`, 5.75 MB at v0.9.2, and more with each vector;
- a file that the schema rejected: `format-check` does so, and the reports of the two implementations on one module agree, and `formatv0` says to check a value against the schema before reading it with a type. A rule between parts on a file that the schema did not accept would give false breaches: without a trap catalog, each trap of a task is "unknown".

— *Rejected:*
- a validator of the subset of our own — above;
- kaptinlin/jsonschema, google/jsonschema-go, Corvus Go, xeipuuv/gojsonschema and qri-io/jsonschema — by the facts above;
- `spec.Format` at run time — above. The first question to the author named ≈20 MB — the space on disk by `du`; the author confirmed the choice on the right number;
- `module.*` on any file that YAML read, and the rules of parts on those that passed the schema: the report would diverge from `format-check`, and the boundary would fall between the rules `module.*` and the rules of parts;
- a rule between parts when the files that it reads itself have passed the schema: the author would see more per round, but the report would diverge from `format-check`, and each task would hold a list of the rule's inputs and a test of it;
- naming also the errors inside the branches of `anyOf` and `oneOf`: no example names them, and the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 8, names here `anyOf` and `oneOf` themselves, and at a branch of a common name, a `oneOf` with four `const`, the report would get five lines instead of one;
- cleaning out the "unevaluated" fields beside a subschema that failed: so 2020-12 says, so ajv gives, and the form of the report of the CLI T37 decides;
- an error of `Validate` instead of a panic — `module.Check` would have to return it as code 2, though it is a defect of `module-kit`, not of input and output (R22);
- waiting for a release of santhosh with the fix of #276 — its date is unknown, and the vocabulary closes the bug on v6.0.3;
- a textual order of pointers — `#/items/10` would come before `#/items/2`, and a mixed one, where numbers are compared as numbers and names as text, is not transitive: `2 < 10`, `10 < 1a`, `1a < 2`.

— *Consequence:*
- `github.com/santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema/v6` and `golang.org/x/text` are in `go.mod`, and Dependabot `gomod` raises them. A pull request with a new santhosh passes the vector test, `TestCollect` and `FuzzValidate`; with the fix of #276 it is a reason to remove the vocabulary `propertyNames`;
- the release binary does not carry them yet: the CLI does not call the check until `check`, T37. With it, the `LICENSE` of santhosh and of x/text must go with the release — Д15 in table 4 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md);
- the list `paths` of the release holds `internal/schema/schemas/*.schema.json`, and with them the files `internal/schema/*.go` and `module/*.go` without tests, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1;
- a move to a new tag of `spec` updates the copy of the schemas (the amendment to R20);
- in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md) — В23 «в реестр spec»: a Go package of `spec` that embeds the schemas alone;
- a breach of a schema has no line and column, only a pointer. The place in the file by the pointer the YAML reader will give, if the form of the report of `check`, T37, asks for it.

— RUN.md, T22; `go.mod`, `go.sum`; `internal/schema/`; `module/`; `internal/vectors/`; `.github/workflows/release.yml`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.6 and 4.

## The open view

**R24. The open view is built by a walk of the schemas by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 6, — `schema.Open` in `internal/schema`: the mark `x-open` counts only in a subschema that applies to the place and passes, and whether a branch of `anyOf`, `oneOf` or `if` passes the walk asks of the validator. The package `openview` gives the open view of each part of the layout — `Manifest`, `SkillGraph`, `Traps`, `Techniques`, `SolvingSteps`, `Lesson` and `Task`: it takes the part as a type of `formatv0`, and gives the view as a type of `formatv0/open` (R72 spec). The vectors `open-view/` are passed.**

- **The walk** (`internal/schema/open.go`) — as `openViewOf` in `tools/format-check/openview.mjs` of `spec` at v0.9.2:
  - at a place there apply its subschema and what that subschema brings there: the subschema of its `$ref` beside the other keywords, each branch of `allOf`, the branches of `anyOf` and `oneOf` that pass, `then` when `if` passes, and `else` when it does not, and the branches of `dependentSchemas` whose key the object has. The subschemas of a field are under `properties`, under each key of `patternProperties` that matches it, and under `additionalProperties` where none of them matched in the same subschema; the subschema of an element of an array is under `prefixItems` at its number, or else under `items`;
  - a field is open when at least one of its applied subschemas carries the mark, an element of an open array is open, and the root is always open. An open object without open fields stays an empty object;
  - whether a branch passes is decided by `Validate` of the same subschema by a pointer, compiled once: `task.schema.json#/$defs/answer_slot/allOf/2/if`. santhosh does not collect annotations (R23), and the walk replaces them, as `format-check` replaces them for ajv. A subschema that the walk reached by two paths — two references to one definition, or references in a circle — is taken once: there is no repeated work, and a circle does not loop the walk;
  - a boolean `if` the walk reads as 2020-12 does: `true` always passes, and `then` applies. `openViewOf` takes `if` only when it is an object, but the schemas of 0.3 have no boolean `if`, and the implementations have nothing to diverge on yet;
  - `patternProperties` and `prefixItems` are not in the schemas of 0.3, and `dependentSchemas` is not even among the keywords of the format ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 5). Section 6 names all three, and the walk holds them, and a test checks them on schemas of its own;
  - `Open(location string, value any) any`, `location` as with `Validate`. The value does not change, and the view is new maps and slices. The format defines the view only of a value that the schema accepts; for another, `Open` gives what the same walk gives. A place or a reference that names nothing is a panic, as with `Validate`.
- **API** (`openview`). The part is a value of a type of `formatv0`, as the consumer holds it after reading the package. The function writes its JSON and reads it into a value of the YAML reader — with `int64` and `float64` —, checks it against the schema of the part and, if the schema does not accept it, gives an error with all the breaches. Then comes the walk, and the view is read strictly into the type of `open`: a field that the type does not know is a panic, a defect, because the type is given by the same marks (R72 spec). Together with the check against the schema, the open view of the task `zones-and-picture` of the vector of the open view costs 0.30 ms, and that of the task `gaps-ladder-1` of the example `with-lessons` 0.13 ms (`go test -bench`, AMD Ryzen AI 9 365, 2026-11-04). The open view of an instance of a template and the digest of the open view come with T31 and T33.
- **The vector test.** The runner `open-view` reads `part.yaml` by the YAML rules, the schema of the part and strictly a type of `formatv0` (`readVector`), and `open-view.yaml` by the YAML rules and strictly the type of `open` (`readOpenView`); both give a JSON value too. There are two comparisons, and the order of keys matters in neither. The view that the walk gives is compared with `open-view.yaml` as a JSON value, as in `format-check`: so a field that is absent differs from a field with a zero. The view that the function of the part gives is compared with the value of the type of `open`: so the library gives the view that the vector holds, and in its type. 14 lines of `open-view/` left the skip list; it has 341.
- **Tests:** `TestOpen` — on schemas of its own, a case for each rule of the open view, for `true` in place of a subschema, for a circle of references and for one definition by two paths; `TestOpenAtSubschema`, `TestOpenFaults`, `TestTokensOf` and `TestConcurrentOpen` under `-race`; `FuzzOpen` — any text through the YAML reader and each schema of the format: the view is built without a panic and lies within the value. In `openview` — each valid example of each part, an invalid part, a type that does not hold an open field, and `jsonValue`.

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T23 — «—»):
- the walk is in `internal/schema`: it reads the schemas as they are written and asks the validator about subschemas, and both are held by this package (R20, "JSON Schema 2020-12 and the marks `x-open`"). `openview` is a layer of types for consumers;
- types at the input and the output: `api`, the eval runner of `tutor` and the CLI hold the parts as types of `formatv0` — the packages `types/formatv0` and `types/formatv0/open` are named by the card 3.7 spec —, and the type of `open` will not read a closed field (R72 spec);
- the check against the schema at the input: a type of `formatv0` holds neither `required`, nor `pattern`, nor conditions between fields, and the branches of the walk are chosen by the validator. The format does not define the view of an invalid part, and the function does not invent it;
- a panic on a defect — as with `Validate` (R23).
- a branch of `anyOf`, `oneOf` or `if` the walk checks against the validator anew at each place, though the whole part has already been checked: the review of T23 proposed caching the answers or checking a branch only by the keywords that choose it. This is not done: 0.13–0.30 ms per task is not the price for which the walk would start a second parsing of the schemas, and a cache by value is a key for each place;

— *Rejected:*
- the open view by a list of fields of its own: it is held by the marks in the schemas (R05 spec; R45 spec), and T23 forbids this;
- at the input, a JSON value of the YAML reader: the consumer holds the parts as types, and the value of the reader is the library's internal matter;
- at the output, a JSON value: each consumer would have to read it into a type itself;
- without the check against the schema: a value outside the schema would silently get a view that the format does not define;
- the walk in `openview`, and from `internal/schema` the schemas as JSON: the parsing of references and pointers would be split between two packages.

— *Consequence:*
- T31 builds the open view of an instance with the same `openview.Task`, and `solve` gets the numbers of the fields of the open view of the template ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 24); T33, the digest of the open view, by the canonical text of the value of `open`;
- the list `paths` of the release holds the files `internal/schema/*.go` and `openview/*.go` without tests, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1. The binary does not carry the open view while the command `open`, T40, does not call it.

— RUN.md, T23; `internal/schema/`; `openview/`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

## The rules of parts

**R25. The module check holds the rules of the manifest, of the skill graph and of the catalogs — `manifest.dependencies`, `manifest.bands`, `manifest.rung-templates`, `skill-graph.acyclic`, `skill-graph.known-skills`, `skill-graph.retired-ids`, `skill-graph.own-anchors`, `traps.known-skills`, `task.known-traps` and `task.known-techniques` — and computes the tiers of skills, `module.Tiers`. A rule reads a part as a type of `formatv0` and names the place of the value that breaks it. A rule within one part runs on each file that passed the schema — both in `Check` and in `CheckFile` —, and a rule between parts only when all the files of parts have passed the schema (R23). The vectors of these rules and of `tiers/` are passed.**

- **Within a part and between parts.** Within one part — `manifest.bands` and four rules of the skill graph: their vectors lie as files, and `CheckFile` passes them, as an example of a part ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 8). The other five read two parts or more — the manifest and the graph, the manifest and the tasks, a catalog and the graph, a task and a catalog — and run as the rules between parts of R23. A part that the module does not have the rule does not find, so a trap of a task in a module without a catalog is unknown, as in the vector `task.known-traps/no-trap-catalog`.
- **Types.** A value that the schema accepted is read strictly into the type of the part of `formatv0` — `schema.Decode`, shared with `openview` —, once per file, and the rules within a part and between parts take the same values: so `formatv0` says to read a value from outside (R72 spec), and a rule is written by fields, not by maps. A value that the schema accepted and the type does not read is a panic, a defect of `module-kit`.
- **What is decided where the format text is silent:**
  - the place of a breach is the pointer of the value that breaks the rule: a repeated ID of a band is `#/bands/1/id`, a grade of the audience outside the bands or in two of them is `#/audience/grades/1`, a band without grades is `#/bands/1`, a grade of a band that is not in the audience is `#/bands/0/grades/1`; an unknown prerequisite and an anchor that the module itself teaches are `#/skills/<ID>/prerequisites/<number>`, and a skill on a cycle is `#/skills/<ID>/prerequisites`; a retired ID that a skill also bears is `#/retired_skills/<ID>`; an extra anchor of the manifest is `#/dependencies/<number>`, and a missing one is `#/dependencies`, or `#` when there is no field. For `manifest.rung-templates` the place is `#` of the manifest, and the message names the first task by path with an item with a verdict;
  - a cycle is each skill that stands on it: a member of a strongly connected component of two skills or more, or a skill that names itself. The components are found by Tarjan's algorithm, in time linear in the size of the graph. The message names the other skills of the cycle, and a skill that also names itself is told this too, in the same breach. A skill that only rests on a cycle gets no breach, as in the vector `three-skills.yaml`;
  - an item with a verdict is an item at least one answer slot or box of a line of which is checked by a kind with a verdict: `number`, `fraction`, `choice` or `predicate` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15). So `format-check` reads it too — `hasVerdict` in `tools/format-check/leaks.mjs` at v0.9.2;
  - an anchor is a prerequisite of the form `core.<name>`: a local ID has no dot ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 13), and an anchor that is not in the shared taxonomy the schema rejects.
- **Tiers** — `Tiers(formatv0.SkillGraph) (formatv0.SkillTiers, error)`, by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 12: a skill without prerequisites among the module's skills is tier 1, the others are one tier above their highest prerequisite, and an anchor gives no tier. A graph whose prerequisite names no skill of the module, and a graph with a cycle, have no tiers — this is an error, as `problem` of `tiersOf` of `format-check` is. The tiers are needed by the package, T33; the module check does not compute them: there is no rule about them.
- **The vector test.** The runner `tiers` reads a vector by the schema and the type `formatv0.TierVector` and compares the `Tiers` of the graph with `skill_tiers`. 31 lines left the skip list — 26 vectors of rules and 5 vectors of tiers —; it has 310.
- **Tests** (`module`): `TestPartRules` — each rule on a whole module, at each place of a breach, and that the rules between parts wait until all the files pass, and the rules within a part do not; `TestPartRulesOfAFile` — `CheckFile` runs the rules of its part and not one between parts; `TestPartRuleMessages`, `TestSelfAndCycle`, `TestHasVerdict`, `TestTiers`, `TestCycles` and `TestTypedFault`, and in `internal/schema` `TestDecode`. `TestRulesAreTheFormats` holds each rule in the tables of the format text, and `TestChecksTheFormat` that neither an example nor a vector of another rule breaks the new rules. In the corpus of `FuzzCheck` are the vectors of all the rules that the check holds, except `yaml.*`: `FuzzRead` takes them.

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T24 — «—»):
- a rule within a part also in `CheckFile`: an example of a part "meets every format rule that can be checked within its part, and `module-kit`'s CI runs it too" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 8), and the vectors of `manifest.bands` and of the rules of the graph lie as files;
- the rules between parts — by R23: so the report agrees with `format-check`;
- types: a rule by fields is checked by the compiler, and `formatv0` is given by the same schemas (R72 spec);
- the place is the value, not the file: the author fixes the value, and the line of the file by the pointer the report of the CLI will take from the YAML reader, if its form, T37, asks for it (R23).

— *Rejected:*
- rules on a JSON value, by maps `map[string]any`: dozens of conversions whose errors the compiler does not see;
- one breach `skill-graph.acyclic` per cycle: the author who fixes one skill would not see which other skills the cycle runs through;
- tiers in the report of the check: there is no rule about tiers, and the package build computes them ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 12).

— *Consequence:*
- T25 puts the rules of a task and of a lesson into the same `withinPart` and `betweenParts`, and T33 takes `Tiers` into the canonical view;
- the list `paths` of the release holds the files `module/*.go` without tests, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1.

— RUN.md, T24; `module/`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

## The rules of a task and of a lesson

**R26. The module check holds the rules of a task and of a lesson that need neither a check kind, nor the leak check, nor templates: `task.known-skills`, `task.skill-roles`, `task.own-anchors`, `task.known-band`, `task.known-pictures`, `task.age-categories`, `task.zones` and `task.assessability`, and for a lesson `lesson.known-skills`, `lesson.known-tasks`, `lesson.task-skills`, `lesson.known-pictures`, `lesson.areas`, `lesson.age-categories` and `lesson.order`. They run as the rules of R25 do: within one part — `task.skill-roles`, `task.zones` and `lesson.areas`, on each file that passed and in `CheckFile` —, and the others between parts, when all the files of parts have passed the schema. The vectors of these rules are passed.**

- **Within a part and between parts.** The boundary is the same as in R25: a rule within a part reads only its own file. `task.known-pictures` and `lesson.known-pictures` read one part, but also the listing of the module — the files of `media/` —, and `CheckFile` has no listing. This is a rule that is checked only on a whole module ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 8), and it runs between parts.
- **What is decided where the format text is silent:**
  - the skills of an item are its own, or else the task's ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14). `task.skill-roles` checks the skills of the task only when at least one item takes them: the rule speaks of the skills of an item — "of the task, for an item that names no skills of its own". `task.known-skills` and `task.own-anchors` check each place where the task names skills;
  - `task.assessability`: an item with a rubric requires `rubric` of its primary skills, and the other items `verdict`. A rubric stands only on an answer slot `sheet`, and such an answer slot is the only one in the task, so the kinds do not mix within one item. A skill that is not in the graph the rule skips — this is `task.known-skills` —, and a skill of the task that breaks the rule in several items is named once;
  - zones overlap when their intersection has an area, and touching at an edge is no overlap, as the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14, says. The breach stands at the zone that comes later by items and answer slots, and names the earlier one;
  - `lesson.age-categories` gives two breaches: a category outside the audience — at the category itself, `#/age_categories/<number>`, and a lesson that, together with its tasks, no category fits — at `#` of the lesson. A task that the module does not have takes no part in this intersection: it breaks `lesson.known-tasks`;
  - `lesson.order`: lesson A comes after lesson B when a skill of A requires, through the prerequisites of the module's skills, a skill of B that is not among the skills of A ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21). A cycle in this order is found by the same search for strongly connected components as for `skill-graph.acyclic` (R25) — now generalized —, and the breach stands at each lesson on the cycle, `#/skills`. The order is derived from the skill graph, and a graph with a cycle of prerequisites gives no order: then the rule waits until the graph passes `skill-graph.acyclic`, as a rule between parts waits for a file that the schema rejected (R23) — otherwise one cycle of the graph would repeat as a breach at each lesson of its skills;
  - `lesson.known-tasks` and `lesson.task-skills` — at the segment, `#/segments/<number>/task`, and a picture at its own `picture`.
- **The vector test.** 28 lines of the vectors of these rules left the skip list; it has 282.
- **Tests** (`module`): `TestTaskRules` — each rule on a whole module, at each place of a breach, and lessons in order; `TestTaskRulesOfAFile` — the rules within a task and a lesson in `CheckFile`, where zones that touch at an edge do not overlap; `TestTaskRuleMessages` and `TestReviewedCases` — the boxes of lines with a verdict without rung templates, and a cycle of the graph under two lessons. Four cases of `TestPartRules` (R25), on which the rules of a task and of a lesson now fired too, are rebuilt so as to touch only their own rule.

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T25 — «—»):
- one boundary with R25 — the report agrees with `format-check`, and `CheckFile` checks an example of a part by what "can be checked within its part" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 8);
- `task.skill-roles` — by the letter of the rule: the skills of the task are skills of an item only when the item names none of its own;
- one breach for a skill of the task in `task.assessability`: the place is one, and the author fixes it once.

— *Rejected:*
- `task.known-pictures` and `lesson.known-pictures` within a part: `CheckFile` has no files of the module, and an example of a part would be rejected for a picture that only a whole module holds;
- checking by `task.skill-roles` the skills of the task when all the items name their own: such skills belong to no item;
- one breach `lesson.order` per cycle — for the same reasons as for `skill-graph.acyclic` (R25).
- `lesson.order` on a graph with a cycle too: lessons in which there is no error would get a breach that goes away together with the cycle of the graph;

— *Consequence:*
- the rules of a task that the slot check shows by itself — T26, the predicate — T27, the leak — T29, templates — T31; their vectors stay in the skip list;
- the list `paths` of the release holds the files `module/*.go` without tests, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1.

— RUN.md, T25; `module/`; `internal/vectors/testdata/skipped.txt`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

**R27. The verdict of an answer slot and of an item is given by the package `verdict`: the kinds `number`, `fraction` and `choice` by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15, — normalization, the notation of numbers, exact comparison and the trap of a wrong value —, and a rubric has no verdict. NFKC is `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm` v0.43.0, and the version of Unicode, 17.0.0, the release names by the constant `verdict.UnicodeVersion` and by the second line of `module-kit --version`. The module check holds the rules `task.choice-key`, `task.choice-options`, `task.fraction-key` and `task.trap-values` within a part, by the verdicts of the same check. The vectors `verdicts/` of three kinds and the vectors of the four rules are passed, except the case with a template.**

- **API** (`verdict`):
  - `Verdict` — `correct`, `not_yet`, `unreadable` and `unchecked`, and `Reason` — `blank` and `notation`: types of its own with the words of the format. The reasons of an observation T28 will add to `Reason`;
  - `AnswerSlot` — the binding, as the format writes it, the check, and the options of an answer slot bound `choice`. `AnswerSlots(item)` gives the answer slots of an item in order, and `ChoiceOptions` the options of a choice: the answer slot's for the binding `choice`, otherwise the check's own;
  - `OfItem(ctx, item, values)` and `OfSlot(ctx, slot, value)` — the verdict of an item and of an answer slot by the values as they are written, and `nil` is nothing read. `SlotVerdict` holds the reason for `unreadable`, and for `not_yet` a copy of the trap value that matched, with the ID of the trap and the text for the learner. `ItemVerdict` is the verdict of an item and of its answer slots;
  - `Same(slot, a, b)` — whether two values are equal as the check compares them: for a choice the normal forms, an option or not, for a number and a fraction the values in the notation of the kind, for a predicate the integers;
  - an error gives no verdict: `context` is cancelled; an item has no answer slots, or the values are not one per answer slot; a value is not in UTF-8; a check that the format does not accept — a kind not among the five, no key, a key not in the notation of the kind, a choice without options, and a placeholder of a template in the key or in a trap value of `number` and `fraction` is `ErrPlaceholder`; a rubric is `ErrNoVerdict`; a predicate on an integer in notation — until the sandbox, T27. A check that breaks one of the four rules does give a verdict: by it the rules read the check.
- **Normalization** — steps 1–7 and the form of a choice, and at the edge cases as `verdicts.mjs` of `spec` does:
  - at step 1 the neighbours of a vulgar fraction are characters of the original string, so `½½` and `²½` are both replaced; superscript and subscript characters are the whole block U+2070–U+209F;
  - steps 3 and 4 come after NFKC and catch what it gives: U+2011 becomes U+2010, then `-`;
  - only spaces are trimmed, and between steps 5 and 6 there is no trimming: `= rungs` and `7 rungs .` with the unit `rungs` are not in notation;
  - a unit is compared by the lowercase letter of each character, and its length is counted in characters: `İ` is two bytes, `i` one. The lowercase letter is `unicode.ToLower`, the simple mapping of UnicodeData, field 13;
  - the key is read as written, and the read value and the trap value after normalization, for a number with the units.
- **Numbers** — the notation by the regular expressions of the format text, no longer than 16 characters. The value is a `big.Rat`, as in R56 spec, and irreducibility is the GCD of the numerator and the denominator as written: `big.Rat` reduces them.
- **Rules** (`module`, `checks.go`) — within a part, on each task file that passed and in `CheckFile`: they need only the file itself.
  - `task.choice-key` — a key not among the options as written;
  - `task.choice-options` — an option that is `Same` with an earlier one;
  - `task.fraction-key` — a key to which its own check with `form: reduced` does not give `correct`: the key is in notation, and this is exactly "reducible";
  - `task.trap-values` — a trap value that is not `not_yet`, for a choice not an option as written, or a value that is `Same` with an earlier one.

  A task with `template`, and a check to which `OfSlot` answers `ErrPlaceholder` — `{` in the key or in a trap value of `number` or `fraction`, as with `holdsPlaceholder` of `format-check` —, are not read: a template is checked by its instances, and a placeholder outside a template is the rule `task.placeholders`, T31. So a placeholder is recognized in one place. The function by answer slot, `slotRules`, gives breaches rather than putting them into the report: T31 will take it on instances. An error of `OfSlot` on a check that the schema accepted is a defect of `module-kit`, a panic, as with `typed` (R25).
- **The version of Unicode.** `verdict.UnicodeVersion` is `norm.Version`, the version of the NFKC tables with which the library is built, and a test holds it equal to `unicode.Version` and to 17.0.0. `module-kit --version` writes `Unicode 17.0.0` as its second line — `unicode.Version` of the standard library —, and the test of `cmd` expects `verdict.UnicodeVersion` in the output. So the code of `x/text` does not enter the binary while its `LICENSE` does not go with the release (Д15 in table 4 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md)). With T37 the CLI imports `verdict` through `module`, and the line will take the constant.
- **Where it diverges from the second implementation of `spec`** — on a run of more than 30 non-starters: `norm.NFKC` goes together with the Stream-Safe Text Process ([UAX #15](https://www.unicode.org/reports/tr15/), section 13) and inserts U+034F, and ICU in `verdicts.mjs` does not. There is no vector with such a run, and verdicts are hardly touched by it: such a value is not in the notation of a number and is not equal to an option of a choice. This is row В24 of table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md), «в реестр spec».
- **The vector test.** The runner `verdicts` reads a vector by the schema and a type of `formatv0` and checks the verdict, the reason and the trap of each answer slot, and the verdict of the item. The case with `predicate` is "no code yet", until T27. 22 lines left the skip list — 13 of `verdicts/` and 9 vectors of rules —; it has 260. `rules/task.trap-values/trap-value-alike-the-key-on-an-instance.yaml` is a template; T31 passes it.
- **Tests:**
  - `verdict` — `TestOfSlot`, 89 cases: the steps, notation, units, fractions, traps, choice and the predicate before the sandbox. Also `TestOfSlotErrors`, `TestTrapIsTheTrapValue`, `TestAnswerSlots`, `TestOfItem`, `TestItemOf`, `TestSame`, `TestUnicodeVersion`, `TestSteps`, `TestNFKCIsStreamSafe`, `FuzzOfSlot` and `FuzzNotation`;
  - `module` — `TestCheckRules`, `TestCheckRulesOfAModule`, `TestCheckRuleMessages` and `TestVerdictOfFault`;
  - `cmd/module-kit` — the output of `--version`: its second line the test checks against `verdict.UnicodeVersion`.

— *Why* (the author's decisions of 2026-11-04 when planning T26, all by the recommendation of the plan, — RUN.md, T26, «Решено автором»; the rest are the executor's decisions):
- `x/text/unicode/norm` is a direct dependency since T22 (R23), BSD-3-Clause, and with `go1.27` its tables are Unicode 17.0.0, as is `unicode` of Go 1.27.3. NFKC, White_Space, the category N and the lowercase letter are of one version. NFKC tables of our own are thousands of lines of code and data;
- the version is named by the reader's release ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15), and the description of the release the shared `release.yml` assembles itself. The constant is for `api` and the eval runner of `tutor`, which keep the version of `module-kit` with an attempt and with a report, and `--version` is for the module author and CI of `modules`;
- types of its own: `formatv0.SlotReadingReason` has no reasons of an observation, which T28 needs, and a trap by its ID alone would not give `api` a text after a wrong value. `context` and `error` at once, by the agreement of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.4: a cancellation is an error without a verdict, and T27 does not change signatures;
- normalization inside `verdict`: a shared internal package is started by the task that needs it a second time ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6; R20), and `module` takes only `OfSlot`, `Same` and `ChoiceOptions`;
- the rules within a part: they read only the task itself, as `task.zones` does (R26).

— *Rejected:*
- NFKC tables of our own from UCD 17.0.0;
- the version of Unicode only as a constant, or only in the documents;
- the types of `formatv0` in the API — `SlotReading` and `SlotReadingReason`; an API without `context` and `error` until T27;
- normalization at once in `internal/`: its second consumer, the leak, will come with T29;
- irreducibility by `big.Rat`: `SetString` stores `6/8` as 3/4;
- the rules on the template itself: the valid `examples/task/valid/fraction-template.yaml` with the trap `"{whole}/{part}"` would give a false `task.trap-values`.

— *What is not closed:*
- `unicode` and `norm` will diverge on a Go with a new version of Unicode, if `norm` has not taken it yet — `//go:build go1.27` is true on Go 1.28 too —, and at a consumer that raises x/text by MVS. `TestUnicodeVersion` sees this only on the toolchain of `module-kit`, and the NovaTrail repositories take Go from the container of `spec`;
- the Stream-Safe Text Process against ICU — В24;
- `7 rungs .` with the unit `rungs` is not in notation, and `7 .` is right: the letter of steps 5 and 6, as with `verdicts.mjs` — В25;
- moving normalization and rational numbers out into `internal/` — T29.

— *Consequence:*
- the predicate — T27: `OfSlot` on an integer in notation waits for the sandbox. The join of two readings and the verdict by an observation — T28, in the same package, with `itemOf` and `Same`; the leak — T29; the rules on the instances of templates — T31, with the same `slotRules`;
- the list `paths` of the release holds the files `verdict/*.go`, `module/*.go` and `cmd/module-kit/main.go` without tests, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1.

— RUN.md, T26; `verdict/`; `module/checks.go`; `cmd/module-kit/main.go`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.6 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-05:* the CLI checks modules, and the binary carries `golang.org/x/text`, and with it its `LICENSE` in the release (R38). So the second line of `--version` now names `verdict.UnicodeVersion`, not `unicode.Version`. Both are still equal: the tests of `verdict` hold this. — RUN.md, T37.

## The check predicate

**R28. The Starlark of the format is executed by the package `internal/sandbox` on go.starlark.net `v0.0.0-20261021141710-4f4267fb140d`, pinned in `go.mod` and excluded from Dependabot `gomod`: the dialect of the format, its 33 built-in names by a list of its own, the limit of steps "the count reaches", the clock, the cancellation of a `context` and `recover`. The verdict of a predicate is given by `verdict` in the sandbox: `True` is `correct`, `False` is `not_yet`; an error, a result that is not a `bool`, the limit of steps, the clock and a panic are `unchecked`; a cancellation of the `context` is an error without a verdict. The module check holds `task.predicate-expression` within a part. The vectors `verdicts/predicate/` and `rules/task.predicate-expression/` are passed.**

- **The sandbox** (`internal/sandbox`):
  - `CheckExpr(src, names...)` — an expression of the dialect that names only `names`, the names that it binds itself and the built-in names of the dialect. The parsing is `syntax.FileOptions.ParseExpr` with the options of the dialect, the names are `resolve.ExprOptions` with its own list of 33 names, not with `starlark.Universe`. An error is the line and the column where go.starlark.net finds them, and its words: `1:8: got '=' after expression, want EOF`, `1:1: undefined: number`;
  - `Eval(ctx, limits, src, env)` — an expression on its own thread: first `CheckExpr` on the names of `env`, then the parsing again and `starlark.EvalExprOptions`. The parsing is done twice because the resolver writes into the expression, and `EvalExprOptions` resolves it once more; ≤ 1000 characters of a predicate are parsed in microseconds;
  - `Limits{Steps, Clock}`, zero being no limit; `Predicate` — 1,000,000 steps of the format and 1 s of the clock of `module-kit` (R11). The limits of a solver T32 will add;
  - the thread — `SetMaxExecutionSteps` and `OnMaxSteps`, the clock — `time.AfterFunc`, the cancellation — `context.AfterFunc`. Each calls `Thread.Cancel` with its own reason, and the error names the first: `ErrSteps`, `ErrClock` or the error of the `context`, wrapped. A panic of the interpreter or of a built-in function — `recover` and `ErrPanic`. `print` writes nothing: without a `Thread.Print` of its own, go.starlark.net writes to stderr. There is no `Thread.Load`: `load` cannot stand in an expression, and the format forbids it to a solver (T32);
  - a `context` done before the evaluation is an error at once, without a thread. An evaluation that ended before the interpreter saw the cancellation returns its result or its error, even if the clock or the `context` fired while it was ending: the reason is taken only by an error in which the interpreter named it a cancellation, "Starlark computation cancelled: <reason>" (`interp.go`, `checkStepsSlow`). So `12 % value == 0` on `0` stays `unchecked`, and not an error of a `context` that was cancelled at the same moment. This is a fix by the review: at first the reason replaced any error.
- **The verdict of a predicate** (`verdict`):
  - `OfSlot` and `OfItem` pass the `context` to the evaluation. Empty and not in notation — before the sandbox, as in T26. An integer in notation goes into `value` as a number, `starlark.MakeBigInt`, exactly: `value == 9007199254740993` on `9007199254740993` is `correct`;
  - `True` is `correct`, `False` is `not_yet`, and an error, a result that is not a `bool` — even a truthy one, `[value]` —, the limit, the clock and a panic are `unchecked`, without a reason: the `unchecked` of a predicate has none, neither in the format nor in `SlotVerdict`. A cancellation of the `context` is an error: `errors.Is` with `context.Canceled` or `context.DeadlineExceeded`;
  - a predicate that breaks `task.predicate-expression` gives `unchecked`: an error of an expression — "an error … give `unchecked`" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15), and a check that breaks a rule that it shows by itself does give a verdict (R27);
  - `CheckPredicate(expression)` — the rule `task.predicate-expression` in one place: only `verdict` knows the name `value`.
- **The rule** (`module`) — in `slotRules`, within a part, on each file that passed and in `CheckFile`, at `#/items/<number>/answer_slots/<number>/check/predicate`. A task with `template` the check skips, as the rules of T26 do: in a template the predicate is a string of the task, where `{name}` is a placeholder and `{{` is `{` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 23), and the rule reads the instance — T31, by the same `slotRules`.
- **Dependencies.** go.starlark.net — BSD-3-Clause, direct. It takes `golang.org/x/sys/unix`, BSD-3-Clause: MVS would take v0.42.0 from its `go.mod`, and govulncheck v1.9.0 marked GO-2026-5024 on it — `windows`, `NewNTUnicodeString`, fixed in v0.44.0 —, which the code does not reach. `x/sys` is raised to the latest, v0.49.0, as x/text was in R23, and govulncheck writes "No vulnerabilities found". `ci-licenses` sees go.starlark.net and `x/sys/unix` as BSD-3-Clause. The CLI's binary does not carry them yet — `go list -deps ./cmd/module-kit` —, and from T37 their `LICENSE` files go with the release (Д15 in table 4 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md)).
- **The address reserve.** The package `go.starlark.net/starlark` on 64-bit linux and darwin reserves 4 GiB of address space at the start of the process — `mmap` with `PROT_READ` — for small integers, and if that fails, it writes "Starlark failed to allocate 4GB address space" to `log` and computes integers more slowly (`int_posix64.go`). Every process that imports `verdict` inherits this — `api` and the eval runner of `tutor`: the reserve takes no memory, but a limit of the process's address space, `ulimit -v`, sees it.
- **Dependabot:** `go.starlark.net` is in `ignore`, beside the modules of `spec`: its version is part of the format (R88 spec).
- **The vector test.** The runner `verdicts` takes the predicate too. 6 lines left the skip list — 4 of `verdicts/predicate/` and 2 of `rules/task.predicate-expression/` —, and 254 are left in it; before they were struck out, the test named exactly them.
- **Tests:**
  - `internal/sandbox` — `TestDialect`: the options, the 33 names — the keys of `starlark.Universe` and the list of the format text, in order; `TestVersionIsTheFormats`: the version in `go.mod` is the one that the format text names; `TestCheckExpr`, `TestEval` — also that a name that the process added to `starlark.Universe` is not a name of the dialect; `TestSteps`: 999,990 steps and a stop at 1,000,000; `TestClockAndCancel`, `TestFirstCauseWins`, `TestPanic`, `TestPrintWritesNothing`, `TestConcurrentEval` and `FuzzCheckExpr`;
  - `verdict` — the cases of a predicate in `TestOfSlot`, `TestPredicateStops` — the limit, the clock and the `context`, through `OfItem` too —, and `TestCheckPredicate`; `FuzzOfSlot` now runs predicates too, and `unchecked`;
  - `module` — four cases of `TestCheckRules` and the messages of `TestCheckRuleMessages`.
- **The measurement** — 2026-11-04, Go 1.27.3, AMD Ryzen AI 9 365, 20 threads; a probe in the session's folder, which did not go into the repository:

  | Expression, `value` = 49 | Steps | Time | With `-race` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `value in [n * n for n in range(99997)]` | 999,990, the result `True` | 13–15 ms | not measured |
  | `value in [n * n for n in range(99998)]` | a stop at 1,000,000; without a limit — exactly 1,000,000 | 12–13 ms | not measured |
  | `[n for n in range(1 << 62) if False] == []` | a stop at 1,000,000, 10 steps per turn | 3.6 ms | 21 ms |
