  | `[n for n in range(100000) if sorted(range(1000)) == None] == []` | a stop at 1,000,000, 20 steps per turn: at the 50,000th turn | 932 ms | 8.6 s |

  The last row diverges from the measurement of R11 — the amendment to R11 is about this.

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T27 — «—», the result is set by the documents):
- one sandbox for the predicate, the template expressions and the solver — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.4 and 3.6; `Limits` and the thread are what they share;
- a list of names of its own in the resolver: the dialect is the 33 names that the format lists, while `starlark.Universe` is a variable of which go.starlark.net says "The Go application may add or remove items from the universe dictionary" (section 4). A name that the process of `api` added does not become a name of a predicate, and the rule and the verdict read the same;
- `EvalExprOptions` on an environment with `value`: so the vector counts the steps — 999,990 for `steps-below-the-limit` —, and a wrapper `lambda value: …` would add the steps of a call and take the vector past the limit;
- the first reason — as the thread itself has it: `Cancel` keeps it, "preserving earlier reason if any" (section 4);
- `unchecked` without a reason — the format and the contract do not name one, and the same release repeats the verdict on the stored value (R03 spec);
- `x/sys` — to the latest, as x/text in R23: it is simpler to close a finding of govulncheck by a raise than to keep it open.

— *Rejected:*
- `starlark.Universe.Has` instead of a list of its own: a process that adds a name to `Universe` would widen the dialect, the rule and the verdict;
- the predicate as `lambda value: …` or `ExprFunc` — one parsing per answer slot: other steps, and the vectors at the limit diverge;
- an error without a verdict on a predicate that breaks `task.predicate-expression`: the format gives an error of an expression `unchecked`, and R27 gives a verdict to a check that breaks a rule that it shows by itself;
- a reason of `unchecked` as a field of `SlotVerdict`: neither the format nor the contract of the observation names it. How the eval report writes the `unchecked` of a predicate is for `spec` to decide — В26 in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md);
- a fuzz test of the evaluation: a built-in function takes a gigabyte per step (Д10), and the worker of the fuzz test would fall from memory. `FuzzCheckExpr` checks only the parsing and the names, and the evaluation is run by `FuzzOfSlot` on the predicates of its own cases;
- `x/sys` v0.42.0, as go.starlark.net requires: govulncheck marks GO-2026-5024.

— *What is not closed:*
- memory — Д10;
- the template expressions and the solver — T31 and T32 on the same sandbox: the thread of a solver, on which the top level and all the `solve` calls go in one count and under one clock, is the business of T32;
- `task.predicate-expression` on the instances of a template — T31;
- how the eval report writes the `unchecked` of a predicate — В26 «в реестр spec».

— *Consequence:*
- the join of two readings and the verdict by an observation — T28: the predicate already gives a verdict, and it has to tell the `unchecked` of an observation and of a predicate apart;
- the list `paths` of the release has the files `internal/sandbox/*.go`, `verdict/verdict.go`, `module/*.go`, `go.mod` and `go.sum`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1.

— RUN.md, T27; `internal/sandbox/`; `verdict/`; `module/checks.go`; `internal/vectors/`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; `go.mod`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.4, 3.6 and 4.

## The observation

**R29. The validity of an observation `novatrail.observation.v1`, the join of two readings and the verdict by an observation are held by a new package `observation` over `verdict`, with four functions — one per rule of the [contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 10, and per field of its vector: `Validate`, `Join`, `Verdicts` and `Essay`. An invalid observation is the error `ErrNotValid`, and that means there is no observation: each answer slot that AI reads is `unchecked`. So an `unchecked` in the verdict by a valid observation is always the `unchecked` of a predicate. `verdict` stays without protobuf and gives the join its normal form of a value. The vectors `observation/v1/` of `contracts.Vectors` are passed.**

Decided by the author on 2026-11-05, in the planning of T28, by the recommendation of the plan: the package and the form of the API.

- **The package `observation`** imports `verdict`, `formatv0` and `observationv1`, the generated contract package of the module `spec/contracts`. The protobuf runtime comes only with it: `verdict`, `module` and the CLI do not take it.
- **`verdict` gives the join** four reasons of reading — `Illegible`, `Ambiguous`, `NoFinalAnswer` and `SeveralFinalAnswers`, beside `Blank` and `Notation` —, `NormalForm(slot, value)` and `ItemOf(slots)`:
  - `NormalForm` — the value as the kind normalizes it, without the key and without Starlark, and whether it is in notation. For a choice — the normal form, in notation when it is the form of the key or of an option; for `number`, `fraction` and `predicate` — steps 1–7, with the units of a number, in notation when the kind takes what is left. An empty form is empty. A rubric normalizes nothing;
  - `ItemOf` — the former `itemOf`: the verdict of an item from the verdicts of its answer slots, and without answer slots `unchecked`: nothing is checked;
  - a value not in UTF-8 `NormalForm` gives as it is, not in notation, as `Same` equates it to nothing.
- **`Validate(items, o)`** — the conditions of the [contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 10: each answer slot that AI reads — one not bound `choice` — once, in the order of the task, and no others; a value that is not empty and not only White_Space; a reason that is not `UNREADABLE_UNSPECIFIED` and is in the table of `module-kit`; `UNREADABLE_NO_FINAL_ANSWER`, `UNREADABLE_SEVERAL_FINAL_ANSWERS` and `crop_box` only for `anywhere`, and `crop_box` only beside a value, from 0 to 1000, `ymin < ymax` and `xmin < xmax`; for `sheet` — a value, `UNREADABLE_BLANK` or `UNREADABLE_ILLEGIBLE`. Beyond the text — a value not in UTF-8: there is no such message of the package (В27). The error names each condition broken, as the `breaches` of the second implementation do.
- **The table of reasons** — an explicit mapping `Unreadable` → `verdict.Reason`. A reason that the package adds later — even at a consumer that raises `spec/contracts` higher by MVS — is not valid to this release, as the [contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 10, requires: "A reason added to the package after a release of `module-kit` is not valid to that release". `TestReasonsAreThePackages` holds the table equal to the enumeration of the pinned tag.
- **`Join(items, first, second)`** — without `context` and without Starlark:
  - an essay is an error, it has one reader; a check that the verdict rejects is an error, whatever was read; if either is invalid — `ErrNotValid`, and the error names which;
  - a value with an empty normal form is `UNREADABLE_BLANK` before the pairing;
  - two values join when both are not in notation with one normal form, or both are in notation, equal by `verdict.Same`, and their verdicts by `verdict.OfSlot` are the same — the verdict, the reason and the trap's ID. For a predicate the verdicts are not compared: one integer gives one verdict;
  - when they join — the value of the first, the `crop_box` of the first or the second; two same reasons — that reason; the rest — `UNREADABLE_AMBIGUOUS`, without `crop_box`;
  - the joined observation is a new message: the places and the `crop_box` are copied, unknown fields of the input do not pass over.
- **`Verdicts(ctx, items, o, tapped)`** — the verdict of each item in the order of the task. `o` is what `Join` gave, or what `Validate` accepts: the reading of one reader, the truth of the markup. `nil` means there is no observation: each answer slot of AI is `unchecked`, and the answer slots `choice` get a verdict. A value — `verdict.OfSlot`, a predicate — in the sandbox with `ctx`; a reason — `unreadable` with the reason by the table; a tap — `OfSlot` on the text of the option, and without a tap — `blank`; an item — `verdict.ItemOf`. The errors are a `ctx` that is done, a task with a rubric (`verdict.ErrNoVerdict`), an item without answer slots, an invalid observation, a tap not at its own place and a check that `OfSlot` rejects.
- **The checks of the answer slots come before the reading.** `Join` and `Verdicts` first call `verdict.OfSlot` without a value on each answer slot that AI reads: so the check alone is read — the kind, the key, the options, the placeholder and the predicate —, without Starlark. A check that the verdict rejects is an error, whatever was read and whether there is an observation at all. Inside the join an error of `OfSlot` is therefore a defect of `module-kit` and a panic. This is a fix by the review: at first only two values in notation gave the error, while values not in notation, reasons and `nil` passed.
- **`Tapped{Place, Value}`** — as `api.v1.TappedChoice` (`attempt.proto`): only the taps, in the order of the task. A place not bound `choice`, one out of order or twice, or a tap without a place is an error.
- **`Essay(items, o)`** — `Submission`: `SubmissionAssessment` — a value, the text goes to the assessment by the rubric; `SubmissionUnreadable` — `UNREADABLE_BLANK` or `UNREADABLE_ILLEGIBLE`; `SubmissionUnchecked` — `nil`. An invalid one is `ErrNotValid`. A task that is not an essay is an error: an essay is a task whose only answer slot is bound `sheet` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 14 and 15).
- **В26.** Invalidity is named by `Validate`, `Join` and `Essay` with the error `ErrNotValid`: the runner puts `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_INVALID` by it, knows `FAILURE` and `LIMIT` itself, and an `unchecked` in `Verdicts` on a valid observation is the `unchecked` of a predicate. Which reason the report writes beside it is still for `spec` to decide — В26 «в реестр spec».
- **The dependency.** `google.golang.org/protobuf` v1.36.12, BSD-3-Clause, is direct: the tests take `protojson` and `proto`, and `observation` the generated package. This is the version that `spec/contracts` v0.9.2 requires. connect-go is not imported and does not go into the build. Dependabot `gomod` raises protobuf: it is not part of the format. `go list -deps ./module ./cmd/module-kit` is without protobuf, so it does not touch Д15.
- **The vector test.** The runner `observations` is in `internal/vectors/observation_test.go`. The file is strictly JSON, `DisallowUnknownFields`; the canvas and the items — by the schema of a task, `task.schema.json#/properties/canvas` and `#/$defs/item`, as in `observation.mjs`, and strictly by the types of `formatv0`; the observations — by `protojson.Unmarshal`, which refuses an unknown field and reads a number outside the enumeration, `1000` ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4). `valid` — by `Validate`, `joined` — `proto.Equal` with `Join`, `verdicts` — by `Verdicts`, `submission` — by `Essay`, where `ErrNotValid` is `unchecked`. `TestObservationsRefuse` holds that the runner refuses on a corruption of each field. 7 lines left the skip list — all of `contracts/vectors/observation/v1/` —, and 247 are left in it; before they were struck out, the test named exactly them.
- **Tests:**
  - `observation` — `TestReasonsAreThePackages`, `TestValidate` — a case per condition, `TestJoin`, `TestJoinSharesNothing`, `TestJoinErrors`, `TestVerdicts`, `TestVerdictsErrors`, `TestEssay` and `TestNotUTF8IsNoMessage`;
  - fuzz tests, the observation being foreign input: `FuzzJoin` — two readings of one answer slot of each kind and binding: there is a joined observation only when both are valid, the joined one is valid and gets a verdict, in the reverse order — the same "value or reason", and a reading joined with itself is itself, except an empty normal form, which is `UNREADABLE_BLANK`; `FuzzObservation` — any bytes through `proto.Unmarshal` on the tasks of the vectors: nothing panics, and a valid one joins with itself and gets a verdict or the outcome of an essay. The seed corpus is the observations of the vectors;
  - `verdict` — `TestNormalForm`, a value not in UTF-8 too, and `TestItemOf` — on `ItemOf`, without answer slots too.

— *Why:*
- a separate package: the format and the contract are split in `spec` the same way — the root module `spec` requires nothing, protobuf only the nested `contracts` (R64 spec), and the second implementation takes the key of the join from `verdicts.mjs` in `tools/contracts/observation.mjs`. `verdict` without protobuf is needed by the module check and the CLI, which read no observations;
- four functions — a rule per function: each is held by its own field of the vector, the repeat of a verdict in `api` and the truth of the markup at the runner are the same `Verdicts` on one observation, and the `unchecked` of an invalid observation and the `unchecked` of a predicate are told apart by the form of the API, without the field in `SlotVerdict` that R28 rejected;
- `Join` without `context` and without Starlark: verdicts are compared only for `number`, `fraction` and `choice`, and a predicate on one integer gives one verdict. Otherwise the clock of `module-kit` would make the join depend on the machine;
- a value not in UTF-8 is invalid, not an error: proto3 requires of a string "A string must always contain UTF-8 encoded or 7-bit ASCII text", and protobuf-go neither writes nor reads such a string ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4). An invalid observation is "as a failure of AI", and the learner gets `unchecked`, not a refusal of the server;
- the table of reasons is its own, not the enumeration of the package: so a new reason at a consumer with a new `contracts` too is invalid until `module-kit` names it.

— *Rejected:*
- everything in `verdict`, as the table of Go packages of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6, had it: protobuf would come into `module`, and from T37 into the CLI's binary and into Д15;
- one function `Read` on 0–2 observations with the result `{Joined, Invalid, Items, Submission}`: two forms of the result, and the meaning depends on the length of the slice;
- the reason of `unchecked` as a field of `SlotVerdict` — R28 rejected it;
- the taps as one value for each answer slot `choice`, `nil` without a tap, as the vector writes them: `api` gets only the taps (`api.v1.TappedChoice`), and the form is its own;
- comparing in `Join` the verdicts of a predicate too: Starlark on each pair and the clock in the join;
- a value not in UTF-8 as an error without a verdict, as for `verdict.OfSlot`: the observation comes from AI, and an invalid one is its outcome, not a defect of the caller.

— *What is not closed:*
- a value not in UTF-8 the contracts text does not name — В27 «в реестр spec»;
- which reason the eval report writes beside the `unchecked` of a predicate — В26 «в реестр spec»;
- `Verdicts` on an instance of a template — with T31: `api` gives an instance with numbers, and a task with a placeholder is `verdict.ErrPlaceholder`.

— *Consequence:*
- `api` and the eval runner of `tutor` take `observation`, and the types of the verdict from `verdict`;
- the list `paths` of the release has the new `observation/*.go`, `verdict/*.go`, `go.mod`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1.

— RUN.md, T28; `observation/`; `verdict/`; `internal/vectors/`; `internal/sandbox/sandbox_test.go`; `go.mod`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2, 2.6, 3, 3.1, 3.6 and 4.

## The leak check

**R30. The leak check is held by the package `leak`: `For(task)` builds the search of a task — the keys of its checks `number`, `fraction` and `choice` that the texts of their item do not show, and its leak phrases —, and `Search.In(text)` gives what of them was found in the text, each a `Leak` with its place in the task. A template is the error `ErrTemplate`: it is searched by its instances. Normalization and the notation of numbers moved from `verdict` to `internal/normal`: its second consumer came. The module check holds `task.no-leaks` — within a part the texts of the task itself, between parts the texts of the catalogs and the rung templates — and `lesson.no-leaks` between parts, on tasks without a template. The vectors `leaks/` and those of these rules are passed, except two cases with a template.**

- **API** (`leak`), by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20:
  - `For(task) (*Search, error)` — what is searched for: the key of each check `number`, `fraction` and `choice` of each item, except a key that the same search finds in a text that the learner sees with its item — in the paragraphs of the statement and the `alt` of its pictures, in the `text` of the item, in the `text` pieces of its line and in the options of its answer slots `choice` —, and each leak phrase. A key of a number and of a fraction not in the notation of its kind is not searched for, as in `leaks.mjs` of `spec`: a placeholder outside a template breaks `task.placeholders` (T31). A template is `ErrTemplate`;
  - `Search.In(text) []Leak` — what was found, in the order of the task: the keys by items and answer slots, then the phrases. `Leak{Kind, Written, Item, Slot, Phrase}` — a key or a phrase as the task writes them; for a key — the item and the answer slot from 0, the answer slots in the order of `verdict.AnswerSlots`, for a phrase — its place in `leak_phrases`. A key of one magnitude in two answer slots is found twice. The search changes nothing after `For`, and one `Search` searches from several goroutines at once;
  - `api` builds the search on the instance of an attempt — a task with numbers, without `template` — and searches every one of the tutor's words with it: if anything was found — the fallback text (R54 spec).
- **The search:**
  - the normal form — step 1, NFKC, steps 3 and 4 and the simple lowercase mapping: `normal.Choice(normal.Scripts(text))`. A character of a word is one of the categories L, M and N of the package `unicode`; U+FFFD is not a character of a word;
  - a phrase — a key of a choice and a leak phrase — is searched for as a substring of the normal form at each place where it stands, and is found where an edge that is itself a character of a word has no character of a word outside it: so `odd` is found in "oddly odd". The substring is searched for by bytes, `strings.Index`: both forms are UTF-8, and a match of bytes begins and ends on a boundary of characters. An empty form is found nowhere;
  - numbers — by the characters of the normal form, from left to right: at a digit 0–9 — the longest run of the form of a number, a fraction, a decimal or an integer; the run is a number when it has no character of a word outside it, and the search goes on after the whole run. The value is exact, a `big.Rat`, leading zeros and all. A key is compared by magnitude, and the text gives no sign. The numbers of a text are read once and only when a key of a number or a fraction is searched for: for a task with phrases alone no `big.Rat` is built (a fix by the review);
  - a byte not in UTF-8 in a text is U+FFFD: step 1 reads the text by the characters of Go. The rest of the text around such a byte is searched as usual.
- **Normalization** — `internal/normal`, as R27 and [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6, promised: step 1 — `Scripts`, steps 2–4 — `Text`, `Lower`, the form of a choice — `Choice`, steps 1–7 — `Number`, the form of a unit — `Unit`, the notation of a number — `Notation`, `Written{Value, Lowest}`, — and `UnicodeVersion`. The code moved without changes of meaning, `verdict` calls it, and `verdict.UnicodeVersion` is `normal.UnicodeVersion`. `TestSteps`, `TestNFKCIsStreamSafe` and `FuzzNotation` moved together with it, and `unitsOf` stayed in `verdict`: it reads the check.
- **The rules** (`module`, `leaks.go`):
  - `task.no-leaks` within a part — the texts of the task itself: `givens`, `unknown`, the `text` of each trap value and the `simpler_case` of each item. `CheckFile` holds them too: `format-check` checks an example of a part the same way, without other parts;
  - `task.no-leaks` between parts — the title and description of each technique that the task names, the `trap_advice` of each trap that it names — in `traps` and in the trap values —, and the rung templates of the manifest, when the task has an item with a verdict;
  - a breach of `task.no-leaks` is in the file of the task, one per text, at the place of the text; for a text of another part — at the place where the task names it: `#/techniques/<number>`, the first place of the trap, and for a rung template — `#`. The message names a text of another part by its file and place, `techniques.yaml#/techniques/draw/title`, each key found by the place of its answer slot, and a phrase by its place;
  - `lesson.no-leaks` between parts — the title of the lesson, the paragraphs and the `alt` of the pictures of its explanations, of its statements and of the demos of its worked examples, by the keys and phrases of each task of the module that the lesson names. A breach is at the place of the text in the lesson, one per text and task, and names the segment of the task and the file of the task — the path by which the lesson found it, not its `id`: a file with another's `id` breaks `module.file-ids`, and the rules between parts run all the same (a fix by the review);
  - the search of a task is built once per its path, `checker.searchOf`, and serves both rules: within a part, between parts and each lesson that names it (a fix by the review);
  - a task with `template` both rules skip, and a lesson does not search for the keys of a template: they are searched for on the instances, T31. A task, a technique and a trap that the module does not have the rules skip: that is `lesson.known-tasks`, `task.known-techniques` and `task.known-traps`.
- **The vector test.** The runner `leaks` reads a vector by the schema and the type of `formatv0` and checks the set of what was found in each text against it. 29 lines left the skip list — 17 of `leaks/` and 12 vectors of rules —, and 218 are left in it; before they were struck out, the test named exactly them. `rules/task.no-leaks/key-of-an-instance-in-the-simpler-case.yaml` and `rules/lesson.no-leaks/key-of-an-instance-in-the-explanation` are templates, and T31 passes them.
- **Tests:**
  - `leak` — `TestIn`: texts that free a key, a choice written by hand, two keys of one magnitude, the order of what was found, a byte not in UTF-8 and a placeholder; `TestFor`, `TestNumbersIn`, `TestHolds`, `TestConcurrentIn`, `TestKeyOf` and `FuzzNumbers`. The fuzz test writes a number of each form with leading zeros: between characters that are not of a word the search finds it, by itself and once, and with a character of a word beside it neither it nor its tail; on any text the numbers are the same as those the model reads — the regular expression of the longest run;
  - `module` — `TestLeakRules`: each place of a breach of both rules on a whole module; `TestLeakRulesOfAFile`, `TestLeakRulesSkipTemplates`, `TestLeakRuleMessages` and `TestLeakRuleNamesTheTasksFile`. `TestChecksTheFormat` holds that neither an example nor a vector of another rule breaks the new rules;
  - `internal/normal` — `TestSteps`, `TestNFKCIsStreamSafe` and `FuzzNotation`.

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T29 — «—», the result is set by the documents):
- the search is built once per task: `api` searches every one of the tutor's words on one attempt, the module check dozens of texts of one task, and the free keys are computed from the texts of the item once;
- `ErrTemplate`, not a search by the template as it is: the phrase `{a} rabbits` would be found nowhere, and the tutor's words that give the answer away would pass silently;
- normalization is in `internal/`: its second consumer is the leak, as R20 and R27 required. Neither `verdict` nor `leak` gives it out: the forms are the format's business, not the API's;
- the answer slots of an item are `verdict.AnswerSlots`: the order of answer slots is one in the verdict, the observation and the leak, and `Leak.Slot` is the same place as in the verdict;
- `task.no-leaks` within a part and between parts — the boundary of R25 and R26: a rule within a part reads its own file, and `CheckFile` checks an example of a part, as `format-check` does, which has no other parts in it;
- the breach is in the file of the task: the rule is a rule of the task, and the report puts `task.*` in the file of the task, as with `task.known-traps`. Where the text of another part stands, the message says;
- a byte not in UTF-8 is U+FFFD, not an error: the leak check is a safeguard, and a text with such a byte is searched whole, rather than passing silently. An error of `In` would add a branch to each call of `api`, and would find no leak.

— *Rejected:*
- one function `Find(task, text)`: the free keys and the forms of the phrases would be computed for each text;
- the search of a phrase by comparing characters at each place, as in `leaks.mjs`: `strings.Index` over the bytes of UTF-8 finds the same places faster;
- normalization as exported functions of `verdict`: the forms of the leak would stand in the API of the verdict;
- one breach per task, as in `format-check`: the author would not see which text to fix;
- a breach of a text of another part in that part's file: the rule is the task's, and for a technique shared by several tasks each has its own leak.

— *What is not closed:*
- templates — T31: the texts of an instance by its keys and phrases, the texts of the module and of a lesson by the keys of each instance; a key and a phrase with a result of the solver — T32;
- the Stream-Safe Text Process against ICU concerns the leak too — В24.

— *Consequence:*
- `api` takes `leak` on the tutor's words, and the verdict and the leak normalize with one code;
- the list `paths` of the release has the new `leak/leak.go`, `internal/normal/*.go` and the edits of `verdict/verdict.go` and `module/*.go`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1.

— RUN.md, T29; `leak/`; `internal/normal/`; `verdict/`; `module/`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3 and 3.6.

## The generator and the instance of an attempt

**R31. The generator is the package `internal/random`, as R10 decided: `Stream` with five functions, `New(high, low)` — a stream from two words, `Words(seed, names)` and `Place(seed, names...)` — a place of randomness, and on an argument outside the domain — the error `ErrDomain`, and the function draws no integers. What goes outside is `template.AttemptInstance(seed, task, count)` — the number of the instance of an attempt: `below(count)` of the place with the names `template`, the task's ID and `instance`, for count from 1 to `MaxInstances`, 10,000, and a refusal is `template.ErrDomain`. The vectors `random/` are passed, and the comparison with `math/rand/v2` and `hash/fnv` runs as an ordinary test on every `go test` and as a fuzz test with the same corpus.**

- **API** (`internal/random`), by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 22:
  - `New(high, low uint64) *Stream` — a stream whose state is the two words as they are; `Words(seed uint64, names []string) (high, low uint64, err error)` — the words of a place; `Place(seed uint64, names ...string) (*Stream, error)` — its stream. The constants are the numbers of the format text, with the hexadecimal notation beside them;
  - `Integer() uint64`, `Below(b uint64) (uint64, error)`, `Between(a, c int64) (int64, error)`, `Fraction() float64` and `Permutation(n int) ([]int, error)`, and `MaxPermutation` is 2^31 − 1. The order is an `[]int`, as with Go's `Perm`;
  - `ErrDomain` — one error for all the refusals, and what is wrong the message says: `below(0)`; `between` with a > c and with a count above 2^63 − 1 — first a ≤ c, then `uint64(c) − uint64(a)` no greater than 2^63 − 2; `permutation` outside 0…2^31 − 1; a place without names, and a name that is empty, with `|` or not in UTF-8. A refusal of a function draws no integers, a refusal of a place builds no stream;
  - a `Stream` is not for two goroutines at once, and the package has no shared state: a stream lives in the call that built it.
- **The instance of an attempt** (`template/attempt.go`):
  - `AttemptInstance(seed uint64, task formatv0.TaskID, count int) (int, error)` — the number from 0, in the order of the counter. A count outside 1…`MaxInstances` and an ID that is not a name of a place are the error `template.ErrDomain`, and with the ID of a valid task this never happens (`common.schema.json`). `template.ErrDomain` is `random.ErrDomain`: `api` and the eval runner of `tutor` do not import the package `internal/random`, and `errors.Is` finds it, whichever refusal gave it (a fix by the review);
  - the function takes no template and counts no instances: their numbers, in order, the package carries ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 23 and 25), and count is their number. The instances of a template and its open view are T31, and a function over this one that gives the instance itself will come with them or with the package, T33.
- **The vector test.** The runner `random` is `randomDraws` in `internal/vectors`: a vector is read by the YAML reader, the schema `random-vector.schema.json` and the type `formatv0.RandomVector`, and the numbers written as strings by `strconv`. If a seed and names are given, `Words` is checked against `state`; then the stream from the words of `state` gives the calls in order, and a fraction is compared as a double. A call without a known field fails the case, though the schema would not let it pass anyway. 12 lines left the skip list — all the vectors of `random/` —, and 206 are left in it; before they were struck out, the test named exactly them. `TestRandomDrawsRefuse` holds that the runner catches a wrong field of each kind and an integer that a word of 64 bits does not hold.
- **Tests:**
  - `internal/random` — `TestWords`: the words of the vectors, each refusal of a place, wherever the invalid name stands, and é of one character and of two — different places; `TestDraws`: the number of integers of each function, in steps from state to state — `below(1)` and `between(a, a)` draw one, `permutation(0)` and `permutation(1)` none, `below(2^63 + 1)` from the words 0 and 2 — five, as in `below-refusals.yaml`; `TestRefusals`: on each refusal — `ErrDomain`, the zero value and the previous state; `TestEdges`;
  - the comparison with the standard library — `TestReference` and `FuzzReference` with a shared `compare`: the words of a place against `hash/fnv`, the names as one string, joined by `|`, as the text of a place writes them: there is no `|` in a name, so the fuzz test reaches any list of valid names, with a line break too (a fix by the review), and then, on one stream, `integer`, `below`, `between`, `fraction` and `permutation` against `Uint64`, `Uint64N`, `Int64N`, `Float64` and `Perm`, and after each function the state of the stream is compared with `PCG.MarshalBinary`. So the number of integers that a function draws is checked too. `TestStateAsPCGWritesIt` holds that `MarshalBinary` writes the state as the test writes it;
  - the inputs of `TestReference` — the words of 12 vectors and the words 0 and 2^64 − 1, each with each bound of `below` — 0, 1, powers of two and their neighbours, 2^63 ± 1, 2^64 − 1 — and in turn with the edges of `between`, counts of permutations up to 1024 and names of places, about 2,700 inputs, and 5,000 more inputs that the generator itself gives from the seed 2026. The corpus of `FuzzReference` is the same inputs, except those that the generator gives: they are not data but a consequence of the code. A permutation longer than 1024 the comparison only rejects or skips;
  - `template` — `TestAttemptInstanceOfTheVectors`: 16 seeds of all five vectors of `templates/` without a solver. The number of the instance is derived from the numbers of the vector in the order of the counter and written in the test, and the test reads the vectors from `spec.Format` and holds that they give the same seeds and the same numbers and that there are no other vectors without a solver: on a new tag of `spec` the copy will not fall behind the vectors (a fix by the review); `TestAttemptInstanceIsItsPlace`: on each count from 1 to 10,000 the number is below count and is the one that the place `template`, ID, `instance` gives; `TestAttemptInstanceRefuses`.

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T30 — «—», the result is set by R10):
- `Words` and `New` are exported by the package: the vector test compares the derived words with `state`, and the stream from the words with the calls, as R10 requires. `Place` is for a template, which needs no words;
- one `ErrDomain`: the caller needs to know that the argument is outside the domain, and which one and why is the message's. `internal/random` does not go outside, and `template` gives the error out under its own name;
- `AttemptInstance` takes count, not the template: `api` counts no instances and executes no expressions — the package carries their numbers ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 23) —, and counting the instances is the counter and the expressions of T31;
- the task's ID is not checked against the form `local_id`: a place is defined on any name, the ID comes from a checked package, and the form is held by the module check. A name that is not a name of a place is an error, not a panic;
- the state after each function is compared with `PCG.MarshalBinary`, not by the next integer: so the number of integers drawn is checked exactly, not by a coincidence of numbers.

— *Rejected:*
- a function of the instance of an attempt from the template, `formatv0.Task`, with a count of the instances: it needs the counter of T31, and `api` knows the number of instances from the package;
- a check of the ID against `common.schema.json` in `AttemptInstance`: a second place for the form `local_id`, and it does not change the number;
- an error for each function: the caller would tell apart five errors of one meaning;
- a check of the number of integers by the next integer of two streams: two different states can give one next integer.

— *Consequence:*
- T31 passes the vectors `templates/` by seeds through `AttemptInstance`: the instance of a seed is the number among the instances that it will build;
- the list `paths` of the release has the new `internal/random/random.go` and `template/attempt.go` and the edits of `doc.go` of two packages, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1. The CLI does not take them: `go list -deps ./cmd/module-kit` is without third-party modules, and Д15 does not change.

— RUN.md, T30; `internal/random/`; `template/`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.3 and 3.6.

## Task templates

**R32. Task templates are the package `template`: `Instances(task)` checks a template by the rules that it breaks by itself — `task.template-names`, `task.template-expressions`, `task.placeholders` and `task.template-instances` — and counts its instances in the order of the counter, and `Instance(task, numbers)` substitutes the numbers of an instance in a single pass, without expressions and Starlark. An expression of the subset is read and typed by a parsing of its own, as in `format-check` of `spec`; beyond it, go.starlark.net of the format must parse it, and the sandbox computes it: `sandbox.Compile` compiles an expression once, and `Func.Call` evaluates it on each combination. The module check checks a template by its instances: the rules of a task that read the strings of the task — on each instance, and a breach names the numbers of the first instance that breaks the rule at that place; the rules that read no strings — on the template as it is written; `task.instances` holds each instance by the schema of a task. The vectors `templates/` without a solver and the vectors of the rules of templates are passed.**

- **API** (`template`), by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 23:
  - `Instances(task) ([]formatv0.Numbers, []Breach)` — the numbers of each instance in the order of the counter: each parameter and each derived number by its name. A template that breaks a rule of templates gives breaches and not a single instance; a task without a template — neither. There are no results of the solver among the numbers: the solver comes with T32;
  - `Instance(task, numbers) (formatv0.Task, error)` — the instance: each placeholder of each string outside `template` is its number, in decimal digits and with `-` before a negative one, and a doubled brace is one, in a single pass; there is no `template`, and the `id` is the template's. The function executes no expressions and no Starlark: `api` substitutes the numbers that the package carries, by the number of `AttemptInstance` (R31), and the open view of the instance is `openview.Task` of it. An error is for a task without a template, for a string that breaks `task.placeholders`, and for a placeholder whose number is absent; the instance is not checked against the schema of a task — that is `task.instances` of the module check;
  - `Breach{Rule, Pointer, Message}` — a breach of a rule of templates with the place of the value, and `RuleNames`, `RuleExpressions`, `RulePlaceholders` and `RuleInstances` are the IDs of these rules: the rules of templates are the validity of the template itself, and the package needs this same count (T33). `module` gives them under its own names, `RuleTaskTemplateNames` and the others, and adds `RuleTaskInstances`;
  - `Shown(template, numbers)` — the numbers of an instance, as a breach names them: the parameters in the order of the names, `a = 1, b = 4`, as `instanceShown` in `templates.mjs` of `spec`.
- **The order of the rules** — as in `format-check`: the names, the expressions and the placeholders are all checked, and if at least one is broken, the combinations are not counted. Then — an empty domain, at each of its parameters, `#/template/parameters/<name>`; the product of the sizes of the domains is a `big.Int`, and more than 10,000 is at `#/template/parameters`, with the exact number; the first combination on which an expression fails or a derived number goes beyond ±(2^53 − 1) — one breach at that expression, and the count stops; not a single instance — at `#/template/constraint`. The names are at `#/template/derived/<name>` and `#/template/solver/results/<number>`, the expressions at their own place.
- **Expressions:**
  - the parsing — its own tokens, grammar and types of the subset (`template/expression.go`), as `expressions.mjs` of `spec`: tokens are separated by spaces U+0020 or by nothing, a name or an integer is the longest run of `[a-z0-9_]`, an integer has no leading zero, comparisons do not chain, and the types `int` and `bool` follow the rules of the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 23. An error names the token and its place;
  - the dialect — beyond the subset, an expression must be accepted by `sandbox.CheckExpr`, as a check predicate is: the subset is Starlark, and it runs in the dialect of the format. In two places go.starlark.net parses less, and a fuzz test holds that there are only two: a space before the first token it reads as an indent, "got indent", and a nesting greater than the depth of its parsing — 75 parentheses or 987 unary minuses in a row — is "excessive nesting". `format-check` accepts both, and there is no vector for either — row В28 of table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md), «в реестр spec»;
  - the evaluation — `sandbox.Compile(src, names...)` once: a program of one assignment `_result = <expression>` with the names as predeclared, built from the tree of the parsed expression, not from the text — it compiles everything that `CheckExpr` takes, a comment and a line break too, is as deep for the parsing as the expression itself, and makes the same steps as `Eval` (a fix by the review) —, and `Func.Call` on each combination — `Program.Init` on its own thread, with its own values of the names. An expression has no limit of steps and no clock: it calls no functions and holds no loops, and its steps are bounded by its length ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 23). Division by zero is a breach of `task.template-instances` with the words of Starlark: "floored division by zero", "integer modulo by zero". The derived numbers are computed in the order of the names, then the constraint — on the parameters and derived numbers in the same order; `and` and `or` do not compute the right operand when the left one has decided;
  - the order of the names is by code units, and a name is ASCII, so this is the byte order of Go.
- **Placeholders** (`template/placeholders.go`) — a string is read from left to right: `{{` is `{`, `}}` is `}`, `{name}` is a placeholder, and any other brace is an error at the place of its character. The strings of a task outside `template` are the strings of its JSON value, `schema.ValueOf` — the function moved from `openview` to `internal/schema`: three packages take it now. A breach — one per string: a brace, a name that does not exist, or a result of the solver outside the key and the trap value of `number` and `fraction`, the solution and the leak phrase. Outside a template, a key or a trap value of `number` and `fraction` with a placeholder is `task.placeholders` at `…/check`, one per answer slot: a placeholder is still recognized in one place, `verdict.ErrPlaceholder` (R27).
- **The module check** (`module/templates.go`):
  - a template is expanded once, in `parts.add`, for all the rules that read its instances: the breaches of its rules, and without them each instance, its numbers and which keywords of the schema of a task it does not pass;
  - within a part — the rules of a template, `task.instances` and, on each instance that the schema accepts, the rules that the slot check shows by itself, and `task.no-leaks` on the texts of the task; between parts — `task.no-leaks` on the texts of other parts and `lesson.no-leaks` of a lesson that names the template, by the keys and phrases of each instance. Each instance is checked by its own `checker`, so the search of the leak is built per instance, not per path;
  - the author fixes the place where a rule is broken: the report keeps the breaches at that place of the first instance that breaks the rule there, and each begins with "on the instance first = 2, second = 7:". 10,000 instances whose trap value is their key give one breach, not 10,000. An instance that breaks the rule at the same place otherwise — a text with the key of one instance and the key of another — is seen when the place is fixed (a fix by the review: before, the documentation said that each instance breaks a place the same way);
  - the rules that do not read the strings of a task — `task.skill-roles`, `task.zones`, `task.known-*`, `task.own-anchors`, `task.assessability`, `task.age-categories`, `manifest.rung-templates`, the rules of a lesson except the leak — read the template as it is written, and a breach stands once, without numbers. Their fields — IDs, words of enumerations, numbers and paths — hold no braces by the schema, so for each instance they are the same as for the template;
  - `task.instances` — each keyword of the schema at each place, by its first instance that does not pass it: different keywords are different errors (a fix by the review) —, except `unevaluatedProperties`, if there are others: the keys of an instance are the same as those of the template, which passed the schema, so a field stays "unevaluated" only beside a subschema that did not pass — and that is a keyword of its own. Otherwise one long digit of a key would give seven breaches;
  - a template with a solver is checked in T31 only by its own rules: the rules of a task on its instances read the results of the solver, and those come with T32. CI of `spec` checks such an instance "on what holds no result", and `module-kit` waits for the results.
- **The cost.** A template of 10,000 instances — the example `missing-factor` with domains from 1 to 100 — `CheckFile` checks in 1.5 s: the schema of the instances takes 0.6 s, the substitution and the round trips of JSON 0.5 s, and the count of the expressions in the sandbox less than 0.1 s (`go test`, AMD Ryzen AI 9 365, 2026-11-05). The template `heads-and-legs` of 81 combinations is counted in 2 ms.
- **The vector test.** The runner `templates` reads a vector by the schema and the type `formatv0.TemplateVector`; the template is checked by `module.CheckFile` — its JSON, and JSON is YAML ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 4) —, and the runner expects an empty report; then the number of instances, and for each attempt the number of `AttemptInstance` by the seed, its numbers, the instance, equal to the vector's as a JSON value, and its open view. A vector with a solver is `errNoSolver` until T32. 22 lines left the skip list — 5 vectors of `templates/` without a solver and 17 vectors of rules: `task.template-names`, `task.template-expressions`, `task.placeholders`, `task.template-instances`, `task.instances` and the template cases of `task.trap-values`, `task.no-leaks` and `lesson.no-leaks` —, and 184 are left in it. Before they were struck out, the test named exactly them.
- **Tests:**
  - `template` — `TestTypeOf`: each rule of the tokens, the grammar and the types; `TestPiecesOf`, `TestStringsOf`, `TestInstances`: the order of the counter, a constraint with lazy `and` and `or`, floored division; `TestInstancesBreaches`: each rule of templates at each place, and the expressions of the subset that go.starlark.net does not parse, and the deepest that it parses; `TestInstance`, `TestShown`, `TestTakesResults`; `TestAttemptInstanceOfTheVectors` now also holds that `Instances` at the number of an attempt gives its parameters. `FuzzTypeOf` checks the parsing against go.starlark.net: an accepted expression it accepts too, except in the two places of В28, and on any two integers it gives a value of the same type or fails with division by zero. `FuzzPiecesOf`: what was read and written back is the same string;
  - `internal/sandbox` — `TestCompile`: the order of the names, the name `_result` among the names, a comment and a line break — in parentheses, at the start and before the expression —, an error of the expression, the steps of the vectors at the limit — the same as those of `Eval` —, and a cancellation of the `context`; `TestConcurrentCall`;
  - `module` — `TestTemplateRules`, `TestTemplateRulesOfAModule`, `TestTemplateRuleMessages` and `TestInstancesByKeyword`; `TestLeakRulesOfAFile` — a template is now searched by its instances; `TestChecksTheFormat` holds that neither an example nor a vector of another rule breaks the new rules;
  - `internal/schema` — `TestValueOf`, moved from `openview`.

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T31 — «—», the result is set by the documents):
- the meaning of a template is in `template`, together with the IDs of its rules: the rules of templates speak of the template itself, and the package build, T33, needs the same count; the rules of a task on instances are rules of `module`, as before;
- a parsing of its own of the subset, and beyond it the parsing of go.starlark.net: the messages are in the words of the subset and about its tokens, the parsing is the same as that of the second implementation, and that the dialect takes less is seen as a separate breach, not silently;
- an expression is compiled once: a template has up to 10,000 combinations, and parsing with compilation on each would repeat the same work;
- the rules without strings — by the template: 10,000 identical breaches "task.known-band" do not help the author, and the result is the same;
- the breaches of the first instance at a place, not one per rule, as in `format-check`: the author sees each place to fix and the numbers on which it breaks;
- a template with a solver waits for T32: a check of an instance without results is code that T32 would remove, and without it there are no false breaches.

— *Rejected:*
- computing the expressions with arithmetic of its own on `big.Int`, as `format-check` does: the format requires the meaning of Starlark and the dialect of the format, and arithmetic of its own would be a second implementation inside one;
- reading the subset by a walk of the tree of go.starlark.net: its parsing takes more — unary `+`, `in`, tuples, the conditional expression —, and the messages would be in the words of Starlark, not of the subset;
- `lambda <names>: (<expression>)` in `Compile`: the wrapper spends levels of parsing, and an expression at the limit that `CheckExpr` accepts would not compile — the fuzz test found this;
- checking all the rules of a task on each instance and naming the numbers at each breach;
- keeping in the report each distinct breach at a place: for a template where the trap value is the key, there would be as many of them as instances, "the trap value N gets correct" for each N;
- checking an instance of a template with a solver, skipping the results, as `format-check` does;
- in `task.instances` — all the keywords of the schema, with the cascade of `unevaluatedProperties`;
- reading a template once for all its instances by a separate API: 1.5 s per template of 10,000 instances, and the API would grow for the sake of one module check.

— *What is not closed:*
- a space before the first token and nesting deeper than the parsing of go.starlark.net — В28;
- a template with a solver — T32: the rules of a task and `task.instances` on its instances, the `inputs` of the vectors, two vectors of `templates/` with a solver and 16 vectors of `task.template-solver`.

— *Consequence:*
- T32 puts the results of the solver into the numbers of the instances: the module check then checks the instances of a template with a solver too, and `errNoSolver` leaves the runner `templates`;
- T33 takes the numbers of `Instances` into `instances` of the canonical view, and the digest of the open view of an instance from `openview.Task(Instance(…))`;
- `api` builds the instance of an attempt so: `AttemptInstance`, `Instance` by the numbers from the package, `openview.Task`;
- the list `paths` of the release has the new `template/expression.go`, `template/placeholders.go` and `template/instances.go` and the edits of `template/doc.go`, `internal/sandbox/*.go`, `internal/schema/*.go`, `openview/*.go` and `module/*.go`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1. The CLI does not take them: `go list -deps ./cmd/module-kit` is without third-party modules, and Д15 does not change.

— RUN.md, T31; `template/`; `internal/sandbox/`; `internal/schema/`; `openview/`; `module/`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.3, 3.4 and 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-05 (T32):* `Instances` takes the caller's `context` and returns an error — `Instances(ctx, task) ([]formatv0.Numbers, []Breach, error)`. The count looks at this `context` before each combination, and after the count the solver runs with the same one, and its results go into the numbers of each instance; `task.template-solver` is also a rule that a template breaks by itself, `RuleSolver`. The error is only a `context` that is done: it is never a breach (R11). A template with a solver the module check reads by its instances, as a template without one. — R33; RUN.md, T32.

## Template solvers

**R33. The solver of a template is executed by the package `template` inside `Instances(ctx, task)`: after the count of the instances the program of the solver runs once — `sandbox.Exec` on its own thread, with the format's budget of 100,000,000 steps and the clock of `module-kit` of 60 s, `sandbox.Solver` —, and `solve` is called on each instance in order on the same thread, with a frozen dict of the numbers that the open fields of the template show, `Inputs(task)`. The global values of the program after its top level are frozen, as Starlark freezes a module. The results go into the numbers of the instance, and the rules of a task, `task.instances` and the leak read the instances of a template with a solver as they read those without one. A solver that fails is one breach of `task.template-solver`, on the first instance where it fails, with its numbers, or before the first, and the count stops; a `context` that is done is an error without a breach. The vectors `rules/task.template-solver/` and `templates/` with a solver are passed.**

- **The sandbox** (`internal/sandbox`):
  - `CheckProgram(src)` — a program of the dialect: the parsing by `syntax.FileOptions.Parse` with the options of the dialect; `load`, wherever it stands, is "the dialect has no load" at its place; the names — `resolve.File` without predeclared names and with its own list of the 33 built-in ones, not with `starlark.Universe`, as for `CheckExpr` (R28). An error is the line, the column and the words of go.starlark.net: `2:1: got newline, want ':'`, `2:45: undefined: gcd`;
  - `Exec(ctx, limits, src)` — the `context`, then `CheckProgram`, the parsing again and `starlark.FileProgram` outside the thread: a program that `CheckProgram` accepted and the compiler did not is a defect of `module-kit`, a panic, not the `ErrPanic` of a solver. Then `Program.Init` on its own thread and the freezing of the global values: "Immediately after execution of a Starlark module, all values in its top-level environment are frozen" (the Starlark specification in `doc/spec.md` of go.starlark.net 4f4267fb140d, Freezing a value, read 2026-11-05) — so does `starlark.ExecFileOptions`, while `Program.Init` itself does not freeze. An error is as for `Eval`: the `context`, `ErrSteps`, `ErrClock`, `ErrPanic` or an error of the top level; then there is no `Program`;
  - `Program.Global(name)` — the value of a global name; `Program.Call(fn, args...)` — a call on the thread of the program, within what the top level and the earlier calls have left of the steps and the clock, with the same errors; after a panic `Call` calls nothing and returns the same error, and after `Close` it returns `ErrClosed`: the program no longer has the clock and the watching of the `context` (a fix by the review). `Program.Close()` stops the clock and the watching of the `context`, and it can be called again;
  - `Solver` — `Limits{Steps: 100_000_000, Clock: time.Minute}` (R11);
  - the thread is moved out of `run` into a type of its own: `start` — a thread with the limit of steps, the clock and the watching of the `context`, `do` — one evaluation on it with `recover` and the first reason of the stop, `end` — the end of the clock and of the watching. `run` is `start`, `do` and `end` for one evaluation, and `Program` is `start` in `Exec`, `do` for the top level and for each `Call`, `end` in `Close`. So the predicate, the template expressions and the solver stop by one code;
  - the count is checked against the vectors: the top level of `within-the-budget` is 10 steps, the call of `solve` with a loop of 9,999,996 turns is 99,999,980, 99,999,990 in all, and there is a result; one turn more — `reaches-the-budget` — and the count reaches 100,000,000, and the call stops. The steps are 10 per entry into a Starlark function, per return of each call in it, of a built-in function too, and per each backward jump, and the thread sees a cancellation there too (`interp.go`, `checkSteps`): a clock that fired between calls stops the next call at its entry.
- **The solver** (`template/solver.go`), by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 24:
  - `Inputs(task)` — the names of the numbers that `solve` gets: the parameters and derived numbers whose placeholders stand in the strings of the open view of the template as it is written, `schema.Open`, in the order of code units — as `solverInputsOf` in `templates.mjs` of `spec`. The openness of the fields is held by the schema of a task, and the list of the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 24, is its retelling: the statement, and the `alt` of its picture too, the givens and the unknown, the text of the item, the pieces of its line and the options of an answer slot bound `choice`. The key, the trap value and the trap text, the solution, the leak phrase and `simpler_case` are closed. A name in doubled braces is not a placeholder, and a string that breaks `task.placeholders` gives no names of its own;
  - together with the rules of the names, the expressions and the placeholders — `CheckProgram`: a program that does not parse, holds `load` or names a name outside the dialect breaks the rule "before the first instance", however the instances would be counted, and the combinations are then not counted;
  - after the count — `Exec`, and `solve` must be a function of the program — `def` or `lambda`, `*starlark.Function` — with one parameter, `NumParams() == 1`, which may have a default value, — without keyword-only parameters, `*args` and `**kwargs`: its only parameter gets the dict positionally. go.starlark.net counts `def solve(*, numbers)` as a function of one parameter, and a call with one argument fails on it — this is a fix by the review; the words of the breach write the parameters as `def` writes them: "a, *more, b, **rest". A built-in function is not a function of the program, and `*args` would get a tuple;
  - on each instance — a new `starlark.Dict` with the numbers of `Inputs`, inserted in order, each a `starlark.MakeInt64`, frozen, and a call on the same thread. The result is a `*starlark.Dict` whose keys are strings, the names of the results, exactly those, and whose values are `starlark.Int` within ±(2^53 − 1): `bool` and `float` are not `int`. The order of the check of the result: not a dict; the first key of the dict that names no result; the first result in the order of `results` that is absent, is not an `int` or is out of bounds;
  - a breach is one, at `#/template/solver/program`: "the solver fails before the first instance: …" or "on a = 2, b = 7, …", the numbers being `Shown`, as for `task.template-instances`. An error of the program is the words of go.starlark.net and the place of the innermost call of the program, as its backtrace ends: "solve fails: fail: no factor fits, at 5:9 in solve"; the steps — "the count of steps reaches 100000000, the budget of the template"; the clock — "module-kit's clock stops it after 60 s"; a panic — "the interpreter panicked: …". The words on each vector are in the report of T32;
  - the caller's `context` the count looks at before each combination, and the solver runs with it: `Exec` and each `Call`. Its cancellation is an error of `Instances`, without numbers and breaches (R11): "template: the task times: context canceled" before the count or in it, "…: sandbox: context deadline exceeded" in the solver. Expressions run without `context`, as in T31: their steps are bounded by their length. This is a fix by the review: at first the `context` went into each expression, and a template without expressions and a solver did not see it at all. Which error is the `context`'s is said by `sandbox.ContextDone`, one for the sandbox: the verdict of a predicate takes it too (R28) instead of its own check (a fix by the review).
- **The module check** (`module/templates.go`) — `expand` calls `Instances` with `context.Background()`, one template at a time, and an error that such a `context` does not give is a panic of a defect. A template with a solver is no longer skipped: each rule that reads the instances of a template (R32) reads its instances with the results too. `RuleTaskTemplateSolver` is in the list of rules. How the CLI checks solvers one per CPU and gives its own `context` is T37: `Check` and `CheckFile` do not change in this task.
- **The vector test.** The runner `templates` checks the `inputs` of a vector — in any order — against `Inputs`, and fails a vector without a solver that has `inputs`; the numbers of each attempt are checked against the numbers of the instance that its own run of the solver gave, and `errNoSolver` is gone. 18 lines left the skip list — 16 vectors of `task.template-solver` and 2 vectors of `templates/` with a solver —, and 166 are left in it. Before they were struck out, the test named exactly them.
- **Tests:**
  - `internal/sandbox` — `TestCheckProgram` — also that a name that the process added to `starlark.Universe` is not a name of the program; `TestProgramSteps` — a budget of 120 on a program whose top level is 10 steps and `solve(n)` 20 + 10·n: a result at a count of 110 and a stop when the count reaches 120, in one call and across several, — the same limit as `within-the-budget` and `reaches-the-budget` hold at 100,000,000, — and `ErrClosed` after `Close`; `TestProgramGlobals` — the freezing; `TestProgramClockAndCancel` — the clock between calls, the `context` and `ContextDone`; `TestProgramPanic`, `TestConcurrentExec` and `FuzzCheckProgram`, which executes nothing: `x = x + x` doubles a string without a loop or a call (Д10), so it holds the parsing, `load` and that what is accepted compiles;
  - `template` — `TestInputs`: each open and closed field; `TestSolve`: the dict in order, `int`, the bounds, `lambda` and a default value; `TestSolverBreaches`: each breach with its own words; `TestSolverStops`: the budget of a template, of an instance and of the top level, and the clock on lowered `solverLimits`, and 1300 at a count of 1290 and 1290 at that count — as `within-the-budget` and `reaches-the-budget`; `TestInstancesContext` and `TestConcurrentInstances`. `TestAttemptInstanceOfTheVectors` now holds the vectors with a solver too: the number of an instance does not depend on the solver, and the template is counted with a solver that returns 0, not with the solver of the vector, which may run up to the budget;
  - `module` — the cases of `templateRuleCases` with a solver: its own rules, a solver that fails on an instance, `task.trap-values` and `task.no-leaks` on the results, — and their words in `TestTemplateRuleMessages`.
- **`FuzzCheck`** takes a corpus without the vectors of `task.template-solver`: four of them run up to the budget, about a second under `-race`, and each run of the fuzz test checks a file twice — `Check` and `CheckFile`. A solver comes into the corpus with the example `number-thought-of-template`.
- **The measurement** — 2026-11-05, Go 1.27.3, AMD Ryzen AI 9 365, `go test`: a solver at the budget — the loop `for turn in range(n): pass` — 0.10–0.16 s per 100,000,000 steps, the template `number-thought-of` of 35 instances — 7–11 ms. Under `-race`, each package separately: `module` — 13.5 s against 9.2 s on `main`, `internal/vectors` — 9.1 s against 3.2 s; `FuzzCheck` with the vectors of the solver in the corpus ran 10 s, and without them 4.3 s against 3.6 s on `main`. The whole `ci-test` takes 23 s.

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T32 — «—», the result is set by the documents):
- the solver inside `Instances`: the format names `task.template-solver` a rule of a template, and the numbers of an instance "every parameter, every derived number and every result of the solver" (`formatv0.Numbers`), and both the module check and the package (T33) await them — one call gives them all. This is a function for one template: how many templates run at once the caller decides (R11);
- the thread is in the sandbox, not in `template`: the limits, the clock, the `context`, `recover` and the first reason are the same for the solver as for the predicate and the expressions;
- the freezing is the meaning of the language that the format refers to. Without it a solver could remember earlier instances, and the result of an instance would depend on the order not only through the steps. The format is silent about it, and there is no vector — row В29 of table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md), «в реестр spec»;
- `Inputs` — by the open view of the template as it is written, as in the second implementation, not by a list of fields: so both read one schema;
- the program is checked before the count: it does not depend on the instances, and the author sees it beside the other breaches of the template;
- one breach and a stop at the first failure: the budget and the clock are shared, and after a failure they say nothing about the following instances, and the format requires naming the instance "on which the solver fails";
- the `context` — before each combination, not in each expression: an expression of the subset is bounded by its length, so the count sees a cancellation through the combination, and a template without expressions sees it too (R11).

— *Rejected:*
- `Solve(ctx, task, instances)` apart from `Instances`: the module check and the package build would call two functions, and numbers without results are not the numbers of an instance of the format;
- not to freeze the global values, like `Program.Init`: the language requires freezing;
- to check `solve` only by a call: `def solve(*args)` would pass, although its parameter gets a tuple, not a dict;
- a breach on each instance where the solver fails: after a failure of the budget they cannot be counted, and identical failures do not help the author;
- the whole backtrace in the breach: the line is longer than the report, and the place where the program failed is the last frame of the program;
- `module.Check(ctx, …)` right now: how the CLI calls the check — the solvers one per CPU and a `context` of its own — T37 decides, and the signature of `Check` would change in the tests of T21–T31 for the sake of one `context.Background()`;
- the vectors of `task.template-solver` in the corpus of `FuzzCheck`: above; a fuzz test that executes programs — as in R28.

— *What is not closed:*
- the freezing of a solver's global values in the format text and a vector for it — В29 «в реестр spec»;
- the solvers one per CPU and the `context` of the CLI — T37;
- memory — Д10.

— *Consequence:*
- T33 takes the numbers of `Instances`, already with the results, into the `instances` of the canonical view; `api` does not execute the solver: the package carries the results ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 23 and 25);
- in the list `paths` of the release — the new `template/solver.go` and the edits of `template/*.go`, `internal/sandbox/*.go`, `module/*.go` and `verdict/verdict.go`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1. The CLI does not take them: `go list -deps ./cmd/module-kit` is without third-party modules, and Д15 does not change.

— RUN.md, T32; `template/`; `internal/sandbox/`; `module/`; `verdict/`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.4 and 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-05:* the solvers one per CPU and the `context` of the CLI, which were left here to T37, R38 decided. Now `module.Check(ctx, fsys)` expands the templates in a pool of `runtime.GOMAXPROCS(0)` goroutines and with the caller's `context`, and `modulepackage.Build` takes the numbers of the instances from the check, `module.CheckModule`, and does not run the solver a second time. Only `CheckFile` runs with `context.Background()`. — RUN.md, T37.

## The module package in code

**R34. The canonical text is written by `internal/canonical.Text` — the function of R09 over a tree of JSON values, with a refusal `*canonical.Error` at the value's place. The package is built by `modulepackage.Build(ctx, fsys)`: first `module.Check`, and a module with breaches is a `*Refusal` with the report, not a package; then the canonical view from the JSON value of each file of a part as it is, integers given as doubles — as integers, `skill_tiers` by `module.Tiers` and `instances` by `template.Instances` — with the results of its own run of the solver; the listing of the device part, the inventory and the digest. The package is the bytes of its files, `Package.Files`, and the CLI writes them to disk (T37). `modulepackage.Read(fsys, digest)` reads a package as files by the chain: the inventory by the package digest, then the parts by the inventory and the files of `device/` by the listing, and checks each text against the schema and against its canonical text. The digest of the open view of a task and of an instance is `openview.TaskDigest`, `sha256:` of the canonical text of the open view. The vectors of `packages/` are passed.**

- **The canonical text** (`internal/canonical`), by R09 and the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 25:
  - `Text(value)` — an object, an array, a string, `int64`, `bool` and `nil`; the members of an object go in the order of UTF-16 code units, `utf16.Encode`, so a character outside the BMP comes before U+E000–U+FFFF; a string — `\"`, `\\`, `\b`, `\t`, `\n`, `\f`, `\r`, the other characters up to U+001F — `\u00` and two lowercase digits, the rest as it is;
  - a refusal — `*Error{Pointer, Message}` at the first value that the text does not hold: a fractional number, an integer given as `float64` — the build converts it, before the function —, an integer outside ±(2^53 − 1), a string or a name not of UTF-8, a value of another Go type, `int` and `map[string]string` too. The place is a JSON pointer, as with the breaches of the check;
  - `Digest(bytes)` — `sha256:` and 64 lowercase digits, `Descriptor(bytes)` — the digest and the size, as types of `formatv0`;
  - the tests — `TestEscapes`, `TestEveryControlCharacter`, `TestText` — the order, nesting and a surrogate pair against U+FB01, — `TestRefusals` and `TestDigest`. `FuzzText` builds a value of the format from its input and checks `Text` against `jsontext.Value.Canonicalize`; the file is `//go:build goexperiment.jsonv2`, and with `GOEXPERIMENT=nojsonv2` the Go package builds and the tests run without it.
- **The build** (`modulepackage/build.go`, `view.go`, `device.go`):
  - the module's files — a walk of the folder without names beginning with `.` and the folders under them, and without `eval/`; a member of the view — by the layout: `manifest`, `skill_graph`, `traps`, `techniques`, `solving_steps`, and `tasks/<id>.yaml` and `lessons/<id>.yaml` — by the local ID from the file's name, which the check has already checked against `id` (`module.file-ids`);
  - the value is what `internal/yaml` gave, and `integers` turns a `float64` with an integer value within ±(2^53 − 1) into `int64`: the `5e1` of the vector `strings` is `50`. Another `float64` stays, and `Text` rejects it — on a checked module there is none, the schemas hold the numbers to integers;
  - `instances` — by the templates in the order of IDs, `template.Instances(ctx, task)` with the caller's `context`: the solver goes by a run of its own, and the test checks the results of the vector `with-solver` against it (Д44 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). A cancellation of the `context` is an error of `Build`, not a refusal;
  - the inventory — `module_format_version`, `id` and `version` from the manifest and the descriptors of the two texts; the package digest is the digest of the text of the inventory. `Package` also carries the inventory, the view and the listing, read strictly into the types of `formatv0`, — what `api` takes;
  - a breach of the rules of templates or a refusal of the text on a module that has passed the check is a defect of `module-kit`: an error with these words, and a value that the schema accepted and the type does not read is a panic, as in `module` (R25).
- **Reading** (`modulepackage/read.go`, `files.go`, `json.go`):
  - the inventory — by the package digest, it has no size; then its format version by the place that all versions keep — another version is `ErrFormatVersion` —, the schema of the inventory and the file being equal to its canonical text. The parts — by the descriptors of the inventory, with the schemas `canonical-view.schema.json` and `$defs/device_listing`; the manifest of the view names the same `module_format_version`, `id` and `version` as the inventory. Then the files of `device/` by the listing, in the order of paths;
  - the refusals — `ErrChain`: there is no file, or the digest and size are not the right ones; `ErrFormatVersion`; `ErrNotCanonical`: not JSON, something extra after the value, not by the schema, not the canonical text — `1.0` or a space —, an inventory of another module. Each — with the file's path. A file of the folder that the chain does not name is not read;
  - JSON is read by `encoding/json` with `UseNumber`: an integer is `int64`, another number `float64`, and the check against the canonical text rejects it. The check of the text makes a separate check of digits and escaping needless: a package read is byte for byte the one that `Build` built;
  - the tests — `TestRead` — a package as files is read back into the same `Package`, with extra files beside it, — `TestReadRefuses`, a case for each refusal, `TestReadUnreadable` and `TestParse`. `FuzzRead` gives three arbitrary texts, reads them by the digest of the inventory with the files of `device/` of the vector `with-media-and-notice`, and holds that `Read` does not panic, an error carries one of the three reasons, and an accepted package is exactly the files read. The corpus is the three texts of the package of each vector of `packages/`.
- **The digest of an open view** (`openview.TaskDigest`): the task — as a value of `formatv0`, its JSON value is checked against the schema of the task, the open view — the walk of the schemas `schema.Open`, as with `Task`, but before the type `formatv0/open`, so that the text has exactly what is in the view, — and `sha256:` of its canonical text. For an instance — from `template.Instance`, with the numbers substituted (R93 spec). The format has no vector of the digest: `TestTaskDigest` holds the digests of the seven tasks of `open-view/task/` and of the instance of `heads-and-legs` by seed 0, which the second implementation of `spec` computed — `openViewOf` and `canonicalText` of `tools/format-check` v0.9.2, 2026-11-05.
- **The vector test** — the runner `packages`: `package.yaml` — by the schema and the type of the vector; the package of `module/` — by `Build`; its three texts are byte for byte equal to the canonical texts `server_part`, `device_part` and `inventory` of the vector, the inventory and the digest — to the values of the vector, and the package as files is read by `Read` back into the same one. Six lines of `packages/` left `skipped.txt`, and it has 160.

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T33 — «—», the result is set by the documents):
- the check inside `Build`: a package is built only from a checked module (R45 spec), and a refusal of the function of the text then means a defect (R09), and the caller cannot build a package bypassing the check. The cost is a second parsing of the files of the parts, milliseconds;
- the canonical view — from the values of `internal/yaml`, not from the types of `formatv0`: so R09 requires — "as it is", without default values and the order of a struct's fields. The types — only for `skill_tiers`, `instances` and what `Package` gives;
- the package as bytes, not a write to disk: the library has no input or output (repositories 1 spec), and `api` does not keep packages as a folder;
- reading checks each text against its canonical text, not only the digest: the digest holds the bytes, and the schema and the canon — that these bytes are a package of the format, so the caller gets a view that it trusts, and an inventory of another module will not pass;
- `TaskDigest` — by the JSON value of the walk, not by `open.Task`: so the digest does not depend on how the type of `spec` writes empty fields, and matches the second implementation by construction.

— *Rejected:*
- `Build` without the check, by the caller's ready report: a package from an unchecked module would become possible by one error of the call;
- `Read` that checks only the digests: a file with foreign JSON and a right digest is impossible, but an inventory whose parts name a package of another module would pass;
- the digest of an open view through `json.Marshal(open.Task)` and `Text`: it would depend on the tags `omitzero` of the types of `spec`;
- a function `Write(dir)` in `modulepackage`: writing is the CLI's business (T37), and Go's `fs.FS` does not write.

— *What is not closed:*
- writing the package to a folder and the command `build` — T37; the solvers one per CPU and the `context` of the CLI — there too: `Build` so far checks the module by `module.Check`, which counts the templates with `context.Background()`, and the solvers a second time — with the caller's `context`;
- the format has no vector of the digest of an open view — a row of table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md), «в реестр spec».

— *Consequence:*
- the module index (T34) writes its text by `canonical.Text` and names the packages by `Package.Digest`; the command `open` (T40) writes `open-views.json` with the same text, and the digest of each task by `openview.TaskDigest`;
- in the list `paths` of the release — the new files `internal/canonical/*.go`, `modulepackage/*.go` and `openview/digest.go`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1. The CLI does not take them: `go list -deps ./cmd/module-kit` is still without third-party modules, and Д15 does not change. The task added no dependencies: the reference of the fuzz test is the standard library.

— RUN.md, T33; `internal/canonical/`; `modulepackage/`; `openview/`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.2 and 3.6.

## The module index

**R35. The module index is built by `moduleindex.Build(Release)`: the previous index — by its file or by an explicit `First`, the release's packages — `*modulepackage.Package`, the tag and the withdrawals by `$defs/withdrawals`. The entries of the previous index stand as they were, a package of a version that the previous index names leaves that version's entry, the others get a new one with the release's tag, and the withdrawals are marked last. The build is rejected by a `*Refusal` with all the breaches of the rules `module-index.*`, and the index's file is the canonical text `canonical.Text`. The build reads the previous index as the build writes it: by the schema of its own version, by the canonical text and with `teaches` in order, — and it does not read an index that a newer format version built. `moduleindex.Read` reads an index of any format version by the places that all versions keep, gives each entry, whatever format version it is of, and counts a version as withdrawn for any reason. `moduleindex.Newest` chooses the version of a new attempt — the newest by semver unwithdrawn one within the format's window. The parsing and the order of a module's versions are `internal/semver`, shared with T35, and the parsing of JSON is `canonical.Parse`, shared with reading a package; it rejects a text not in UTF-8 and a number outside double. The vectors of `indexes/` are passed, and the valid examples of the index hold what the schema does not say.**

- **The build** (`moduleindex/build.go`), by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 26:
  - `Release{Previous, First, Tag, Packages, Withdrawals}`. "No previous one" is `First`, and an empty `Previous` is not an index: a file that was not read will not start a new index (R16). `First` together with `Previous` is an error too;
  - the previous index — JSON; then its format version by the place that all versions keep — a version newer than that of `module-kit`, `module.NewerFormat`, is a refusal; then the schema `module-index.schema.json` of the version of `module-kit`, the file being equal to its canonical text, and the `teaches` of each entry in the order of code units, which the schema cannot say.
