Only then — the type `formatv0.ModuleIndex`. An index that did not pass is `ErrPrevious`. The build holds each entry "as it was" in an index of its own version, which passes its schema, so an index with a member or a reason that its schema does not know cannot be read, and an index of a newer format version means that the CLI in CI of `modules` was rolled back, and the build does not read it either. The format text is silent about this — В31 in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md), «в реестр spec»;
  - the packages are checked before the rules: each is not `nil`, of the format version of `module-kit`, with the module's ID and version by their forms and an entry that `$defs/entry` accepts, — as `modulepackage.Build` and `Read` give them. Otherwise — `ErrRelease`, and before any refusal: the rules are read only for a release where everything is in form. Then the packages go in the order of the module's ID, the version by semver and the digest, so neither the index nor a refusal depends on the order of the input, and the version of each is parsed once. A package of the same version with the same digest a second time changes nothing;
  - a new entry — the `module_format_version` of the inventory, `Package.Digest`, the tag and `teaches`: the anchor skills from the `teaches` of the skills of the graph of the canonical view, each once, in the order of bytes. The ID of an anchor skill is ASCII, so this is also the order of UTF-16 code units. A module without a graph and skills without anchors have no `teaches`;
  - the rules: `module-index.one-package` — two packages of the release of one version with different digests, or a package and an entry of the previous index with another digest; `module-index.version-above` — a new version below some version of its module from the previous index, withdrawn ones too, and the entry stands all the same, so that its withdrawal gives no second breach, as with `index.mjs` of `spec`; `module-index.known-withdrawals` — a withdrawal of a version that is not in the new index; `module-index.entries-kept` — a withdrawal with another reason than that of the withdrawn version. The build has no other ways to break `entries-kept`: it does not touch the entries of the previous index. A repeated withdrawal with the same reason changes nothing, and a version that comes out in this same release can be withdrawn too;
  - a refusal — `*Refusal{Breaches}`, where `Breach{Rule, Module, Version, Message}`, in the order of the module, the version, the rule and the words: "module-index.version-above: example/times-tables 0.1.1: it is new in the release, and the previous index names 0.1.10 and 0.2.0 above it: a new version stands above every published one, withdrawn ones too". A build with a breach is rejected as a whole, and does not build an index without what breaks the rule;
  - the error `ErrRelease` — a tag not of the form `vX.Y.Z`, withdrawals not by `$defs/withdrawals` — an empty object too —, a package not of the form above, and a first release without packages: an index names at least one version (`modules`, `minProperties: 1`);
  - the result — `module_format_version` `module.FormatVersion` and the entries, as canonical text. Its bytes the CLI writes to `module-index.json` (T38).
- **Reading** (`moduleindex/read.go`), by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 2 and 26:
  - `Read(data)` takes from the root only `module_format_version` and `modules`, and from an entry — `module_format_version`, `package_digest`, `release`, `teaches` and `withdrawn`; a member at another place, which the next version may add, is not read. `withdrawn` is any string, and the version is withdrawn whatever the reason: `Withdrawn` carries it as written. The other members are checked by the index's schema by their forms: the forms and meanings of these places are also the same in all versions, and a member not in its form is `ErrNotIndex`;
  - an entry of a format version that `module-kit` does not read, `Read` gives together with the others: version comparison needs each published version of the module ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27), and it is the choice of a version, `Newest`, that "skips" it. The index is not rejected because of it;
  - `Read` does not require the canonical text: it is part of the format version, and the next major may change it. A name that stands in an object twice — no build writes it — is taken by the last member, as `encoding/json` reads it;
  - `Newest(index, id)` — the newest by semver version of the module among those that are not withdrawn, for any reason, and whose format version is within the window, `module.ReadsFormat`. The same one `api` gives a new attempt (architecture 3.3 api), by it the module teaches the anchor skills of its entry, and `open` takes it: how to combine there the index of the release with the marks of the newest index, T40 decides.
- **Shared code:**
  - `internal/semver` — `Parse` of a module version by the form `common.schema.json#/$defs/module_version` and `Version.Compare` by rule 11 of Semantic Versioning 2.0.0. The numbers have no bound, as in the format: they are held as text and compared first by length, so 1.10.0 is above 1.9.0 and no version overflows. The Go package is started here, not in T35, although [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6, requires a shared package to be started by the second task: so T34 itself requires — «разбор и сравнение semver — общие с T35», — and T35 comes next, and it would have no reason to move the code;
  - `module.NewerFormat(v)` — whether the format version MAJOR.MINOR is newer than `module.FormatVersion`, by the values of the numbers: 0.10 is newer than 0.9. It stands beside `ReadsFormat`, at the window of versions, and compares the version MAJOR.MINOR as the module version MAJOR.MINOR.0: the forms of their numbers are the same;
  - `canonical.Parse` — the former `parse` of `modulepackage/json.go` (R34), moved to `internal/canonical/json.go`: parsing JSON with integers as `int64` was needed a second time, and the Go package whose tree of values it builds took it. `modulepackage` calls it, and its test is `TestParse` in `internal/canonical`. Beyond what it did before, it rejects a text not in UTF-8, which `encoding/json` would read with U+FFFD in place of each bad byte (RFC 8259 requires a JSON text to be in UTF-8), — so `Read` will not accept a withdrawal reason with broken bytes, — and a number outside double: `1e700` the former parsing gave as `+Inf`, and the schema validator panicked on it. So `modulepackage.Read` too had crashed since T33 — on an `inventory.json` such as `{"module_format_version":"0.3","id":1e700}`, — and it was found by `FuzzBuildPrevious` of this task: `FuzzRead` of T33 in 1.7 million runs did not reach such an input. The input lies in `moduleindex/testdata/fuzz/`, and `TestReadRefuses` of `modulepackage` holds the case of the inventory.
- **Tests:**
  - `moduleindex`: `TestRulesAreTheFormats` — `Rules` are equal to the list of refusals of `index-vector.schema.json`; `TestBuildFirst`, `TestBuildNext` — the previous entries as they were, an entry of version 0.1, a repeated package, a withdrawal of a new version and a repeated withdrawal; `TestBuildRefuses` — each rule with its words and several breaches in order; `TestBuildErrors` — a case for each error, a previous index of version 0.10, `teaches` out of order and a package not in form beside a breach of a rule; `TestTeachesOf`; `TestReadExamples` — the valid examples of the index are read as by the schema and the type, the invalid ones are `ErrNotIndex`, except `unknown-reason`, which `Read` reads as a withdrawal; `TestReadLaterVersion` — a member and a reason of the next version, an entry of version 1.4, a text that is not canonical; `TestReadRefuses`; `TestNewest`. In `module` — `TestNewerFormat`, in `internal/semver` — `TestParse`, `TestParseIsTheSchemas`, `TestCompare` and `FuzzCompare` against `math/big`;
  - the fuzz tests: `FuzzRead` holds that `Read` does not panic, an error is `ErrNotIndex`, the canonical text of an accepted index is read into the same index, and `Newest` takes an unwithdrawn version of the window no lower than the others; `FuzzBuildPrevious` gives an arbitrary previous index to the release of the vector `next-release` and holds that the build does not panic, gives an index, a refusal or `ErrPrevious`, and a built index holds each entry of the previous one as it was. The corpus of both is the indexes of the vectors of `indexes/` and the examples of the index;
  - the vector test — the runner `indexes`: `index.yaml` — by the schema and the type of the vector; the package of each module of `modules/` — by `modulepackage.Build`; the previous index — by its canonical text, as the release of `modules` holds it; the built index is byte for byte equal to the canonical text of the vector's `index`, and a refusal — only by the rule of `refusal`. The previous and the built indexes `Read` reads back into the values of the vector. Eight lines of `indexes/` left `skipped.txt`, and it has 152 — the vectors of `versions/`;
  - the valid examples of the index the runner `examples/valid` also holds to what the schema cannot say, as `indexFormProblems` of `format-check` of `spec` does: the root names the version of `format/VERSION`, and the `teaches` of each entry is in order (`indexForm`, `TestIndexForm`). T22 promised this («Открыто»).

— *Why* (the executor's decisions: «План» of T34 — «—», the result is set by the documents; what the review changed — RUN.md, T34, «Сделано»):
- an explicit `First`, not `nil` in place of the previous index: `os.ReadFile` of an empty file gives an empty slice, not a `nil` one, and an empty file read by mistake would start a new index silently — what the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 26, and R16 forbid;
- the previous index — as the build writes it, not by places: the build writes an index of its own version, which its schema accepts, and the entries in it must stand "as they were", byte for byte. The previous index is signed, and `module-kit` built it, so in fact it is the canonical text with `teaches` in order; anything else would mean that something is wrong in the chain;
- a previous index of a newer format version is a refusal, even without new members: a release whose CLI is older than the CLI of the previous release is a rollback, and it must fail loudly, not rewrite the root of the index to the old version;
- the packages — by form before the rules: otherwise a package not in form beside a breach of a rule would give a refusal by the rule, and the author would not learn that the package was not built by `modulepackage`;
- `Read` — by places and without the canonical text: so the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2, requires, and `api`, `diff` and `open` read indexes that other versions built too;
- `Read` gives the entries of other format versions too, and `Newest` skips them: `diff` needs each published version of the module and its format version ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5), and not rejecting the index because of them the text requires of any reader;
- a refusal with all the breaches at once, in the order of the module and the version: CI of `modules` shows the author everything in one run, and the order does not depend on the order in which the CLI read the package folders;
- parsing versions without `math/big`: the length and the order of digits give the order of values when there are no leading zeros, and the form forbids them.

— *Rejected:*
- `Build(previous []byte, …)` with `nil` in place of "no previous one": see "Why";
- to read the previous index by places and silently drop the members that the schema does not know: an entry would become other than it was, and `entries-kept` would not notice it;
- to accept a previous index of a newer format version when the schema lets it pass, and write the root with its own version, as `index.mjs` of `spec` does: a rollback of the CLI would pass silently;
- `Read` that drops the entries of format versions outside the window: `diff` would have to read the index once more, with code of its own;
- `Read` that requires the canonical text: an index of the next major version with another canonical text would not be read, although its places are the same;
- comparing versions through `math/big`, like `BigInt` in `versions.mjs` of `spec`: for the sake of the comparison the numbers would have to be parsed, while the length and the digits give the order;
- to leave `parse` in `modulepackage` and write a second one in `moduleindex`: two parsers of JSON with one job would diverge.

— *What is not closed:*
- В31 in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md), «в реестр spec»: what the index build does with a previous index that a newer format version built or whose form its schema does not accept;
- reading `withdrawals.yaml`, the command `index` and its flags — T38; combining the index of the release with the marks of the newest index for `open` — T40.

— *Consequence:*
- `moduleversion` (T35, T36) takes `internal/semver` and `moduleindex.Read`: all the versions of the module and their format versions;
- in the list `paths` of the release — the new `moduleindex/*.go`, `internal/semver/*.go`, `internal/canonical/json.go` and the edit of `module/check.go`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1. The CLI does not take them: `go list -deps ./cmd/module-kit` is still without third-party modules, and Д15 does not change. The task added no dependencies.

— RUN.md, T34; `moduleindex/`; `internal/semver/`; `internal/canonical/json.go`; `module/check.go`; `modulepackage/files.go`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.2 and 3.6.

## Version comparison in code

**R36. A new version of a module is compared with the published ones by `moduleversion.Compare(next, published)`: the published ones — packages as files without `device/` with their digests, and each is read by `modulepackage.ReadParts` by the chain of digests; the newest — by semver, withdrawn ones too. `Changes` finds the version part of two packages of one format version by the table of the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27, and gives a report by places: each row of the table — at its own exact place, and everything else — a patch at each place where the views diverged. `Steps`, `StepPart` and `SameMajor` hold the rule of 0.y.z. The vectors of `versions/` with a part within one format version — 119 — are passed; 30 refusals and three format transitions wait for T36.**

- **What is decided** (the author's decisions of 2026-11-05 in the planning of T35 — RUN.md, T35, «Решено автором»):
  - the split of the vectors of `versions/`: T35 — 119 cases with `version_part`, where all the published versions are modules of format 0.3; T36 — 30 refusals and three format transitions with a part: `format-transition`, `format-transition-zero-y` and `earlier-format-older-version`;
  - the report — by places: a table row — at the place that it names, an answer slot — `answer_slots/<j>` or `line/<k>` in the item, a change of an instance — at the place in the task, with the numbers of its parameters; everything else, except `manifest.version`, — a patch at each place where the JSON values of `server.json` and `device.json` diverge, by members and elements, and the `instances` of a template — as a whole. A difference at the place of a table row or under it, by tokens, is not written, and under `skill_tiers` and `instances/<ID>` — when the skill graph or the task itself diverged: they follow them. The order — by part from major, then by place. `Comparison.String()` writes the report as lines, like the breaches of the module check, and `--json` — the same `Comparison` with JSON tags; T38 prints it ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5);
  - the input — packages as files with digests already in T35: the item of T36 «опубликованные пакеты сверены по цепочке дайджестов» moved here, and T36 adds only reading a package of 0.2 by places and the rules `module-version.*`;
  - the executor's decisions: a check is compared by meaning with the same code by which the verdict reads it, — `comparedBy` and `unitsOf` moved from `verdict` to `internal/normal` as `Compared` and `Units`, and the public API of `verdict` did not change; the order of places — `schema.ComparePointers`, moved from `module`; raising a version — `semver.Version.Raise`; `SameMajor` — for `api`, which moves a learner within the same major (architecture 3.5 spec).

— *Why:*
- the code repeats the meaning of `foundPartOf` of the second implementation of `spec`, `versions.mjs`, which holds all the vectors; the report is more exact: in `versions.mjs` everything beyond the table rows is one line of patch `#`, and the author would not see what gave the patch. The parts do not change from this: each table row is no lower than patch;
- `skill_tiers` and `instances` stay in the comparison: `Read` does not recompute them, and a package of another builder may diverge only in them — then it is a patch, as in `versions.mjs`. Only a difference that follows another one is not written;
- packages as files — at once: CI of `modules` gives `diff` the packages without `device/` ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5), and the function's input does not change any more with T36;
- `Compared` — in an internal Go package, not in the API of `verdict`: the meaning of a check is read by the verdict and the comparison, and `api` does not need it.

— *Rejected:*
- the split 122/30 — the format transition then in T35 — and 129/23 — `module-version.above` and `step` then in T35: the bodies of T35 and T36 already split it so, and the rules are better read together;
- one line of patch, as in `versions.mjs`: see "Why";
- the input — `*modulepackage.Package` in memory: T36 would change the input and the tests;
- to exclude `skill_tiers` and `instances` from the comparison: a package of another builder that diverged only in them would get "none" where `versions.mjs` gives patch;
- a second `comparedBy` in `moduleversion`: two codes of one meaning would diverge.

— *What is not closed:* the rules `module-version.*`, a package of 0.2 by the places that the format keeps in all versions, and 33 vectors — T36; the command `diff` — T38.

— *Consequence:* in the list `paths` of the release — the new `moduleversion/*.go`, `internal/normal/compared.go` and the edits of `internal/semver`, `internal/schema`, `modulepackage/read.go`, `verdict` and `module`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1. The CLI does not take them: `go list -deps ./cmd/module-kit` is still without third-party modules, and Д15 does not change. The task added no dependencies.

— RUN.md, T35; `moduleversion/`; `internal/semver/`; `internal/normal/compared.go`; `internal/schema/errors.go`; `modulepackage/read.go`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 3, 3.2, 3.5 and 3.6.

## The rules of versions and the format transition

**R37. The rules `module-version.*` are held by `moduleversion.Compare`: each breach is a `Breach{Rule, Message}` in `Comparison.Breaches`, and `Refused()` says that the new version is rejected. `above` — for each published version, withdrawn ones too; `step` — for the newest, only when `above` let it pass; the other six — by the places that the format keeps in all versions, for each published one, so they hold across a format transition too. A published package of an earlier format version is read by the chain of digests and only by these places: the part is major while the format is v0. All the vectors of `versions/` are passed, and the skip list is empty.**

- **What is decided** (the executor's decisions: T36 has no «Решить при планировании», the result is set by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 2 and 27, and by the vectors):
  - the places (`moduleversion/places.go`) — the format version, the module's ID and version from the inventory; the languages of the manifest, the IDs of the bands in order, the keys of skills, tasks, lessons, traps and techniques, and `retired_skills` with `replaced_by` from the canonical view. They are read by one function over JSON values, both for a package of version 0.3 and for an earlier one: the rules see the packages the same way. A package without a place where the format keeps it is `ErrNotCanonical`;
  - reading a published package (`moduleversion/published.go`): `inventory.json` by the package digest and the format version by the place that all versions keep. A version that `module-kit` reads — `modulepackage.ReadParts`, as since T35. An earlier one — `server.json` by the digest and size that the inventory gives, without the schema and without the canonical text: they are set by the version of the package, not of the reader. A newer one and one not of the form MAJOR.MINOR — `ErrFormatVersion`;
  - the part across a transition — `Major` by one change `inventory.json #/module_format_version: major: the module moves from version 0.2 of the format to 0.3`. The table does not compare packages of different format versions: so does `versions.mjs` too (`movePart`). From v1 on, the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27, gives no part of a transition, and there is no question yet: the window of reading is the one version 0.3, and earlier ones are read only by places;
  - the order of breaches — `above`, `step`, then `retired-complete`, `retired-kept`, `retired-known`, `ids-kept`, `languages-kept` and `bands-kept`; the words — as in `placeBreaches` and `compareVersions` of `versions.mjs`. The report writes each as a line `refused: <ID>: <words>` before the changes, and `--json` as `breaches`; the exit code 1 by them `diff` will give, T38;
  - the vector test: a refusal of a vector — exactly one rule, its own; a vector with a part — not a single breach and the same part.

— *Why:*
- the format text requires reading a package of an earlier version "by their places alone, not by the schema of its own version" (section 2), so the schema of 0.3 is not applied to a package of 0.2, and the digests of the chain are;
- the six rules by places hold across a transition — [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27: "the rules hold through those places alone"; a shared function of places rules out that a rule sees a package of 0.3 otherwise than one of 0.2;
- `step` only without `above`: so the table of rules requires ("applies to a new version that `module-version.above` lets pass"), and the vector `below-the-newest` would otherwise breach two rules.

— *Rejected:*
- a refusal of a package of 0.2 by an error, as in T35: the format promises to read it, and the three vectors of a transition with a part expect this;
- to read a package of 0.2 through `modulepackage`: that one checks the schema and the canonical text of its own version, while the places are all that is promised;
- a breach of `step` together with `above`: a refusal vector would breach two rules, and the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 9, requires one.

— *What is not closed:* the command `diff`, its exit code and the printing of `Breaches` — T38.

— *Consequence:* in the list `paths` of the release — the new `moduleversion/places.go`, `published.go` and `rules.go` and the edit of `compare.go`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1. There are no dependencies, `go list -deps ./cmd/module-kit` is still without third-party modules. The skip list is empty: the first release of the line 0.2 (T39) already meets this condition on the side of the vectors.

— RUN.md, T36; `moduleversion/`; `internal/vectors/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 3, 3.5 and 3.6.

## The commands `check` and `build`

**R38. The CLI gets the commands `check <module>` and `build <module> <folder>`. Their exit code is shared: 0 — accepted, 1 — a refusal by the format rules, 2 — the command could not do its work. The report is text to stdout, a line per breach and a line of the summary, and there is no `--json`. The template solvers run one per CPU inside `module.Check(ctx, fsys)`, and `build` runs each solver once. The package is written to a temporary folder beside the target one and moved to its place. The release carries the `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` of each third-party module that the binary is built from, and `ci-lint` checks their list. The warning of a picture with transparent pixels is split off into T41.**

- **What is decided** (the author's decisions of 2026-11-05 in the planning of T37, by the plan's recommendations):
  - **the split** (rule 11 of RUN.md): the warning of a transparent picture is a separate task, T41, right after T37. With it come a library of its own for reading WebP, test pictures of its own and a fuzz test, and this is one more check;
  - **what to read transparency with** is decided, but T41 carries it out: PNG — `image/png` of the standard library, WebP — `golang.org/x/image/webp` v0.47.0, BSD-3-Clause, the newest version at the Go proxy on 2026-11-05 (of 2026-10-09). Its `go.mod` requires `golang.org/x/sys` v0.49.0 and `golang.org/x/text` v0.43.0 — the versions that are already in place. The decoder reads `ALPH` and VP8L, and rejects an animated WebP with the error `webp: invalid format` (`webp/decode.go` v0.47.0, read 2026-11-05). JPEG has no transparency;
  - **where the warning is**: a separate public Go package, which the CLI calls and from stage 4 `module-registry`, not `module.Report`: so `api`, which takes `modulepackage`, and with it `module`, will not carry `x/image`. A picture that cannot be read is a warning "could not check", not code 2;
  - **the report**: a breach — as the line `Breach.String()` with the path from the folder that the command line gave, — `<path>[:line:column] [#/pointer]: <rule ID>: <message>`; a breach of a schema — `schema <keyword>`, a file that is not YAML — `not YAML`. The last line is the summary: "the module breaks no rule of the format 0.3" or "the format 0.3 refuses the module: N breaches". The report goes to stdout, an error of the call and of input or output — to stderr, as one line `module-kit <command>: …`;
  - **the solvers one per CPU**: `module.Check(ctx, fsys)` expands the templates of the module in a pool of `runtime.GOMAXPROCS(0)` goroutines. Each takes the next template in the order of the paths, and the result takes its place by the task's path, so the report does not depend on the number of goroutines and the order in which they end. A cancellation of `ctx` is an error without a report, and templates that have not started yet do not run. `module.CheckModule(ctx, fsys)` is the same `Check`, and in addition the numbers of the instances of each template by the task's ID for an accepted module; `modulepackage.Build` takes them in place of a second run of `template.Instances`. `CheckFile` — a file on its own — runs with `context.Background()`, as before. The CLI gives a `context` that SIGINT or SIGTERM makes done;
  - **the folder of `build`**: it does not exist or is empty, or else code 2 and nothing is touched. The package is written to the folder `package` inside a temporary `.<name>.<random>` beside it and moved into the target one, and the temporary folder is removed whatever the outcome. The permissions of folders are 0755, of files 0644, as the process's umask adjusts them. The temporary folder of `os.MkdirTemp`, accessible only to its owner, the command does not turn into a shared one: so it was in the first variant, and SonarCloud marked `os.Chmod(…, 0o755)` with the rule `go:S2612`. This finding is closed by the code, not by a mark "false". A refusal by the rules is code 1, the report and nothing on disk; a path of a file of the package outside its folder is an error;
  - **`--version`** names `verdict.UnicodeVersion` on a second line: the binary now carries `x/text`, and the condition of R27 about `unicode.Version` is no longer needed;
  - **the licenses in the release** (Д15): the recipe `release-licenses` in the justfile collects `go list -deps` of the binary on the six platforms of the release and checks the list of modules against `THIRD_PARTY` and `SAME_LICENSE`. The modules as of 2026-11-05: `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` — `LICENSE-go-yaml` and `NOTICE-go-yaml`, `github.com/santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema/v6` — `LICENSE-jsonschema`, `go.starlark.net` — `LICENSE-starlark`, `golang.org/x/text` — `LICENSE-x-text`, `golang.org/x/sys` — `LICENSE-x-sys`; it does not go into the binary for Windows, and the file goes with all of them. The `LICENSE` of `github.com/altnovatrail/spec` is byte for byte our Apache-2.0, and the `LICENSE` of the release replaces it; the recipe checks this too. `release-files` puts the files into `dist/` and into `SHA256SUMS`, and `ci-lint` calls the recipe: a module without its license fails the pull request, not the release.

— *Why:*
- the solvers one per CPU are required by architecture 3.4 and R33: the clock runs by wall time, so there are no more concurrent evaluations than `GOMAXPROCS`. The pool is in the library, not in the CLI: otherwise the CLI would have to expand the templates itself, and `Check` gives no API for this, and `module-registry` needs the same pool from stage 4. Changing the signature of `Check` is free now: nobody takes the line 0.1 (R16);
- a second run of the solver in `Build` would cost one more `within-the-budget` per template — up to 60 s for the worst solver, — and the check has already computed the numbers of the instances;
- the text report is read by CI of `modules` and the author; nobody has asked for `--json` yet, and adding a flag is an extension that breaks nothing;
- a temporary folder and a rename will not leave half a package if the writing breaks off, and the command does not touch a non-empty folder, because someone else's files may lie in it;
- the list of licenses in the justfile, checked against `go list -deps`, catches a new dependency in its own pull request: Д15 piled up since T20, because no check held it.

— *Rejected:*
- the warning of a picture in T37: the task would come out larger than one check;
- a parsing of WebP of its own: `ALPH` with compression 1 and VP8L require a VP8L decoder, and a copy of its own is more code and risk; WebP without a check: the format requires warning of each picture;
- the warning in `module.Report`: `x/image` would go into the binary of `api`; only in `cmd/module-kit`: `module-registry` would not get it;
- the pool only in the CLI: the library has no API to expand templates from outside;
- overwriting a non-empty folder: it would erase what lay there;
- `LICENSE-spec` beside `LICENSE`: the same text twice. When the `LICENSE` of `spec` changes, the recipe will fail, and it will be entered into `THIRD_PARTY`.

— *What is not closed:* the warning of a transparent picture — T41; running the binary on each OS — Д6, T38; that the shared release and `release-verify` accept the new file names — a probe build of `release-files` showed the files and the sums, `release-verify` does not restrict names (`spec/tools/release/verify.mjs`: a name is only `path.basename`), and the next task will check the real release.

— *Consequence:* in the list `paths` of the release — `cmd/module-kit/*.go`, `module/*.go`, `modulepackage/*.go` and `justfile`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1, and it will be the first to carry third-party modules and their licenses. Д15 is closed. The amendment to R33 — below, in its entry.

— RUN.md, T37 and T41; `cmd/module-kit/`; `module/check.go`, `module/templates.go`, `module/rules.go`; `modulepackage/build.go`, `modulepackage/view.go`; justfile; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 3, 3.1, 3.4 and 3.6.

## The warning of a transparent picture

**R39. The new Go package `picture` warns of a picture of the module in which even one pixel is not fully opaque: `Warnings(ctx, fsys)` reads each PNG and WebP in `media/`, and `Check(name, data)` one picture. PNG is decoded by `image/png`, WebP by `golang.org/x/image/webp` v0.47.0, BSD-3-Clause. A picture larger than `MaxPixels`, 2^25 pixels, the Go package does not decode, but warns that it did not check it; the same for a picture that cannot be read. `check` prints the warnings as lines `<path>: warning: …` after the breaches and before the summary, and the exit code does not change from them.**

- **What is decided** (in the planning of T37 — R38 — and in T41, the executor's decisions by its text):
  - **the Go package** — `picture`, outside `module`: `go list -deps` of the Go packages `module`, `modulepackage`, `moduleindex`, `moduleversion`, `observation`, `openview`, `verdict`, `leak` and `template` does not name `x/image`, so `api` does not carry the decoder. It is called by `check` of the CLI, and from stage 4 by `module-registry`;
  - **what is transparent** — a pixel with an alpha less than 0xFFFF, the first by rows. The message names its place and the alpha out of 65535 and repeats why the format needs a light opaque background. A picture that itself knows that it is opaque — `Opaque()` of the types of `image` — is checked at once. A PNG with an alpha channel where all the pixels are opaque, and a palette with `tRNS` whose transparent entry no pixel takes, get no warning;
  - **the limit** — `MaxPixels = 1 << 25`, 33,554,432 pixels, for example 5792 × 5792. A decoded pixel is no more than 8 bytes (`image.NRGBA64`), so the picture will take no more than 256 MiB. The size is read by `DecodeConfig` — the header, without decoding —, and a file whose header promises more is not decoded: so a picture bomb will not eat the memory. The pictures of the examples and vectors of `spec` v0.9.2 are 1 × 1, so the limit is chosen by memory, not by them: a picture that is shown in a task statement with a width in CSS px is much smaller;
  - **an unreadable picture** — `DecodeConfig` or `Decode` give an error, as for an animated WebP, which `x/image` v0.47.0 does not decode, — this is a warning "module-kit cannot read / decode it as …, so it could not check", not code 2: `module.media` checks only the signature, and the module is accepted. A file without the signature of its format gets no warning: `module.media` rejects it, and the report would say one thing twice;
  - **what is read** — the files of `media/` with the extension `.png` and `.webp`, in the order of paths; files and folders with a name beginning with "." — not of the module ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10) — are skipped. JPEG holds no transparency, MP3 is not a picture. A module without `media/` has no warnings; a folder that cannot be read and a done `ctx` `Warnings` gives as an error — code 2 of the CLI;
  - **the tests** — the PNG files the test makes itself with `image/png`: opaque, with an alpha channel but opaque, with one transparent pixel, semi-transparent, a palette with `tRNS` — with the transparent entry in use and without —, gray. The WebP files the test builds by RFC 9649 (November 2024), without files in `testdata/`: VP8L of one color — one simple prefix code per channel, — a simple file and an extended one with VP8X, opaque and transparent, and an extended one with the animation flag, ANIM and ANMF. `TestTestFiles` holds that `x/image` decodes them as the tests think. `TestFormatPictures` — none of the 11 pictures of the examples and vectors of `spec`, except the vectors of `module.media`, is transparent, and each is read. `FuzzCheck` (R33 spec) checks `Check` against the decoder with the limit lowered to 2^16: 60 s, about 23 million runs, without findings.

— *Why:*
- the format requires warning of a picture "with a pixel that is not fully opaque" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14), and in WebP transparency can be in `ALPH` with compression 1 and in VP8L — without a VP8L decoder it cannot be seen (R38);
- the limit is needed because anyone writes a picture, and `DecodeConfig` gives the size cheaply; 256 MiB is a ceiling that the CLI bears on the author's machine and in CI;
- an unreadable picture is also something the author needs to know of: a browser may not show it either. But the format does not refuse for it, so it does not change the exit code either;
- WebP files that the test builds by the specification are repeatable and carry no foreign files with their licenses; the test files of `x/image` would do — `gopher-doc.with-alpha.lossless.webp` and `yellow_rose.lossy-with-alpha.webp` are transparent, — but the module does not record their provenance.

— *Rejected:*
- the warning at the first opaque place of each part of the module, by the references `picture`: the format speaks of each picture of the module, and a file without a reference to it is no more the concern of `module-kit` than a file with a reference;
- to count as transparent a pixel with an alpha less than 0xFF00 — a tolerance for rounding: the format says "not fully opaque", and a semi-transparent shadow on dark paper is seen just as badly;
- to decode a picture of any size: the memory is that of the caller's process, and Go has no limit for `image`;
- to warn of a file without a signature: `module.media` already rejects it.

— *Consequence:* `golang.org/x/image` v0.47.0 is a direct dependency in `go.mod`, raised by Dependabot `gomod`; it requires `x/sys` and `x/text` of the versions that were already in place. In `THIRD_PARTY` of the justfile — `golang.org/x/image=x-image`, and the release carries `LICENSE-x-image`. The list `paths` of the release takes `picture/*.go` by `*.go`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1.

— RUN.md, T41; `picture/`; `cmd/module-kit/main.go`, `cmd/module-kit/build.go`; `go.mod`, `go.sum`; justfile; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 3, 3.1 and 3.6.

## The commands `index` and `diff`

**R40. The CLI has the commands `index` and `diff` over the finished library. `index` builds the index of a release of `modules` from the previous index or an explicit `--first`, from the package folders, the tag and the withdrawals as a file or an explicit `--no-withdrawals`, and writes `--out`, which does not exist yet. `diff` compares a new version — a module's folder or a package — with each published version that `--index` names, by their packages from `--published`, or is told `--first` explicitly. Running the binaries on each OS (Д6) is a separate task, T42.**

- **What is decided** (in the planning of T38. The author did not answer the question about the decisions, and the executor took the plan's recommendations — as the report says; the author may reverse any of them):
  - **the split** (rule 11 of RUN.md): T38 — the commands and their tests on the vectors, T42 — a CI job on six runners with the author's step in the ruleset. T39 waits for both;
  - **the call of `index`** — `module-kit index (--previous <module-index.json> | --first) --tag vX.Y.Z (--withdrawals <withdrawals.yaml> | --no-withdrawals) --out <file> <package folder>...`. Exactly one flag of each pair. An empty `--previous`, a flag twice or neither of a pair — code 2: a recipe of `modules` to which the shared release did not give the previous index fails, and does not start a new one ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 26; the 2026-11-02 amendment to R63 spec). The recipe `release-files VERSION PREVIOUS` of `modules` needs only `if [ -z "$PREVIOUS" ]` and a check of whether `withdrawals.yaml` exists;
  - **the inputs of `index`** — the package folders that `build` wrote: each is read by `modulepackage.Read` by the chain from the digest of the bytes of its `inventory.json`, with `device/`. The withdrawals — YAML by the rules of section 4, then `$defs/withdrawals` of the index's schema. An empty file of withdrawals is not withdrawals, but a breach of form: "no withdrawals" is said by a flag;
  - **the output of `index`** — `--out`, which does not exist yet: the file is written to a temporary folder beside it and moved to its place, as a package by `build` (R38). To stdout — a line with the tag and the number of packages;
  - **the codes of `index`** — 0: the index is written; 1: `*moduleindex.Refusal`, a line `<rule ID>: <module> <version>: <words>` per breach and a line of the summary; 2: `ErrPrevious` — including an index of a newer format version (В31) —, `ErrRelease`, withdrawals not in their form, a folder that is not a package, input or output;
  - **the call of `diff`** — as in architecture 3.5: `module-kit diff [--json] (--index <file> | --first) [--published <package folder>]... <module>`. A module that the index does not name is the first version, code 0. `--first` — when there is no index at all yet. The package of each version of the module from the index, withdrawn ones too, is looked for by the `package_digest` of the entry among `--published`; without it — code 2 with the version and the digest. Extra folders are skipped;
  - **the new version in `diff`** — a folder with `inventory.json` at the root is a package, `modulepackage.ReadParts` by its digest. With `manifest.yaml` — a module, `module.CheckModule` and `modulepackage.Build`, the solvers one per CPU (R38). Neither — code 2. A module that the check rejected — the report as from `check` and code 1, without a comparison;
  - **the report of `diff`** — `Comparison.String()`: the lines `refused: <ID>: <words>`, as the library writes them (R37). The line `<ID>: <path>: <message>` in architecture 3.5 is corrected. With `--json` — `Comparison` through `encoding/json`, and for a module that the check rejected — `{"check": [breaches]}`, with the path from the folder of the command line;
  - **the flags** — a `flag.FlagSet` of the standard library per command, without the output of the Go package itself: a usage of its own, types of its own for a flag given or not, and for a repeated one.

— *Why:*
- the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 26, and R63 spec require that the build be told "no previous one" explicitly, and that an unreadable previous index give a refusal, not a new index; a flag for each "no" makes a missing file an error of the call;
- packages, not modules: the recipe of `modules` builds them anyway — for the archives `sha256-<hex>.tar.gz` (architecture 3.3 `modules`), — and the solvers do not run a second time;
- "not in the index — the first version" is needed by the first release of a new module in a repository where others are already published. That the index is of the newest release is held by CI of `modules`, which checks it with `release-verify`. A version below the published ones is also caught by `module-index.version-above` at the index build;
- the split: "Binaries" is a check that the author puts into the ruleset (rule 4), and CI on six OSes is a check of another nature than the commands on the vectors.

— *Rejected:*
- an empty `--previous` as "no previous one" — so a missing file would silently start a new index;
- module folders at the input of `index` — the solvers would run a second time, and the recipe needs the packages anyway;
- the index to stdout — on a failure half a file would be left in `dist/`;
- to require `--first` for a module that is not in the index too — it breaks the first release of a module beside published ones;
- the text report of `check` with `--json` too — CI that reads JSON would stumble on the parsing;
- a third-party library of flags — a new license in the release for the sake of two commands;
- Д6 in the same task — two checks of different nature.

— *What is not closed:* the form of the call for the recipe of `modules` — В32 «в реестр spec» in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md); running the binaries on each OS — T42.

— *Consequence:* the list `paths` of the release takes `cmd/module-kit/*.go`, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1. There are no dependencies. Д6 is left to T42.

— RUN.md, T38 and T42; `cmd/module-kit/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 3, 3.5 and 3.6.


## Binaries on each OS

**R41. Each of the six binaries of the release is run in CI on a runner of its own OS, on each pull request and push to `main`. "Binaries / build" builds them with the recipe `release-files`, as the release does, and runs the linux/amd64 binary — its result becomes the reference. "Binaries / \<platform>" on six standard GitHub runners runs `.github/binaries.sh`: the sum by `SHA256SUMS`, `--version`, `check`, `build`, `index` and `diff` on samples of the format and a byte-for-byte check of what was built against the reference. "Binaries" waits for both and stands in the ruleset `main`. Д6 is closed.**

- **What is decided** (in the planning of T38, R40, and in T42 by its text):
  - **the runners** — `ubuntu-24.04` (linux/amd64), `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, `macos-15-intel` (darwin/amd64), `macos-15` (darwin/arm64, M1), `windows-2025` (windows/amd64) and `windows-11-arm`. The labels — by the table of standard runners for public repositories (GitHub Docs, "GitHub-hosted runners reference", `data/reusables/actions/supported-github-runners.md` in `github/docs`, read 2026-11-05);
  - **the build** — in the stage `toolchain`, as in the shared `go.yml`: `docker run` as the runner's user, `HOME=/tmp`, `just release-files v0.0.0-ci` and `just binaries-samples`. The recipe `binaries-samples` copies the samples from the Go module `spec` of the version in `go.mod` — CI does not take the submodule (R20) — and makes the copies writable: the Go module cache holds them read-only;
  - **what the run checks** (`.github/binaries.sh`, bash: Git Bash on Windows, `shasum -a 256` on macOS):
    - the binary's sum is the one in `SHA256SUMS`;
    - `--version` — two lines, `module-kit v…` and `Unicode …`;
    - `check` of the example `with-templates` — code 0: the Starlark sandbox and the solver, and on linux and darwin the mmap of `x/sys`, by which go.starlark.net holds integers;
    - `check` of the vector `task.known-band/unknown-band` — code 1 and the rule's ID in the report;
    - `build` of the vector `packages/with-solver` — its digest: the canonical text and the paths on Windows;
    - `build` of the modules of the vector `indexes/first-release`, `index --first` over their packages and `diff` of one of them — code 0;
    - the files of the built package by sha256 and the index are byte for byte the same as those of the linux/amd64 reference: the package and the index depend only on the module's files ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 25 and 26);
  - **one required check** — the job "Binaries" with `if: always()`, which fails if the build or even one run is not `success`. It alone stands in the ruleset, not the six names of the matrix, which would change with the labels of the runners;
  - **the permissions** — `contents: read` for the whole `ci.yml`, `persist-credentials: false`. The matrix takes only the script from the repository, `sparse-checkout`. The artifact lives 3 days;
  - **the actions** — `actions/checkout` v7.0.1 (`3d3c42e`), `actions/upload-artifact` v7.0.2 (`cf430e0`) and `actions/download-artifact` v8.0.2 (`9000827`), as in `ci.yml` of `spec`: the latest releases as of 2026-11-05 (`repos/actions/*/releases/latest`). actionlint v1.7.12 checked `ci.yml` without findings.

— *Why:*
- Д6: of the six binaries only linux/amd64 was run, and since T37 the binary carries go.starlark.net with `x/sys`, which has code of its own on each OS, and writes files by the paths of the OS;
- the byte-for-byte check against the reference catches what the exit code will not tell: another canonical text or other paths in the package on Windows;
- the cost is $0: "Standard GitHub-hosted runners in public repositories" are among what "remains free" (GitHub Docs, "GitHub Actions billing", read 2026-11-05).

— *Rejected:*
- a run by hand at the release — Д6 calls it outright the worse of the two: a binary that does not start would be found by the author, not by CI;
- six required checks by the names of the matrix — the ruleset would have to be edited with each change of a runner's label;
- a path filter on the job — a skipped required check would hang on a pull request without code, and the minutes are free;
- QEMU on one runner — checks neither macOS nor Windows;
- a full checkout and Go on each runner — the binary is checked as the release will build it, not by a build on the spot.

— *What is not closed:* GitHub may reconfigure or remove the labels `macos-15-intel` and `windows-11-arm`. Then the job will fail with the label's name, and a task will change it. "Binaries" is put into the ruleset `main` by the author (rule 4).

— *Consequence:* of the task's edits, only `justfile` (the recipe `binaries-samples`) is in the list `paths` of the release, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.1 with the same binaries. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `.github/binaries.sh` give no release.

— RUN.md, T42; Д6; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `.github/binaries.sh`; justfile; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 3 and 3.1.

— *Amendment 2026-11-06:* `.github/binaries.sh` runs `open` too: as the seventh step — `index --first` over the packages of the vector `indexes/first-release` and the package of the vector `packages/with-solver`, then `open` over them with the seeds 1 and 2^64 − 1. The sums of everything that `open` wrote are checked against the linux/amd64 reference, as the package and the index are (R43). — RUN.md, T40.

— *Second amendment 2026-11-06:* "Binaries" stands in the ruleset `main` (25488826) — as a check of GitHub Actions, `integration_id` 15368, like the others. The executor put it there on the author's instruction on 2026-11-06 through the GitHub API, `PUT repos/altnovatrail/module-kit/rulesets/25488826`. The rest of the ruleset did not change: checked byte for byte before and after. The author's step from "What is not closed" above is done.

## The first release of the line 0.2

**R42. The vector test skips not a single case: the list `internal/vectors/testdata/skipped.txt`, its parsing and the stub `noCode` are removed, and a case that does not pass fails `TestVectors`. `RELEASE_LINE` is `0.2`: the edit of `release.yml` in the list `paths` gives v0.2.0 — the first release of `module-kit` that others take. CI of `modules` and `canvas` take the binary of the CLI that `release-verify` accepted, and `api` and the eval runner of `tutor` — the Go module at a tag.**
- **What was done** (RUN.md, T39):
  - the skip list has been empty since T36, the commit a3635f9 struck out the last 33 lines. On `spec` v0.9.2 the vector test passes all 595 cases of `spec.Format` and `contracts.Vectors`, with 0 skipped (`go test -v -run 'TestVectors$'`);
  - `testdata/skipped.txt`, `parseSkipped`, `unknownSkips`, `outcome`, `errNoCode` and `noCode` left `internal/vectors`, and with them the tests `TestParseSkipped`, `TestUnknownSkips` and `TestOutcome`. What stayed is the sorting into cases, the refusal on a file of a kind that the test does not know, and the refusal on a kind without a single case (R20). Each case is a subtest, and its runner from `runners` must return `nil`;
  - `.github/workflows/release.yml` — `RELEASE_LINE: "0.2"`. The number is computed by the recipe `release-version` of `spec` by the `RELEASE_LINE` of this file at the commit being released. There are no other releases of the line 0.2, so the first is v0.2.0. Of the task's edits, the list `paths` has `release.yml` and the comment of the Go package `internal/vectors/doc.go`, which does not change the binary; the tests and `testdata/` are not in the list (R18);
  - who takes the release and how — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1. CI of `modules` and `canvas` take the binary at an exact tag and check it with `release-verify` before the first run (R77 spec). `api` and the eval runner of `tutor` take the Go module: `go get github.com/altnovatrail/module-kit@v0.2.0` and `go mod tidy`. The Go module is not a release file: its zip at the tag is built by the Go module proxy, and its hash is recorded by the Go checksum database, like the modules of `spec` (R64 spec). A probe on v0.1.28 in an empty module passed: the Go packages `verdict`, `observation`, `openview`, `modulepackage` and `moduleindex` built, `go mod verify` — "all modules verified". The `go.mod` of `module-kit` has no `replace` and `tool` that would hinder the import.

— *Why:* R16 requires that from v0.2.0 the list be empty and the test not let it grow: then each next release passes all the vectors too. While the list and its parsing stood in the code, any pull request could add a line to it, and only the review held this (R20). Without the list a case that does not pass cannot be got around by anything but code, and the test holds the rule. The parsing of a list that does not exist and its three helpers would be code that no run executes.

— *Rejected:*
- to keep the file and fail the test when it is not empty: the outcome is the same, but the parsing, the three helpers and their tests would remain code that is not executed on an empty list, and "the list must be empty" and "there is no list" are one rule;
- to keep the list for a move to a tag: the new vectors of the tag would stand in it until their code comes. Then a release could come out without passing the vectors, against R16 and the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R76 spec. A move to a tag that brings vectors without code stays red until the code comes;
- to raise the line by a separate pull request after the edit of the test: the edit of the test with `doc.go` would give one more release of the line 0.1, v0.1.29, which nobody takes, and there would be two merges.

— *Consequence:*
- a move to a new tag of `spec` that brings new vectors brings their code in the same task. What the "Consequence" of R20 says, "the new vectors of the tag go into the code or into the list", now means only "into the code";
- the merge of T39 gives the release v0.2.0. Its files are checked by `release-verify`, and the Go module by `go get` in an empty module. The check goes after the merge, together with the author (RUN.md, T39, «Открыто»);
- row 3.3 of COORDINATION.md `spec` is done when T39 is marked on `main`, and v0.2.0 has come out and `release-verify` has accepted it. The release is awaited by `modules` (row 3.6), `api` (row 6.5) and the eval runner of `tutor`. This is row В33 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»;
- to R07, R16 and R20 — amendments.

— RUN.md, T39, tables 1 and 3; `internal/vectors/`; `.github/workflows/release.yml`; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 3.1 and 3.6.

The first failed at the signature: cosign did not get an OIDC token from GitHub, "identity token request to provider github-actions failed with status 504 Gateway Timeout", even before publication, and there was no tag. There was nothing to fix in `module-kit`: the author restarted the run. `just -f spec/justfile release-verify module-kit v0.2.0 <file>` accepted all 16 files — "signed by the shared release.yml of .github v0.1.15", — `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` matched, `module-kit_v0.2.0_linux_amd64 --version` — `module-kit v0.2.0` and `Unicode 17.0.0`, and `check` of the example `with-templates` — code 0. `go get github.com/altnovatrail/module-kit@v0.2.0` and `go mod tidy` in an empty module built a program on `verdict`, `observation`, `openview`, `modulepackage` and `moduleindex`, and `go mod verify` — "all modules verified". — RUN.md, T40.

## The command `open`

**R43. The command `open` writes the open views of a release of `modules` for the samples page of `canvas`: `module-kit open --index <release index> --latest <newest release index> --seed N [--seed N]... --out <folder> <package folder>...`. For each module it takes the version of a new attempt by the index of the release, with the marks of withdrawal from the newest index. In `open-views.json` each task has an entry with its open view and digest, and a template an instance for each seed. Beside it are the files of the device part in `modules/<ID>/<version>/`. The marks are combined by `moduleindex.WithLatest`, and the open view as a JSON value is given by `openview.TaskView` and `ManifestView`.**
- **The call** (`cmd/module-kit/open.go`):
  - `--index` is the index of the release from which the page is built; `--latest` is the index of the newest release, the same file when the release is the newest. CI of `canvas` has checked both with `release-verify` (R77 spec);
  - `--seed` is a seed of the format: unsigned 64-bit, in decimal digits, with no leading zero ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 22). It repeats; the same seed twice, another spelling or no seed at all — usage and code 2. The page build chooses the seeds;
  - the package folders are positional, as with `index` and `diff`, each by the digest of the bytes of its `inventory.json`. An extra folder does no harm, a missing needed one is code 2, and a release with nothing to open needs no folders;
  - `--out` — the folder does not exist or is empty, or else code 2. It is written through a temporary folder beside it, as `build` writes a package.
- **The choice of versions:**
  - `moduleindex.WithLatest(release, latest)` gives the index of the release where each entry is withdrawn if the newest index withdrew it, with its reason. An entry of the release that the newest one lacks, holds otherwise or whose withdrawal it does not hold is `ErrLatest`: such an index is not the newest, and the command exits with code 2;
  - for each module of the release's index, in the order of IDs, — `moduleindex.Newest`: the newest by semver unwithdrawn version within the format's window, as for a new attempt in `api` (R35). Each newer version that it passed over is named on stdout — a withdrawn one with its reason, one of another format version with its number —, and a module without a version by a line `<ID>: no version to open`;
  - the package of the chosen version is read by `modulepackage.Read` by the chain of digests, with `device/`. The ID, the version and the format version of the inventory must be those that the entry names, or else code 2.
- **`open-views.json`** is a canonical text (R34):
  - the root is `format: "module-kit-open-views"`, `version: 0`, `module_format_version` and `modules` in the order of IDs;
  - a module has `id`, `version`, `module_format_version`, `release` — the tag of the entry —, `package_digest`, `manifest` — the open view of the manifest — and `tasks` in the order of task IDs;
  - a task has `{"open_view_digest", "task"}`, and an instance of a template also `seed`, as a string of decimal digits. The instances go in the order of the numbers of the seeds. The instance — the number `template.AttemptInstance(seed, ID, count)` among the `instances` of the package, with the numbers substituted, `template.Instance`, — is the one that an attempt with this seed would get ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 23). No solvers run: the package carries the numbers;
  - `task` is the JSON value of the open view before a type took it, `openview.TaskView`, and `open_view_digest` is the digest of exactly its canonical text. So whoever reads the file gets the same digest as the sample has (R93 spec).
- **The files of the device part** — `modules/<ID>/<version>/LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and `media/<path>`, byte for byte those that the chain of digests checked against the listing of the part. The layout is the `device/` of the package: the path of a media file from the open view is found from `media/`, as in the module. The ID is `<publisher>/<name>`, and the pattern of the ID already excludes the names of Windows devices.
- **Codes** — 0 and 2. The command checks no format rules: the packages are read by the chain of digests, so it has no code 1 (R16).
- **Tests:**
  - `open_test.go`: `TestOpenPackages` — all six vectors of `packages/`; `TestOpenInstances` — the instances of `missing-factor` by the three seeds of the vector `templates/missing-factor.yaml`; `TestOpenMedia`, `TestOpenWithdrawn`, `TestOpenFails` — 18 cases of code 2 —, `TestOpenFailsLater`, `TestOpenFormatOfThePackage`, `TestOpenNoModule`, `TestModuleViewsFails`, `TestOpenHelpers`;
  - `moduleindex/latest_test.go`: `TestWithLatest` and `TestWithLatestRefuses` on the vectors of `indexes/`, and `FuzzWithLatest` over a pair of texts through `Read`;
  - `openview`: `TestViews` — the value of `TaskView` and `ManifestView` is equal to the open view of the vectors of `open-view/`, and the digest to the text of that value;
  - `.github/binaries.sh` runs `open` on each OS and checks what it wrote against the linux/amd64 reference (an amendment to R41).

— *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-11-06 in the planning of T40 (RUN.md, T40, «Решено автором»), all by the plan's recommendation:
- `--seed`: a sample names an instance by the task's ID and the seed (`TaskReference` in `sample.proto` of `spec`; R93 spec). Which seeds the page needs, its build knows, and `canvas` has not decided it yet (architecture 3.5 canvas, `main` 602945b, read 2026-11-06). An explicit "at least one" is like the other "none" of the commands (R16);
- a wrapping entry: the element of `canvas` rejects an extra field of the open view (architecture 3.5 canvas), and the seed and the digest are not fields of a task;
- positional folders — as with `index` and `diff`, and CI of `canvas` names the packages by the entries of the index (R45 canvas);
- `modules/<ID>/<version>/` — the same layout as that of `device/`, and the names `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` do not stand beside the media files;
- the value before a type, `TaskView`: the digest is computed from the value before a type (R34), and the text in the file is the same value, so the match is held by code, not by chance. On all seven vectors of `open-view/task/` and on the examples of tasks the value of the type `open.Task` is the same now (a probe of 2026-11-06). But only the agreement of the type generator of `spec` with the marks `x-open` holds this: an empty open object, which the open view keeps ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 6), a type with `omitzero` could lose;
- `WithLatest` is in the library: the rule "the newest index knows of all the withdrawals" is a rule of the format, not of the CLI (R25 spec, R63 spec), and `Newest` stays one rule of the choice of a version (R16).

— *Rejected:*
- `--seeds <file>` with seeds by module and task — the form of the file would be invented before `canvas` asked for it;
- one seed without a flag — the page would not show different instances;
- all the instances of a template — up to 10,000 open views per template (R15);
- `seed` and `open_view_digest` right in the object `open.Task` — `canvas` would cut them out before the element;
- `--packages <folder of folders>`, as the draft of section 3.6 wrote, — one more form of input beside `index` and `diff`;
- `media/<ID>/<version>/<path>` with `LICENSE` beside it — by the draft of section 3.6: a layout that is neither in the package nor in the module;
- code 1 for a module without a version — this is not a refusal of the format, but what the release is: stdout names it, and the page is built from the others.

— *Consequence:*
- the form of the call and of the output for CI of `canvas` — В34 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»;
- the merge will give a release of the line 0.2: the list `paths` has `cmd/module-kit/*.go`, `moduleindex/*.go` and `openview/*.go`;
- amendments to R15 and R41.

— RUN.md, T40, table 3; `cmd/module-kit/open.go`, `main.go`, `published.go`, `build.go`; `moduleindex/latest.go`; `openview/digest.go`, `openview.go`; `.github/binaries.sh`; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 2.5, 3.1 and 3.6.

## The devcontainer image on arm64

**R44. The devcontainer image is built as a whole by a workflow of its own, "Devcontainer", `.github/workflows/devcontainer.yml`: the stage `devcontainer` on `ubuntu-24.04` and `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, as Rebuild Container builds it. Then `.github/devcontainer.sh` checks that the image is of its own architecture and that each tool answers with the version of its `ARG` in the Dockerfile. The workflow runs on pull requests and pushes to `main` that change `.devcontainer/**`, the workflow itself or the script, and by the button. It is not in the ruleset `main`. Д12 and Д14 are closed.**
- **What was done:**
  - the jobs "Devcontainer / amd64" and "Devcontainer / arm64" — `docker build --target devcontainer --tag devcontainer .devcontainer`: the context and the stage are those that `devcontainer.json` names. The build itself checks each archive against its sha256 (R34 spec), and gopls against the lock file `.devcontainer/gopls/` (R19);
  - `.github/devcontainer.sh <image> <Dockerfile> <architecture>` — `dpkg --print-architecture` in the image is the one given. The first line of `node`, `just`, `go`, `buf`, `gh`, `just-lsp`, `claude` and `gopls` in the image names the version of its `ARG`, and not as part of a longer number. `gopls` is in `/usr/local/bin`;
  - the permissions are `contents: read`, `persist-credentials: false`; checkout takes only `.devcontainer` and the script, `sparse-checkout`; the checkout action is the same as in `ci.yml`, v7.0.1 by SHA;
  - on pull request #61 (2026-11-06): "Devcontainer / amd64" — 1 min 18 s, "Devcontainer / arm64" — 1 min 13 s, both green. On arm64 the sums of Node 24.21.0, just 1.58.0, Go 1.27.3, buf 1.75.0, gh 2.101.0 and just-lsp 0.10.0 matched, `go version` is `go1.27.3 linux/arm64`, and gopls was built from the lock file — `golang.org/x/tools/gopls v0.24.0`;
  - the script was checked here before the pull request with a stand-in `docker` that executes the command in this container, built from the same Dockerfile. On amd64 it passed; with the architecture arm64 and with `GO_VERSION=1.27.30` it failed, naming what was wrong. `actionlint` did not run here: running it through `go run` is not allowed in this session. The syntax of the workflow was checked by the run on #61.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-06: a workflow of its own by paths, not one run by the button and not a row «в реестр spec». Д12 waited «если CI начнёт собирать образ на arm64», and Д14 waited for Rebuild Container, which checks the gopls step only on the author's machine. Standard runners are free for a public repository (GitHub Docs, "GitHub Actions billing", read 2026-11-05; R41). By paths — because the image changes only with `.devcontainer/`, and a run on each pull request would spend minutes for nothing. Before this, the CI checks built only the stage `toolchain` on amd64: the shared `go.yml` and "Binaries / build".

— *Rejected:*
- one run by the button: the next raise of Go, buf or gopls in the Dockerfile nobody would check on arm64 again;
- a row «в реестр spec», so that CI of `spec` built the image on arm64: the Dockerfile is from the recipe of `spec`, but the gopls step only `module-kit` and `tutor` have (R19; В19), and `spec` would not build it;
- putting "Devcontainer" in the ruleset `main`: a check that the path filter skipped would not report, and a pull request without an edit of `.devcontainer/` would wait for it forever;
- checking the versions inside the Dockerfile: the stage `toolchain` already prints `go version` and `buf --version`, but only `gopls` compares the version with its `ARG`, and the script holds them all by one rule.

— *Consequence:* Д12 and Д14 are closed. This does not replace a rebuild of the image on a Mac with Apple silicon — Docker Desktop and the Dev Containers extension are their own there —, but a wrong arm64 sum is now seen in CI, not by whoever opened the project. The merge gives no release: the workflow and the script are outside the list `paths`. — `.github/workflows/devcontainer.yml`; `.github/devcontainer.sh`; RUN.md, table 4; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1.

## go-yaml v4.0.0

**R45. `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` is v4.0.0, the first non-RC release, raised by hand on 2026-11-06, before the `cooldown` pause of Dependabot. Д16 is closed, and 2SXE and W5VH, which v4.0.0 still does not read, are a new debt, Д17.**
- **What was done:** `go get go.yaml.in/yaml/v4@v4.0.0` and `go mod tidy` — in `go.mod` and `go.sum` only go-yaml changed. Checked by Д16 (the 2026-11-06 amendment to R21):
  - the test of the format's corpus, the YAML test suite and all the tests of the module pass; the fuzz test of the reader, `FuzzRead` of `internal/yaml`, — 60 s without findings;
  - 2SXE and W5VH do not pass and stay in `suiteUnpassed`: the `yts/known-failing-tests` of v4.0.0 names them itself. From this file v4.0.0 removed 33 lines, but that is a comparison of numbers in its own test of the suite, not the parsing;
  - the API that was removed — the Limit plugin — the code does not take; `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` are the same, and `release-licenses` puts them as before;
  - `ci-lint`, `ci-test`, `ci-vuln` — "No vulnerabilities found" — and `ci-licenses` are green.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-06. The `cooldown` pause of three days would hold v4.0.0 until 2026-11-07 18:36 UTC (`.github/dependabot.yml`), and the API of the RC could change before v4.0.0 — Д16. In the same way, by hand, T18 raised `go.yml` before Dependabot (R18). Rejected: waiting for the pull request of Dependabot — the pause is set against retracted and substituted releases. Here the check above replaces it, and the release came out two days ago, and the Go proxy holds its hash.

— *Consequence:* the merge will give a release of the line 0.2: `go.mod` and `go.sum` are in the list `paths`, and the binary carries the new go-yaml. A pull request of Dependabot for v4.0.0 will not come: the version is already in place. — `go.mod`, `go.sum`; RUN.md, table 4; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.6 and 4.

## An anchor with a colon at the end of its name

**R46. An anchor or an alias whose name by YAML 1.2.2 ends with a colon — `&a:`, `*a:` — the YAML reader names a breach of `yaml.no-anchors` by its own pass `colonAnchor` in `internal/yaml/errors.go`, rather than wait for go-yaml. 2SXE and W5VH of the YAML test suite pass, `suiteUnpassed` is empty, Д17 is closed.**
- **Why go-yaml does not read them.** Its scanner of v4.0.0 takes the ASCII characters ns-anchor-char into the name of an anchor, but cuts the trailing colons off on purpose, "to preserve mapping separators immediately after aliases (issue #109)" (`internal/libyaml/scanner.go`, `isAnchorChar` and `scanAnchor`, v4.0.0). yaml/go-yaml#109, "Anchor map key with no space before colon works in v3; breaks in v4", was closed on 2025-11-15 as not planned (read 2026-11-06). So go-yaml reads `&a: key: &a value` as the anchor `a` with a separator and fails at the second colon: "mapping values are not allowed in this context", 1:8 — a reason with no anchor in it. By YAML 1.2.2 the name of the anchor is `a:` (6.9.2), and the file breaks `yaml.no-anchors`.
- **What the pass does.** When go-yaml has refused with an error that `parseError` did not turn into a rule, `colonAnchor` looks at the lines before the place of the refusal, and on the line of the refusal up to its column. It searches for `&` or `*` at the start of a node: first on the line, but for spaces and tabs, after `[`, `{` or `,`, after `-`, `?` or `:` with a space or a tab, and not in a comment. If the name after it by YAML 1.2.2 ends with a colon, this place gets `yaml.no-anchors`, and the message has the words of go-yaml. Otherwise the refusal is "not YAML", as it was.
- **Tests:** `TestRead` — 2SXE and W5VH, and `&` with a colon inside a plain scalar and in a comment — still "not YAML". `TestYAMLTestSuite` passes both cases; without the pass they fail in both tests (a probe). The new functions are covered at 100%, `FuzzRead` — 120 s without findings.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-06. The condition of Д17 — «когда go-yaml уберёт 2SXE или W5VH из `known-failing-tests`» — will not come: go-yaml decided not to fix this. The sign of the divergence is narrow and exact: the name by YAML 1.2.2 ends with a colon exactly where go-yaml cuts it. The pass runs only when go-yaml has already refused, so it does not change the verdict on the file — it changes only the reason named, and the module author sees "the format has no anchors", not someone else's markup error.

— *Rejected:*
- accepting it as a known limitation and leaving the cases in `suiteUnpassed`: the module author would see a reason that is silent about the anchor;
- a scanner of anchors of its own, before go-yaml, over the whole file: it would have to repeat the parsing of quotes, block scalars and flow collections, and it is needed only where go-yaml has already refused;
- proposing another reading to go-yaml: #109 was resolved as not planned, and v4 holds this on purpose.

— *Consequence:* the merge will give a release of the line 0.2 — `internal/yaml/errors.go` is in the list `paths`. An amendment to R21. — `internal/yaml/errors.go`, `read_test.go`, `suite_test.go`; RUN.md, table 4; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

## The warning about a number in a text of the whole module

**R50. Of a number in a text of the whole module — a rung template `rung_templates`, a trap advice `trap_advice`, the title and the description of a technique — the module check itself warns: `module.Report` has the field `Warnings`, a warning per text in which the leak check reads at least one number, and the numbers of a text are given by a new function of the package `leak`, `Numbers`. `check` prints them as lines `<path> #/pointer: warning: …` after the breaches, before the warnings about pictures, and the exit code does not change from them. So Д19 is closed.**

- **What is decided** (the author's instruction of 2026-11-08 — «делай Д19» —, the executor's decisions by the format text):
  - **what is warned of** — each text that the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20, calls a text of the whole module: the rung templates of the manifest, the advice of each trap of the catalog, the title and the description of each technique. The texts of a task — `givens`, `unknown`, the texts of trap values, `simpler_case` — are not: they are for one task, and `task.no-leaks` holds them by the keys of its task. The texts of a lesson are not either: a lesson names its tasks, and `lesson.no-leaks` holds them;
  - **what a number is** — what the leak check reads ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20, "A number"): `leak.Numbers` gives the numbers of a text by the same steps as the search for a key — `runsIn`, on which `numbersIn` now stands too —, so the warning and `task.no-leaks` never diverge. A number is written as it stands in the normal form of the text: `½` as `1/2`, full-width digits as ASCII, `07:30` as two numbers, `07` and `30`, and "seven", `7th` and `x7` are not numbers. The message names each number once, in the order of the text;
  - **where** — in the package `module`, not as a package of its own, as `picture` is: the texts are parts that the check has already read into the types of `formatv0`, and the warning carries no new third-party module, so `api` takes nothing extra. The warning reads each part whose file passed the schema — in a module that the rules rejected too: the author needs to know of a number in a text in any case. A part that the schema rejected gives no warning, as it gives no breaches of rules either (R23);
  - **order and form** — by the path of the file, then by the place in it, as with breaches; the place is a JSON pointer, without a line and a column, as with the breaches of `task.no-leaks`. The line is `<path> #/pointer: warning: <message>`, and the message repeats why the format wants a text without numbers;
  - **who gets them** — `Check` and `CheckModule`. `CheckFile` does not: it checks the examples and the vectors of parts, and the warning has no vectors. `build` and `diff` print the report of a refusal without the warnings, as they do of pictures (R39);
  - **tests** — `TestNumbers` and a check in `FuzzNumbers`: `Numbers` gives the same numbers that the search reads — each stands in the normal form of the text and has the same value (30 s, about 1.3 million runs, without findings); `TestWarnings` — each kind of text, the order, a rejected module, a part that the schema rejected, the texts of tasks and `CheckFile`; `TestNumbersSaid`; `TestExamplesWarnOfNoNumber` — no example of a manifest, of traps or of techniques of `spec` v0.9.9 holds a number; in the CLI — `TestCheckWarnsOfNumbers`. `TestCheckRefuses` now reads a line of a warning apart: the vectors of `task.no-leaks` `key-in-a-rung-template`, `key-in-a-technique` and `key-in-a-trap-advice` hold a key in a text of the whole module, and that is also a number in it.

— *Why:* the format requires a warning, not a refusal (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R57 spec): `task.no-leaks` finds a number in a text of the whole module only when it is already the key of a task that shows this text — so the epic E3 of `modules` found the numbers in the advice of `wrong-parity` and `time-unit-mixup` only three tasks later. Reading a number by the same code as the search means warning of exactly what may one day become a leak.

— *Rejected:*
- a package of its own, as `picture`: that one stands apart for the sake of the picture decoder, which `api` must not carry (R39), and here there is no new code of dependencies, and the parts have already been read by the check;
- a separate function over `fs.FS`, as `picture.Warnings`: it would read and parse the YAML of all the parts a second time;
- warning of a number in words — "seven", «семь»: the format does not read it as a number ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20, "What is not read as a number"), and a task whose answer is written so holds it in `leak_phrases`;
- a warning of each number by a separate line: an advice with the multiplication table has eight of them, and the place is one.

— *Consequence:* `leak/*.go` and `module/*.go` are in the list `paths` of the release, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.2. Other repositories need nothing: the field `Warnings` of `Report` is new, and the earlier fields are the same; CI of `modules` will see the warnings in the output of `check`, but the exit code is as before. — `leak/leak.go`, `leak/doc.go`, their tests; `module/warnings.go`, `module/check.go`, `module/doc.go`, their tests; `cmd/module-kit/main.go` and tests; README.md; RUN.md, the start of table 4; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 1, 3 and 3.1.
